He has done us a thousand favours, and if he ever succeeds in overthrowing this infamous government he will restore to us all the property we have lost. But *ai de mi*, I cannot see deliverance yet.”
“*Mamita*, do not say such a thing!” exclaimed her daughter. “Do you begin to despair now when there is most reason to hope?”
“Child, what can he do with this handful of ill-armed men?” returned the mother sadly. “He has bravely raised the standard, but the people do not flock to it. Ah, when this revolt is crushed, like so many others, we poor women will only have to lament for more friends slain and fresh persecutions.” And here she covered her eyes with her handkerchief.
Dolores tossed her head back and made a sudden gesture of impatience.
“Do you, then, expect to see a great army formed before the ink is dry on the General’s proclamation?

===Chinese===

“您岂非曾为我们敬爱的将军——为我们深爱的祖国立下了泼天的大功！哪怕我们将您心中所渴盼的一切尽数奉上，也远远抵偿不了这份大恩大德。我们阖家上下生生世世都注定是您的欠债之人。”
听着她这般夸张激越的言辞，我忍不住微微一笑，然而这些话落在耳中却着实甜人心脾。
“您对故土的热忱挚爱确实是一种令人动容的高尚情操，”我有些冒失地随口评论道，“然而圣科洛马将军对于这片土地的福祉而言，难道当真就如此不可或缺么？”
她神色一变，显然受到了冒犯，抿紧双唇闭口不答。“先生，您初来乍到，是个异乡外邦人，对我们这里的情势并不深知，”母亲在一旁温和地打圆场道，“多洛蕾丝切莫计较这个。先生未曾亲历过我们所饱经的惨烈战乱，更不知晓我们的死敌从来都是靠着勾结外邦异族才得以窃取胜利的。啊，先生，他们给这片河山带来了多少腥风血雨、流放通缉与奇耻大辱啊！然而唯独有一个硬汉，他们千方百计也从未能将其击垮征服：自打少年时代起，他就始终冲锋在沙场最前沿，迎着敌人的枪林弹雨毫不退缩，更绝非巴西人的黄金所能收买腐化。对于我们这些丧尽亲朋、历尽迫害、家产被剥夺殆尽以肥私敌与走狗之囊的苦难孤寡而言，他宛如再造青天，这又有何好奇怪的呢？对这所宅院里的孤儿寡母来说，他更胜过至亲骨肉。他曾是我亡夫生前的挚友同袍。他曾施予我们千百次恩惠，设若有朝一日他当真能掀翻那座无耻的伪政权，他便会将我们失去的家业如数奉还。然而，哀哉我身（ai de mi），眼下我却仍看不到破晓的曙光。”
“母亲（Mamita），休要长他人志气！”女儿急迫地反驳道，“如今正是最该满怀希望的关头，您怎能反倒自暴自弃、悲观绝望起来了呢？”
“痴儿，单凭他手头这区区百十号连像样兵刃都凑不齐的人马，他又能成得了什么气候？”母亲满面戚容地叹息道，“他固然英勇地扯起了义旗，可四方的黎民百姓却并未揭竿响应。唉，待到这场起义如往昔无数次那般惨遭剿灭血洗之际，我们这些苦命的妇道人家便唯有在残垣断壁中，为添新坟的至亲与接踵而至的残酷迫害洒泪啼哭罢了。”说到此处，她禁不住用帕子掩面拭泪。
多洛蕾丝猛地一扬下颚，神态间流露出掩饰不住的急躁与不甘。
“难道在您看来，将军讨逆檄文上的墨迹尚且未干，天底下就该凭空变出一支所向披靡的百万雄师么？

===Original===

When Santa Coloma was a fugitive without a follower you hoped; now when he is with us, and actually preparing for a march on the capital, you begin to lose heart﻿—I cannot understand it!”
Doña Mercedes rose without replying, and left the room. The lovely enthusiast dropped her head on her hand, and remained silent, taking no notice of me, a cloud of sorrow on her countenance.
“Señorita,” I said, “it is not necessary for you to remain longer here. Only tell me before going that you forgive me, for it makes me very unhappy to think that I have offended you.”
She turned to me with a very bright smile and gave me her hand.
“Ah, it is for you to forgive me for hastily taking offence at a light word,” she said. “I must not allow anything you say in future to spoil my gratitude. Do you know I think you are one of those who like to laugh at most things, señor﻿—no, let me call you Richard, and you shall call me Dolores, for we must remain friends always. Let us make a compact, then it will be impossible for us to quarrel. You shall be free to doubt, question, laugh at everything, except one thing only﻿—my faith in Santa Coloma.”
“Yes, I will gladly make that agreement,” I replied. “It will be a new kind of paradise, and of the fruit of every tree I may eat except of this tree only.”
She laughed gaily.
“I will now leave you,” she said. “You are suffering pain, and are very tired. Perhaps you will be able to sleep.” While speaking she brought a second cushion for my head, then left me, and before long I fell into a refreshing doze.
I spent three days of enforced idleness at the Casa Blanca, as the house was called, before Santa Coloma returned, and after the rough experience I had undergone, during which I had subsisted on a flesh diet untempered by bread or vegetables, they were indeed like days spent in paradise to me. Then the General came back. I was sitting alone in the garden when he arrived, and, coming out to me, he greeted me warmly.
“I greatly feared from my previous experience of your impatience under restraint that you might have left us,” he said kindly.
“I could not do that very well yet, without a horse to ride on,” I returned.
“Well, I came here just now to say I wish to present you with a horse and saddle.

===Chinese===

“当初圣科洛马身陷绝境、孤家寡人四处亡命之际，您尚且心存希望；如今他已然亲临我们身边，厉兵秣马筹备进军直取京城，您反倒先自乱阵脚丧失了斗志——这等道理我实在想不通！”
唐娜·梅赛德斯没有作答，默默起身退出了房间。那位绝色的热血少女将臻首支在纤手上，凝神陷入了沉思，浑然不再搭理我，一张俏脸上笼罩着一层郁郁的愁云。
“小姐，”我轻声开口，“您其实不必勉强留在这儿陪我。临走前只需告诉我您已原谅了我便好，因为一想到自己无意间冒犯了您，我便只觉寝食难安。”
她霍然回眸，向我展颜绽放出一抹灿若朝阳的笑靥，坦然递过了柔荑。
“哎呀，该是我向您赔罪才是，怪我为了一句无心玩笑便小题大做，”她说，“往后我可决不能由着您的话头，坏了我心底沉甸甸的感激之情。您可知晓，先生，依我看您就是那种无论遇上何等大事都爱一笑置之的游戏人间之徒——不，往后容我直呼您理查德吧，您也管我叫多洛蕾丝便是，因为我们必须永世结为知己。不如咱们立下一个君子协定，如此一来日后便再也不会拌嘴翻脸。您尽可随心所欲去怀疑、质问、嘲笑天下万物，但唯独除了一样不可亵渎——那便是我对圣科洛马的坚定信念。”
“一言为定，我欣然立誓遵从，”我微笑着答道，“这俨然将是一座全新模样的伊甸乐园，园中各样树上的果子我皆可任意采食，唯独这棵树上的智慧之果万不可触碰。”
她放声欢畅地笑了起来。
“我眼下得暂且告退了，”她说，“您有伤在身，又这般劳顿。或许眼下能安稳睡上一觉。”说话间，她又细心地替我的脑后垫上了一只软枕，随后轻步退出了房门；不多时，我便沉沉坠入了舒爽香甜的梦乡。
在圣科洛马折返集镇之前，我在被当地人唤作“白宫”（Casa Blanca）的这座深宅大院里度过了整整 3 天（3 天）无所事事的闲散时光。在经历了此前一段仅靠毫无果蔬搭配的粗粝烤肉果腹的艰苦落魄生涯之后，这几天的幽居岁月于我而言无异于置身天堂乐土。随后，将军凯旋还朝了。他赶来之际我正独自漫步在花园之中，他径直穿过庭院迎向我，亲切热忱地互致问候。
“根据此前见识过你那受不得半分拘束的急躁脾气，我还当真唯恐你早已不辞而别了呢，”他爽朗温和地打趣道。
“在手头连一匹代步骏马都没有的境况下，我便是想溜也插翅难飞呀，”我半开玩笑地回敬道。
“哈哈，我此刻登门，正是特意为了赠予你一匹良驹配一具马鞍的。

===Original===

The horse is standing at the gate now, I believe; but, if you are only waiting for a horse to leave us I shall have to regret making you this present. Do not be in a hurry; you have yet many years to live in which to accomplish all you wish to do, and let us have the pleasure of your company a few days longer. Doña Mercedes and her daughter desire nothing better than to keep you with them.”
I promised him not to run away immediately, a promise which was not hard to make; then we went to inspect my horse, which proved to be a very fine bay, saddled with a dashing native *recado*.
“Come with me and try him,” he said. “I am going to ride out to the Cerro Solo.”
The ride proved an extremely pleasant one, as I had not mounted a horse for some days, and had been longing to spice my idle hours with a little exhilarating motion. We went at a swinging gallop over the grassy plain, the General all the time discoursing freely of his plans and of the brilliant prospects awaiting all those timely-wise individuals who should elect to link their fortunes with his at this early stage of the campaign.
The Cerro, three leagues distant from the village of El Molino, was a high, conical hill standing quite alone and overlooking the country for a vast distance around. A few well-mounted men were stationed on the summit, keeping watch; and, after talking with them for a while, the General led me to a spot a hundred yards away, where there was a large mound of sand and stone, up which we made our horses climb with some difficulty. While we stood here he pointed out the conspicuous objects on the surface of the surrounding country, telling me the names of the estancias, rivers, distant hills, and other things. The whole country about us seemed very familiar to him. He ceased speaking at length, but continued gazing over the wide, sunlit prospect with a strange, far-off look on his face. Suddenly dropping the reins on the neck of his horse, he stretched out his arms towards the south and began to murmur words which I could not catch, while an expression of mingled fury and exultation transformed his face. It passed away as suddenly as it came. Then he dismounted, and, stooping till his knee touched the ground, he kissed the rock before him, after which he sat down and quietly invited me to do the same.

===Chinese===

“坐骑眼下就拴在栅门外呢，我想；不过，设若你真个一得了马便要弃我们而去，那我倒真要后悔不该送你这份厚礼了。切莫急于动身嘛；你来日方长，漫长的人生岁月足够你去大展宏图，且好生在我们这儿多盘桓几日，也好让我们多领略几日良友相伴的雅兴。唐娜·梅赛德斯母女俩眼下可是一心一意盼着能长久挽留你在府上做客呢。”
我当场向他许诺绝不立时开溜，这番诺言于我而言算不上什么难事；随后我们步出门外检视起赠我的坐骑，那是一匹神骏非凡的枣红骏马，配有一副英气逼人的本土高乔鞍鞯（recado）。
“跟我一同上马试骑一番吧，”他说，“我正打算策马出巡一趟独峰丘（Cerro Solo）。”
这一趟策马驰骋畅快至极，鉴于我已多日未曾登鞍，心头早就渴望着用一场淋漓尽致的颠簸运动来为闲散的时光添几分精神。我们在碧草如茵的平原上放缰疾驰，一路上将军知无不言、言无不尽地畅谈着他的宏图伟略，以及那些懂得把握先机、在举事之初便英明地将自身命运与他的帅旗捆绑在一处的有识之士所将迎来的锦绣前程。
那座独峰丘距埃尔莫利诺镇有 3 里格（3 里格）之遥，是一座傲然兀立在平原之上、拔地而起的高耸圆锥形山丘，登顶可极目俯瞰方圆无垠的壮阔平原。几名骑术精湛的游骑哨兵正驻扎在山巅极目警戒；与他们简短交谈了几句后，将军引着我来到百码（100 码）开外的一处高地，那里矗立着一座由沙石堆叠而成的大石丘，我们费了好大一番气力才策马攀上顶峰。伫立在石丘之上，他举手指向四周旷野中清晰可见的一处处地标景致，如数家珍地向我点明那些大牧场（estancia）、河流、远山以及其余地理名胜的方位与名称。周围绵延无际的疆土在他胸中仿佛了如指掌。他终于收住了话头，然而目光却依然凝视着那片沐浴在烈日下的辽阔天地，面容上浮现出一抹奇异深邃、神游天外的茫然神色。突然之间，他松开了搭在马颈上的缰绳，双臂向着南方广袤的天际猛然展开，嘴唇翕动着吐露出一串我无法听清的低语，而一抹交织着狂暴狂怒与得意癫狂的复杂神情瞬间扭曲了他的脸庞。这抹神情来得快，去得也同样猝不及防。紧接着，他翻身下马，单膝触地长跪于顽石之上，俯身亲吻了面前的岩石，随后盘膝坐下，神色平静地挥手示意我也如法坐下。

===Original===

Returning to the subject he had talked about during our ride, he began openly pressing me to join him in his march to Montevideo, which, he said, would begin almost immediately, and would infallibly result in a victory, after which he would reward me for the incalculable service I had rendered him in assisting him to escape from the Juez of Las Cuevas. These tempting offers, which would have fired my brain in other circumstances﻿—the single state, I mean﻿—I felt compelled to decline, though I did not state my real reasons for doing so. He shrugged his shoulders in the eloquent Oriental fashion, remarking that it would not surprise him if I altered my resolution in a few days.
“Never!” I mentally ejaculated.
Then he recalled our first meeting again, spoke of Margarita, that marvellously beautiful child, asking if I had not thought it strange so fair a flower as that should have sprung from the homely stalk of a sweet potato? I answered that I had been surprised at first, but had ceased to believe that she was a child of Batata’s, or of any of his kin. He then offered to tell me Margarita’s history; and I was not surprised to hear that he knew it.
“I owe you this,” he said, “in expiation of the somewhat offensive remarks I addressed to you that day in reference to the girl. But you must remember that I was then only Marcos Marcó, a peasant, and, having some slight knowledge of acting, it was only natural that my speech should be, as you find it in our common people, somewhat dry and ironical.
“Many years ago there lived in this country one Basilio de la Barca, a person of so noble a figure and countenance that to all those who beheld him he became the type of perfect beauty, so that a ‘Basilio de la Barca’ came to be a proverbial expression in Montevidean society when anyone surpassingly handsome was spoken of. Though he had a gay, lighthearted disposition and loved social pleasures, he was not spoilt by the admiration his beauty excited. Simple-minded and modest he remained always; though perhaps not capable of any very strong passion, for though he won, without seeking it, the hearts of many fair women, he did not marry. He might have married some rich woman to improve his position had he been so minded, but in this, as in everything else in his life, Basilio appeared to be incapable of doing anything to advance his own fortunes.

===Chinese===

言归正传，他重新拾起了在路上谈及的旧话，开始开门见山地极力敦促我投身于他麾下的军伍，跟随他的铁蹄直捣蒙得维的亚。据他说，进军大典不日便将鸣锣开道，且势必将以辉煌的胜利而告终，届时他定将厚赏我在拉斯奎瓦斯治安官（Juez of Las Cuevas）魔爪下助他金蝉脱壳的再造之恩。换在寻常光景——我指的是单身未娶之际——这等诱人的泼天富贵足以及令任何热血男儿热血沸腾；然而此刻我却深知自己不得不婉言谢绝，尽管我并未吐露心底真正的隐衷。他以东岸人特有的生动神态耸了耸肩膀，意味深长地打趣说，若是过上几天我回心转意改变了主意，他老人家也丝毫不会觉得意外。
“绝无可能！”我在心底暗暗斩钉截铁地发誓。
随后，他又回忆起了我们的初次相遇，提及了玛加丽塔（Margarita）——那个美若天仙的绝色女童，含笑询问我当初是否也曾觉得匪夷所思，这样一朵娇艳脱俗的名花何以竟会从一株土头土脑的红薯（Batata，巴塔塔）根茎上破土而生？我坦言自己起初确实惊为天人，但随即早已坚信她绝非巴塔塔的骨肉，更与那一门草民毫无血缘瓜葛。他随后主动提议将玛加丽塔的身世源流原原本本地讲给我听；而得知他对一切内幕尽皆了然于胸，我倒也丝毫不觉惊异。
“这是我欠你的情分，”他说，“全当作补偿我那天对你妄议那女孩时口出轻狂的无礼冒犯。然而你须懂得，那时我不过是化名马科斯·马科（Marcos Marcó）的一个穷酸草民，凭着我粗通的那几分戏台勾当，我的言谈举止自然难免像你平日所见到的粗野下层百姓那样，带着几分冷嘲热讽与尖酸刻薄。
“许多年以前，本国曾出过一位名叫巴西里奥·德·拉·巴尔卡（Basilio de la Barca）的人物，此人生得一表人才、器宇轩昂，凡是有幸瞻仰过其风采之人，无不将其奉为至臻完美的绝世美男典范，以至于在蒙得维的亚的上流交际界中，‘巴西里奥·德·拉·巴尔卡’这几个字甚至化作了一句众口相传的口头禅，专用来形容那些容貌倾国倾城的无双男子。尽管他性情活泼开朗、纵情于社交欢宴，却从未因其容貌博得的如潮倾慕而滋生骄矜浮躁之心。他自始至终秉持着淳朴与谦逊；然而或许是他天生欠缺炽烈狂乱的激情，因而尽管他并未刻意逢迎索取，却俘获了无数闺阁名媛的芳心，可他却终生未娶。倘若他存心攀附权贵以改善自身的落魄家境，他本可轻而易举迎娶一位家财万贯的豪门贵妇，然而正如他在一生中所涉足的其余一切名利纠葛一样，巴西里奥似乎骨子里便彻底缺乏任何投机钻营以谋求飞黄腾达的手腕与欲望。

===Original===

The de la Barcas had once possessed great wealth in land in the country, and, I have heard, descended from an ancient noble family of Spain. During the long, disastrous wars this country has suffered, when it was conquered in turn by England, Portugal, Spain, Brazil, and the Argentines, the family became impoverished, and at last appeared to be dying out. The last of the de la Barcas was Basilio, and the evil destiny which had pursued all of that name for so many generations did not spare him. His whole life was a series of calamities. When young he entered the army, but in his first engagement he received a terrible wound which disabled him for life and compelled him to abandon the military career. After that he embarked all his little fortune in commerce, and was ruined by a dishonest partner. At length when he had been reduced to great poverty, being then about forty years old, he married an old woman out of gratitude for the kindness she had shown to him; and with her he went to live on the seacoast, several leagues east of Cabo Santa Maria. Here in a small rancho in a lonely spot called Barranca del Peregrine, and with only a few sheep and cows to subsist on, he spent the remainder of his life. His wife, though old, bore him one child, a daughter, named Transita. They taught her nothing; for in all respects they lived like peasants and had forgotten the use of books. The situation was also wild and solitary, and they very seldom saw a strange face. Transita spent her childhood in rambling over the dunes on that lonely coast, with only wild flowers, birds, and the ocean waves for playmates. One day, her age being then about eleven, she was at her usual pastimes, her golden hair blowing in the wind, her short dress and bare legs wet with the spray, chasing the waves as they retired, or flying with merry shouts from them as they hurried back towards the shore, flinging a cloud of foam over her retreating form, when a youth, a boy of fifteen, rode up and saw her there. He was hunting ostriches, when, losing sight of his companions, and finding himself near the ocean, he rode down to the shore to watch the tide coming in.
“Yes, I was that boy, Richard﻿—you are quick in making conclusions.” This he said not in reply to any remark I had made, but to my thoughts, which he frequently guessed very aptly.

===Chinese===

“德·拉·巴尔卡家族曾几何时在这片疆土上坐拥浩瀚无垠的广袤庄园，且据我所知，乃是源自西班牙本土一支渊源深厚的显赫名门古族。然而在这片多灾多难的土地所遭受的漫长而毁灭性的连年征战岁月中，随着这片国土先后沦入英格兰、葡萄牙、西班牙、巴西以及阿根廷诸邦之手被轮番践踏征服，这个庞大家族日渐式微破落，最终眼看就要走向灯枯油尽的绝境。巴西里奥便是这德·拉·巴尔卡家族最后的孤种孑遗，然而纠缠了这一姓氏整整几代人的厄运之神，却并未对他手下留情。他的一生简直是一连串无穷无尽的惨祸交织而成的苦难史。年轻时他投笔从戎加入了军伍，然而在生平第一场遭遇战中，他便身负奇重之伤，落下了终身残疾，被迫断送了戎马生涯。此后他将仅存的一点微薄私产全数投入经商，结果被一个背信弃义的合伙人卷款潜逃坑得倾家荡产。直到最后，当他已沦落到家徒四壁、食不果腹的凄惨境地时，时年年届四十（40 岁）的他，出于对一位老妇人曾给予他的体贴照拂的无尽感激，竟娶了这位老妪为妻；随后便携妻子一同前往圣玛利亚角（Cabo Santa Maria）以东数里格的荒僻海岸定居。在那个被称作佩雷格里内断崖（Barranca del Peregrine）的荒蛮孤绝之所，在一间低矮狭小的农舍（rancho）里，仅仅依靠着几头牛羊维系生计，他苟延残喘度过了余生。他的妻子虽已上了年纪，却奇迹般为他诞下了一点骨血，是个女儿，取名特兰西塔（Transita）。他们夫妇二人对这孩子毫无教养训导；皆因他们在各方面早已过得与草莽愚民毫无二致，甚至早已忘却了书本字句究竟为何物。加之所居之处地处绝壁荒野，他们终年难得一见一张生人的面孔。特兰西塔的童年时光便是在那片孤寂荒凉的海岸沙丘间漫游度过的，除却野花、海鸟与滔天巨浪之外，她再无半个玩伴。直到有一天，时年她年仅十一岁（11 岁）上下，正像往常那样在浪花间追逐嬉戏，金色的秀发在狂风中肆意飞舞，短小的衣裙与赤裸的双腿被飞溅的海浪浸得透湿，她追赶着退却的潮水，或是伴随着银铃般的欢呼尖叫，躲避着汹涌拍向岸滩、将漫天白沫倾泻在她娇小逃遁身躯上的滚滚狂涛；就在这一刻，一个年方十五岁（15 岁）的少年骑马信步而至，撞见了眼前的这一幕。他原本是在荒原上追猎鸵鸟，在与同伴失散之后，猛然发觉自己来到了大洋水畔，便索性纵马踱向岸边，出神地观赏起汹涌上涨的漫漫潮水来。
“不错，那个少年正是我自己，理查德——你脑子转得向来极快。”这番话并非是在回应我吐露的言辞，而是在解答我心头瞬间泛起的揣测，皆因他向来总能极其准确地洞察旁人的心思。

===Original===

“The impression this exquisite child made on me it would be impossible to convey in words. I had lived much in the capital, had been educated in our best college, and was accustomed to associate with pretty women. I had also crossed the water and had seen all that was most worthy of admiration in the Argentine cities. And remember that with us a youth of fifteen already knows something of life. This child, playing with the waves, was like nothing I had seen before. I regarded her not as a mere human creature; she seemed more like some being from I know not what far-off celestial region who had strayed to earth, just as a bird of white and azure plumage, and unknown to our woods, sometimes appears, blown hither from a distant tropical country or island, filling those who see it with wonder and delight. Imagine, if you can, Margarita with her shining hair loose to the winds, swift and graceful in her motions as the waves she plays with, her sapphire eyes sparkling like sunlight on the waters, the tender tints of the seashell in her ever-changing countenance, with a laughter that seems to echo the wild melody of the sandpiper’s note. Margarita has inherited the form, not the spirit, of the child Transita. She is an exquisite statue endowed with life. Transita, with lines equally graceful and colours just as perfect, had caught the spirit of the wind and sunshine and was all freedom, motion, fire﻿—a being half human, half angelic. I saw her only to love her; nor was it a common passion she inspired in me. I worshipped her, and longed to wear her on my bosom; but I shrank then and for a long time after from breathing the hot breath of love on so tender and heavenly a blossom. I went to her parents and opened my heart to them. My family being well known to Basilio, I obtained his consent to visit their lonely rancho whenever I could; and I, on my part, promised not to speak of love to Transita till her sixteenth year. Three years after I had found Transita, I was ordered to a distant part of the country, for I was already in the army then, and, fearing that it would not be possible for me to visit them for a long time, I persuaded Basilio to let me speak to his daughter, who was now fourteen.

===Chinese===

“那宛如精灵般降世的绝美孩童给我留下的震撼印记，绝非凡尘俗世的文字所能摹画万一。我自幼生长在首都繁华之所，在本国首屈一指的学府中饱读诗书，平日里早已司空见惯了形形色色的绝色佳人。我亦曾渡海远游，亲眼领略过阿根廷各大通都大邑中最令人叹为观止的繁华盛景。须知在我们这里，一个年满 15 岁（15 岁）的少年郎早已谙熟人情世故。然而眼前这个在惊涛骇浪间欢畅嬉戏的孩子，却全然不同于我生平所见过的任何凡胎肉身。我从未将她视作一个纯粹的凡间生灵；她更像是一个从我所未知的飘渺天界误坠红尘的仙子，宛如一只羽翼泛着雪白与湛蓝神采、在我们本土丛林中从未得见的珍禽异鸟，偶然被狂风从遥远的热带异国或海外仙岛吹拂至此，令每一个有幸得见者心驰神往、叹为观止。设若你能凭空遐想，且想象一下玛加丽塔那一头在狂风中肆意飞扬的璀璨金发吧，她在波涛间的顾盼腾挪宛如她所追逐的浪花般轻盈迅捷，那一双蓝宝石般的明眸在日光下的波光潋滟中熠熠生辉，那一幅时刻变幻莫测的娇容上泛着海贝般的柔嫩光泽，还有那一串仿佛与沙鸻鸟野性婉转的啼鸣交相辉映的悦耳笑声。玛加丽塔继承的，仅仅是当年那个名叫特兰西塔的孩童的肉身形骸，却并未继承她那独一无二的灵性精魂。眼前的玛加丽塔不过是一座被赋予了呼吸的绝美大理石雕像；而当年的特兰西塔，在拥有同样曼妙婀娜的曲线与同样无懈可击的容色的同时，却将狂风与烈日的磅礴精气尽数吸纳于一身，通体洋溢着野性、飞扬的生机与烈火——那是一个半是凡人、半是天使的神奇造化。我第一眼望见她，便不可自拔地坠入了情网；然而她在我灵魂深处点燃的，却绝非凡夫俗子的鄙俗情欲。我顶礼膜拜她，梦寐以求将她拥入怀中悉心珍藏；然而在当时以及此后极其漫长的一段岁月里，我却甚至不敢将凡人粗鄙炽热的爱欲呼吸，吹拂向这般娇嫩神圣的无瑕天葩。我登门拜访了她的双亲，向他们彻底敞开了心扉。鉴于我的家族与巴西里奥乃是世交旧识，我顺利求得了他的恩准，允诺我日后只要一得空暇便可前往那处偏僻的农舍（rancho）探视；而我亦庄严立誓，在特兰西塔年满 16 岁（16 岁）之前，绝不向她吐露半字儿女私情。在邂逅特兰西塔整整 3 年（3 年）之后，我奉命调往边陲异地驻防，皆因那时我已然身入军伍；因唯恐自己此番远征将有极长岁月无法登门相见，我极力劝服了巴西里奥，恳请他破例容许我向他当时年方 14 岁（14 岁）的爱女倾吐心声。

===Original===

She had by this time grown extremely fond of me, and she always looked forward with delight to my visits, when we would spend days together rambling along the shore, or seated on some cliff overlooking the sea, talking of the simple things she knew, and of that wonderful, faraway city life of which she was never tired of hearing. When I opened my heart to her she was at first frightened at these new strange emotions I spoke of. Soon, however, I was made happy by seeing her fear grow less. In one day she ceased to be a child; the rich blood mantled her cheeks, to leave her the next moment pale and tremulous; her tender lips were toying with the rim of the honeyed cup. Before I left her she had promised me her hand, and at parting even clung to me, with her beautiful eyes wet with tears.
“Three years passed before I returned to seek her. During that time I sent scores of letters to Basilio, but received no reply. Twice I was wounded in fight, once very seriously. I was also a prisoner for several months. I made my escape at last, and, returning to Montevideo, obtained leave of absence. Then, with heart afire with sweet anticipations, I sought that lonely seacoast once more, only to find the weeds growing on the spot where Basilio’s rancho had stood. In the neighbourhood I learnt that he had died two years before, and that after his death the widow had returned to Montevideo with Transita. After long inquiry in that city I discovered that she had not long survived her husband, and that a foreign señora, had taken Transita away, no one knew whither. Her loss cast a great shadow on my life. Poignant grief cannot endure forever, nor for very long; only the memory of grief endures. To this memory, which cannot fade, it is perhaps due that in one respect at least I am not like other men. I feel that I am incapable of passion for any woman. No, not if a new Lucrezia Borgia were to come my way, scattering the fiery seeds of adoration upon all men, could they blossom to love in this arid heart. Since I lost Transita I have had one thought, one love, one religion, and it is all told in one word﻿—*Patria*.
“Years passed. I was captain in General Oribe’s army at the siege of my own city. One day a lad was captured in our lines, and came very near being put to death as a spy. He had come out from Montevideo, and was looking for me.

===Chinese===

“到了那时，这姑娘对我早已萌生出了无与伦比的深情依恋，每当我策马登门，她总是翘首以盼、欣喜若狂；我们会整天整天相伴在海岸边漫步盘桓，或是并肩坐在俯瞰沧海的悬崖断壁之上，闲话她所熟识的乡野草木，以及那座她百听不厌、心驰神往的繁华遥远的都会景象。当我终于向她敞开心扉之际，起初听到我吐露的那些崭新而奇异的情愫，她惊得不知所措。然而未过多久，眼见她眸中的惶恐渐渐化作了娇羞与依恋，我心头顿生无尽的欢愉。在短短一日之间，她褪尽了孩童的青涩；绯红的血色如朝霞般染透了她的粉颊，旋即又在下一刻化作了战栗的苍白；她娇嫩的双唇终于战战兢兢地触碰到了那只盛满蜜糖的甘醇酒樽边缘。在我依依惜别之前，她郑重许诺将终身托付于我，分别之际更是死死挽住我的衣襟，那双秋水明眸中溢满了晶莹的泪花。
“整整 3 年（3 年）光阴流转，我才得以重返故地寻索佳人。在此期间，我曾向巴西里奥投递了数十封家书，却从未收到过半字回音。在激战厮杀中我两度负伤，其中一次伤势险些要了我的性命。我还曾沦为敌军的阶下囚，在暗无天日的牢狱中苦熬了数月之久。最终我寻得良机越狱逃脱，折返蒙得维的亚求得了一纸休假公文。随即，怀揣着如烈火般炽烈的甜蜜憧憬，我风尘仆仆再度奔赴那片孤绝荒凉的海岸，然而映入眼帘的，却唯有丛生的荒草凄凉覆盖在巴西里奥昔日农舍的废墟之上。在四邻乡野打探之下，我才得知他早在 2 年前（2 年前）便已撒手人寰，在他离世之后，遗孀便携着特兰西塔重返了蒙得维的亚。在城中历经了旷日持久的明察暗访，我最终查实那位老妇人在夫君亡故后未过多久亦郁郁而终，而一位外邦贵妇人则将无依无靠的特兰西塔带离了该地，去向成谜，无人知晓其究竟身在何方。她的杳无音信如同一道巨大的浓黑阴霾，彻底笼罩了我后半生的岁月。撕心裂肺的剧痛固然无法永无休止地蔓延，亦无法长久煎熬肉身；唯独那份关于剧痛的记忆，却将与骨血永生相随。或许正是由于这份永不褪色的凄楚记忆，才注定了我至少在某一方面全然异于常人。我深知自己此生再也无法对任何凡间女子燃起半点男女私情了。不，哪怕再世降临一位颠倒众生的卢克雷齐娅·博尔吉亚（Lucrezia Borgia），将狂热崇拜的烈火火种播撒在普天下每一个男儿心头，那些种子也休想在这颗早已荒芜如大漠的心田里绽放出半朵爱情的花蕾。自从痛失特兰西塔的那一刻起，我胸中便唯独剩下一个念头、一份挚爱、一种宗教，而这一切千言万语，尽数凝聚在简简单单的一个词汇之中——那便是祖国（Patria）。
“悠悠岁月流逝。在围攻我自己的故都蒙得维的亚的那场旷日持久的战役中，我在奥里韦将军（General Oribe）麾下官拜骑兵上尉。某一天，一个少年在我军防线巡逻哨卡前被当场缉拿，险些被作为奸细间谍当场处决。这孩子是从蒙得维的亚城中偷跑出来的，且指名道姓口口声声要寻找我的下落。

===Original===

He had been sent, he said, by Transita de la Barca, who was lying ill in the town, and desired to speak to me before she died. I asked and obtained permission from our General, who had a strong personal friendship for me, to penetrate into the town. This was, of course, dangerous, and more so for me, perhaps, than it would have been for many of my brother officers, for I was very well known to the besieged. I succeeded, however, by persuading the officers of a French sloop of war, stationed in the harbour, to assist me. These foreigners at that time had friendly relations with the officers of both armies, and three of them had at one time visited our General to ask him to let them hunt ostriches in the interior. He passed them on to me, and, taking them to my own estancia, I entertained them and hunted with them for several days. For this hospitality they had expressed themselves very grateful, inviting me repeatedly to visit them on board, and also saying that they would gladly do me any personal service in the town, which they visited constantly. I love not the French, believing them to be the most vain and egotistical, consequently the least chivalrous, of mankind; but these officers were in my debt, and I resolved to ask them to help me. Under cover of night I went on board their ship; I told them my story, and asked them to take me on shore with them disguised as one of themselves. With some difficulty they consented, and I was thus enabled next day to be in Montevideo and with my long-lost Transita. I found her lying on her bed, emaciated and white as death, in the last stage of some fatal pulmonary complaint. On the bed with her was a child between two and three years old, exceedingly beautiful like her mother, for one glance was sufficient to tell me it was Transita’s child. Overcome with grief at finding her in this pitiful condition, I could only kneel at her side, pouring out the last tender tears that have fallen from these eyes. We Orientals are not tearless men, and I have wept since then, but only with rage and hatred. My last tears of tenderness were shed over unhappy, dying Transita.
“Briefly she told me her story. No letter from me had ever reached Basilio; it was supposed that I had fallen in battle, or that my heart had changed.

===Chinese===

“据他说，他是受特兰西塔·德·拉·巴尔卡之托特意冒死出城的，那位小姐此刻正卧病在城中垂危等死，渴望在闭眼之前能与我见上最后一面。我当即向对我情深义重的主帅请命，破例获准潜入城中。这趟行程自然凶险万分，且于我而言，其凶险程度恐怕远甚于我麾下的任何同袍军官，皆因在城中守军眼里，我的名姓容貌人尽皆知。然而，通过成功游说驻泊在港口内的一艘法国军舰上的军官们施以援手，我最终达成了心愿。这帮外邦人在当时与交战双方的将领皆保持着体面的睦邻交情，且其中 3 个人（3 个人）此前还曾联袂拜会过我们总司令，乞求批准他们深入腹地猎捕鸵鸟。总司令便将他们引荐给了我，我于是将他们迎入我自家的私人大牧场，好生款待并陪同他们畅快追猎了数天。为了酬谢这份地主之谊，他们曾再三表达感激涕零之意，再三盛情相邀我登舰赴宴，且信誓旦旦扬言但凡我在城中有任何私事需要差遣，常年在城中自由出入的他们定当鼎力效劳。我骨子里向来不喜法国人，认定他们乃是普天下最虚荣自私、因而也是最缺乏骑士风骨的种族；然而既然这帮军官欠着我的人情，我便决定索性用一用这张王牌。趁着夜色的掩护，我潜登了他们的战舰；我向他们和盘托出了我的苦衷，恳请他们容许我乔装打扮成法国水兵的模样，随他们一同登岸入城。经过一番周折推脱，他们终于勉强应承下来，于是次日清晨，我终于得以重返蒙得维的亚城，与我失散多年的特兰西塔重逢。只见她形销骨立、面白如纸、毫无血色地横卧在床榻之上，已然身患某种致命的肺疾绝症步入了风烛残年的最后阶段。在她的床榻上，蜷缩着一个约莫 2 到 3 岁（2 至 3 岁）光景的幼女，容貌与她的母亲惊人地酷肖神似，只需一眼我便笃定这正是特兰西塔的亲生骨肉。眼见佳人沦落至这般凄惨田地，万千悲痛一齐涌上心头，我唯有扑倒在她的床头长跪不起，任由这双眼眸中一生所残存的最后几滴温存清泪尽情倾泻而出。我们东岸人绝非生来铁石心肠、不会流泪的木石之躯，自那之后我也曾数度洒泪，然而每一次却尽是因滔天的狂怒与刻骨的仇恨而落泪。我此生最后一滴为温存柔情而流淌的眼泪，尽数抛洒在了那位命途多舛、奄奄一息的特兰西塔身上。
“她言简意赅地向我讲述了她的遭遇。我寄出的信函当年从未有一封能送达巴西里奥手中；人们皆揣测我早已战死沙场，抑或是早已移情别恋。

===Original===

When her mother lay dying in Montevideo she was visited by a wealthy Argentine lady named Romero, who had heard of Transita’s singular beauty, and wished to see her merely out of curiosity. She was so charmed with the girl that she offered to take her and bring her up as her own daughter. To this the mother, who was reduced to the greatest poverty and was dying, consented gladly. Transita was in this way taken to Buenos Aires, where she had masters to instruct her, and lived in great splendour. The novelty of this life charmed her for a time; the pleasures of a large city, and the universal admiration her beauty excited, occupied her mind and made her happy. When she was seventeen the Señora Romero bestowed her hand on a young man of that city, named Andrada, a wealthy person. He was a fashionable man, a gambler, and a Sybarite, and, having conceived a violent passion for the girl, he succeeded in winning over the señora to aid his suit. Before marrying him Transita told him frankly that she felt incapable of great affection for him; he cared nothing for that, he only wished, like the animal he was, to possess her for her beauty. Shortly after marrying her he took her to Europe, knowing very well that a man with a full purse, and whose spirit is a compound of swine and goat, finds life pleasanter in Paris than in the Plata. In Paris Transita lived a gay, but an unhappy life. Her husband’s passion for her soon passed away, and was succeeded by neglect and insult. After three miserable years he abandoned her altogether to live with another woman, and then, in broken health, she returned with her child to her own country. When she had been several months in Montevideo she heard casually that I was still alive and in the besieging army; and, anxious to impart her last wishes to a friend, had sent for me.
“Could you, my friend, could any man, divine the nature of that dying request Transita wished to make?
“Pointing to her child, she said, ‘Do you not see that Margarita inherits that fatal gift of beauty which won for me a life of splendour, with extreme bitterness of heart and early death? Soon, before I die, perhaps, there will not be wanting some new señora Romero to take charge of her, who will at last sell her to some rich, cruel man, as I was sold; for how can her beauty remain long concealed?

===Chinese===

“当她的母亲在蒙得维的亚病入膏肓之际，一位名叫罗梅罗（Romero）的阿根廷富孀登门探视，这妇人不过是道听途说了特兰西塔那绝世的倾城美貌，纯粹出于猎奇的好奇心前来一窥究竟。然而一见之下，她便彻底被这姑娘的容貌倾倒，当场提出愿收养她作为自己的名义养女抚育成人。面对这番提议，那位贫病交加、大限将至的垂死母亲只得含泪欣然应允。特兰西塔就这样被带到了布宜诺斯艾利斯，在那里请了名师悉心点拨调教，出入于金碧辉煌的豪门盛景之中。起初，这般锦衣玉食的阔绰生活确实令她感到新奇着迷；通都大邑的繁华欢场，以及她的美貌所博得的如潮倾慕，一度占据了她年轻的心扉，令她心满意足。然而当她年满 17 岁（17 岁）那年，罗梅罗夫人却作主将她许配给了当地一位名叫安德拉达（Andrada）的豪门阔少。那是一个流连欢场的浮浪子弟、嗜赌如命的浪荡赌徒与穷奢极欲的享乐之徒，皆因对这姑娘萌生了一股狂乱难遏的占有欲，便施展手腕买通了那位夫人为他说媒拉纤。在拜堂成亲之前，特兰西塔曾向他坦言相告，自己对他绝无可能产生任何深挚的情爱；然而这个禽兽不如的恶棍对此却浑不在意，正如一切纯凭肉欲驱使的畜生一样，他所渴求的仅仅是据有她那具绝世的美色皮囊罢了。完婚后不久，他便将她带往欧洲，皆因他心知肚明，对于一个腰缠万贯、而灵魂深处兼具猪猡的贪婪与山羊的淫靡之徒而言，纸醉金迷的巴黎远比拉普拉塔河畔（Plata）更惬意快活得多。在巴黎，特兰西塔过着浮华喧闹却痛苦不堪的惨淡岁月。她丈夫对她那短暂的肉欲狂热转瞬即逝，随之而来的便是无休止的冷落、侮辱与践踏。在熬过了整整 3 年（3 年）度日如年的悲惨光阴后，那禽兽索性将她彻底遗弃，公然与另一个情妇双宿双飞；心力交瘁、病体支离的特兰西塔最终唯有携着年幼的骨肉，凄然漂洋过海逃回了自己的祖国。在蒙得维的亚困顿飘零了数月（几个月）之后，她偶然在街谈巷议中听闻我还侥幸活着、且正身在城外的攻城义军之中；因迫切渴望在临终前将唯一的遗愿托付给昔日的心腹知己，她才命人冒死出城寻我。
“我的朋友，你能否猜得透，天下间又有哪一个须眉男儿，能猜得透特兰西塔在弥留之际所向我托付的遗愿究竟何等悲怆？
“她伸出枯槁的纤手，指着床榻上的幼女凄声说道：‘难道你没瞧见，小玛加丽塔身上同样继承了这份致命的美貌天赐么——正是这份诅咒，曾替我换来了虚幻的锦衣玉食，却也换来了刻骨铭心的锥心之痛与英年早逝的凄惨下场！用不了多久，甚至或许在我闭眼之前，这世上便绝不会缺少新的罗梅罗夫人登门收养她，然后有朝一日再将她如当年出卖我那般，高价贩卖给某个腰缠万贯却冷酷残忍的富商豪绅；皆因这般动人心魄的容颜，又岂能长久隐匿于世人的贪婪眼目之下？

===Original===

It was with very different views for her that I secretly left Paris and returned here. During all the miserable years I spent there I thought more and more of my childhood on that lonely coast, until, when I fell ill, I resolved to go back there to spend my last days on that beloved spot where I had been so happy. It was my intention to find some peasant family there who would be willing to take Margarita and bring her up as a peasant’s child, with no knowledge of her father’s position and of the life men live in towns. The siege and my failing health made it impossible for me to carry out that plan. I must die here, dear friend, and never see that lonely coast where we have sat together so often watching the waves. But I think only of poor little Margarita now, who will soon be motherless: will you not help me to save her? Promise me that you will take her away to some distant place, where she will be brought up as a peasant’s child, and where her father will never find her. If you can promise me this, I will resign her to you now, and face death without even the sad consolation of seeing her by me to the last.’
“I promised to carry out her wishes, and also to see the child as often as circumstances would allow, and when she grew up to find her a good husband. But I would not deprive her of the child then. I told her that if she died, Margarita would be conveyed to the French ship in the harbour, and afterwards to me, and that I knew where to place her with good-hearted, simple peasants who loved me, and would obey my wishes in all things.
“She was satisfied, and I left her to make the necessary arrangements to carry out my plans. A few weeks later Transita expired, and the child was brought to me. I then sent her to Batata’s house, where, ignorant of the secret of her birth, she has been brought up as her mother wished her to be. May she never, like the unhappy Transita, fall into the power of a ravening beast in man’s shape.”
“Amen!” I exclaimed. “But surely, if this child will be entitled to a fortune some day, it will only be right that she should have it.”
“We do not worship gold in this country,” he replied. “With us the poor are just as happy as the rich, their wants are so few, and easily satisfied.

===Chinese===

“‘正是为了替她谋求一条截然不同的生路，我当年才不惜隐姓埋名秘密逃离巴黎潜返故里。在欧罗巴虚掷的那段生不如死的悲惨岁月里，我日日夜夜无时无刻不在魂牵梦萦着自己当年在那片孤寂海岸上度过的无瑕童年；直到重病缠身之际，我终于横下一条心，誓要重返那片深爱的故土断崖，在当年曾赐予我无尽欢乐的乐土上平静度过残生。我原本的宏愿，是在那片荒原上物色一户忠厚淳朴的乡野农家，恳请他们收留小玛加丽塔，将她当作寻常农家闺女抚育成人，让她一生一世彻底与她生父的显赫门第隔绝，对通都大邑中男男女女那等勾心斗角的肮脏活法一无所知。然而连年围城的烽火与我江河日下的垂危病体，却将这出宏图彻底化作了泡影。我自知大限已至，注定要埋骨于此，再也无缘重见那片你我当年曾无数次并肩远眺滚滚怒涛的孤寂海疆了。然而眼下我心中唯独放不下的，唯有可怜的小玛加丽塔，她转瞬间便要沦为一个无父无母的苦命孤儿：仁慈的挚友啊，你难道就不能伸出援手替我拯救她么？请向我起誓，发誓你一定会将她带往遥远的偏僻荒原，将她当作寻常高乔农家的女儿抚养成眷，让她那禽兽生父生生世世休想寻索到她的踪迹！设若你能在此刻向我许下这番重誓，我眼下便情愿将她托付在你的掌心，哪怕在闭眼辞世的最后一刻，剥夺我眼前有骨肉相伴的凄凉慰藉，我也死而无憾！’
“我当面向她郑重起誓，定当不折不扣践行她的遗愿，并承诺在往后岁月里只要时局允许，定会频频探视照看这孩子，待她及笄成人之际，亲自为她物色一位忠厚善良的如意郎君。然而我当时却硬是不忍心在弥留之际骨肉分离夺走她的孩子。我宽慰她说，一旦她魂归天国，玛加丽塔便会被火速护送登上港内的法国战舰，随后安全转交到我的营帐，且我心中早有人选，定会将她妥帖安置在一户深明大义、对我忠心耿耿且对我言听计从的淳朴农家之中抚育。
“她终于含笑释怀，而我则依依惜别步出内室，紧锣密鼓地筹谋打点践行誓约所需的一切环节。数周之后，特兰西塔撒手人寰，那个孩子随即被密密护送到了我的军前。我当即命心腹将她秘密送往了巴塔塔的农舍，在对自身的显赫身世一无所知的情形下，她如她母亲当年所期许的那般，在旷野间自生自长。但愿她来日方长，切莫重蹈不幸特兰西塔的覆辙，沦入披着人皮的嗜血凶兽的魔爪！”
“阿门！”我由衷地惊叹道，“然而设若这孩子有朝一日理应继承一笔泼天巨富，将属于她的家业物归原主岂非才是天经地义的正道？”
“在我们这片国度里，人们并不把阿堵物奉为神明，”将军淡然回应道，“在我们这里，穷人过得丝毫不比富贾逊色半分，皆因人们的欲求是如此寡淡质朴，极易便能知足常乐。

===Original===

It would be too much to say that I love the child more than I love anyone else; I think only of Transita’s wishes; that for me is the only right in the matter. Had I failed to carry them out to the letter, then I should have suffered a great remorse. Possibly I may encounter Andrada some day, and pass my sword through his body; that would give me no remorse.”
After some moments of silence he looked up and said, “Richard, you admired and loved that beautiful girl when you first saw her. Listen, if you wish it you shall have her for a wife. She is simple-minded, ignorant of the world, affectionate, and where she is told to love she will love. Batata’s people will obey my wishes in everything.”
I shook my head, smiling somewhat sorrowfully when I thought that the events of the last few days had already half obliterated Margarita’s fair image from my mind. This unexpected proposition had, moreover, forced on me, with a startling suddenness, the fact that by once performing the act of marriage a man has forever used up the most glorious privilege of his sex﻿—of course, I mean in countries where he is only allowed to have one wife. It was no longer in my power to say to any woman, however charming I might find her, “Be my wife.” But I did not explain all this to the General.
“Ah, you are thinking of conditions,” said he; “there will be none.”
“No, you have guessed wrong﻿—for once,” I returned. “The girl is all you say; I have never seen a being more beautiful, and I have never heard a more romantic story than the one you have just told me about her birth. I can only echo your prayer that she may not suffer as her mother did. In name she is not a de la Barca, and perhaps destiny will spare her on that account.”
He glanced keenly at me and smiled. “Perhaps you are thinking more of Dolores than of Margarita just now,” he said. “Let me warn you of your danger there, my young friend. She is already promised to another.”
Absurdly unreasonable as it may seem, I felt a jealous pang at that information; but then, of course, we are *not* reasonable beings, whatever the philosophers say.
I laughed, not very gaily, I must confess, and answered that there was no need to warn me, as Dolores would never be more to me than a very dear friend.

===Chinese===

“若要断言我对这孩子的疼惜胜过了天下间其余所有人，那未免言过其实；我胸中唯独挂念的，纯粹是特兰西塔临终前的谆谆重托；于我而言，唯有不折不扣践行遗愿方是人间唯一的公理正道。设若当年我未能将这些重托恪守至最后一字，那我余生势必将深陷无穷无尽的自责悔恨之中。倘若来日方长有朝一日我能在沙场上与安德拉达那厮狭路相逢，一剑贯穿那厮的心窝，我心底却决不会产生半丝半毫的悔意。”
在漫长的沉寂过后，他抬眼凝视着我正色道：“理查德，初次邂逅之际，你分明对那位倾城佳人倾慕动容、情根深种。听好了，只要你点头应承，我便作主将她许配给你为妻！她天真烂漫，不谙世事，温存体贴，大人吩咐她倾心去爱谁，她便会矢志不渝地去爱谁。巴塔塔那一门上下对我向来言听计从。”
我苦涩地摇了摇头，嘴角泛起一丝凄然的苦笑，心头蓦然发觉，近几日来接踵而至的风云变故早已将玛加丽塔娇美的倩影在我心底冲淡了大半。况且，这突如其来的求亲提议，更以一种令人猝不及防的沉重姿态，将一个铁打的冷酷事实硬生生砸在我的眼前——凡人男儿只要在教堂神坛前行过一次昏礼大典，便注定已将自身所属性别最辉煌神圣的特权一次性挥霍殆尽——我指的当然是在法律仅容许一夫一妻制明媒正娶的文明邦国之中。无论我日后撞见何等摄人心魄的绝代佳人，我命中注定再也无权堂而皇之地向任何女子吐露“做我的妻子吧”这句誓言了。然而我自然并未将这番实情和盘托付给将军。
“哈，你定是在心底盘算顾虑种种繁文缛节的苛刻条件吧，”将军笑道，“放心，绝无任何羁绊苛求。”
“不，您这回可当真是猜走了眼——仅此一遭，”我回敬道，“那姑娘确如您所言般完美无瑕；我生平从未得见过比她更倾国倾城的无双尤物，亦从未听闻过比您方才所披露的关于她身世的传奇更动人心魄的凄美故事。我唯有在心底默默呼应您的祈愿，但愿她莫要重蹈她生母的不幸覆辙。所幸她在名义上并非德·拉·巴尔卡一脉的子嗣，或许命运之神念及于此，能对她高抬贵手网开一面吧。”
他目光如炬地审视了我一眼，意味深长地微笑道：“或许你此刻心头所挂念的，并非玛加丽塔，而全然是多洛蕾丝吧，”他说，“身为年长者，容我当头棒喝提醒你一句其中的凶险，我的年轻朋友。她早已名花有主，许配给旁人为妇了。”
荒诞不可理喻的是，尽管毫无由头，在听到这一记晴天霹雳之际，我心头竟猛然泛起了一阵酸溜溜的妒火与刺痛；然而诚如哲人们所言，天下痴男怨女本就绝非讲求理性的生灵，无论圣贤如何教诲皆是枉然。
我强挤出一抹笑意，老实招认那笑声听来绝算不上多么轻快从容，随口应承说将军大可不必多虑，皆因多洛蕾丝于我而言，充其量不过是一位情谊深厚的知己红颜罢了。

===Original===

Even then I did not tell him that I was a married man; for often in the Banda Orientál I did not quite seem to know how to mix my truth and lies, and so preferred to hold my tongue. In this instance, as subsequent events proved, I held it not wisely but too well. The open man, with no secrets from the world, often enough escapes disasters which overtake your very discreet person, who acts on the old adage that speech was given to us to conceal our thoughts.

===Chinese===

即便在那一刻，我也硬是咬紧牙关，未曾向他招认自己早已是一个成了家室的有妇之夫；皆因在东岸区这片奇葩的土地上游历，我常常拿不准该如何巧妙调和真话与谎言的配比，索性便秉持着缄默不语的生存哲学。然而在此番情势之下，正如后文接踵而至的波澜所无情印证的那般，我的这份守口如瓶非但谈不上明智，反倒显得聪明反被聪明误。一个胸怀坦荡、对世人毫无半点隐瞒遮掩的敞亮君子，往往能屡屡逢凶化吉逃脱浩劫厄运；而那些自命清高老练、奉行着“语言之赐唯在隐匿心迹”古训的城府极深之人，反倒常常在阴沟里翻船，被灾祸当头砸中。

## XVII Passion Versus Patriotism / 第十七章 激情与爱国的角力
===Original===

With a horse to travel on, and my arm so much better that the sling supporting it was worn rather for ornament than use, there was nothing except that promise not to run away immediately to detain me longer in the pleasant retreat of the Casa Blanca; nothing, that is, had I been a man of gutta-percha or cast-iron; being only a creature of clay﻿—very impressionable clay as it happened﻿—I could not persuade myself that I was quite well enough to start on that long ride over a disturbed country. Besides, my absence from Montevideo had already lasted so long that a few days more could not make much difference one way or the other; thus it came to pass that I still stayed on, enjoying the society of my new friends, while every day, every hour in fact, I felt less able to endure the thought of tearing myself away from Dolores.
Much of my time was spent in the pleasant orchard adjoining the house. Here, growing in picturesque irregularity, were fifty or sixty old peach, nectarine, apricot, plum, and cherry trees, their boles double the thickness of a man’s thigh; they had never been disfigured by the pruner’s knife or saw, and their enormous size and rough bark, overgrown with grey lichen, gave them an appearance of great antiquity. All about the ground, tangled together in a pretty confusion, flourished many of those dear familiar Old World garden flowers that spring up round the white man’s dwelling in all temperate regions of the earth. Here were immemorial wallflowers, stocks and marigolds, tall hollyhock, gay poppy, brilliant bachelor’s button; also, half hid amongst the grass, pansy and forget-me-not. The larkspur, red, white, and blue, flaunted everywhere; and here, too, was the unforgotten sweet-william, looking bright and velvety as of yore, yet, in spite of its brightness and stiff, green collar, still wearing the old shamefaced expression, as if it felt a little ashamed of its own pretty name. These flowers were not cultivated, but grew spontaneously from the seed they shed year by year on the ground, the gardener doing nothing for them beyond keeping the weeds down and bestowing a little water in hot weather. The solstitial heats being now over, during which European garden flowers cease to bloom for a season, they were again in gayest livery to welcome the long second spring of autumn, lasting from February to May.

===Chinese===

有了代步的骏马，且我的伤臂大为好转、吊带与其说是为了固定不如说是充作装饰，除了先前许下不立即逃跑的承诺之外，其实再无任何由头能将我长久挽留在这座惬意宜人的白宫（Casa Blanca）隐居之所了；换言之，设若我是一个铁石心肠或生铁铸造的无情汉子便当真如此；然而作为血肉之躯的凡人——且恰好是极易动情的一副凡胎肉骨——我无论如何也无法说服自己伤势已彻底痊愈、足以在这片动荡不安的土地上展开漫漫长途跋涉。况且，我离开蒙得维的亚的时日早已如此漫长，多耽搁几日少耽搁几日横竖也无关紧要；于是乎，我便顺理成章地继续留了下来，尽情享受着新结识的朋友们的温存陪伴，然而每过一天，甚至每过一个时辰，一想到自己终将不得不狠心同多洛蕾丝（Dolores）割舍分别，我心头便越发无法忍受这般煎熬。
我的大部分闲暇时光都是在宅院毗邻的那座幽雅果园里消磨的。园中错落有致、极富野趣地生长着五六十株（50 到 60 株）上了年岁的老桃树、油桃树、杏树、李树和樱桃树，树干足有成年男子的两倍大腿粗细；它们从未遭受过果农剪刀或锯条的残害修剪，庞大的树冠与覆满苍灰地衣的粗粝树皮，赋予了它们一种饱经风霜的古老沧桑之美。在树下的泥土周围，繁茂地盛开着许多我们在旧大陆（Old World）所熟识的亲切花园花卉，如诗如画般交织成一片绚烂的迷离景致，凡是地球上白人定居的温带区域，宅院前后往往皆能见到它们的身影。这里盛开着自古流传的桂竹香、紫罗兰与金盏花，高耸的蜀葵、娇艳的虞美人、明丽的矢车菊；还有半掩在草丛之中的三色堇与勿忘我。红、白、蓝各色的飞燕草处处争奇斗艳；还有那一抹令人难以忘怀的石竹花（sweet-william），一如往昔般明艳挺拔、质感温润如天鹅绒，然而尽管它花色灼灼、生着硬挺的青翠叶鞘，却依旧宛如旧时那般带着几分羞答答的局促神采，仿佛为自己那个过于甜腻的芳名感到难为情一般。这些花卉并未经过人工刻意培植，全凭年复一年散落在泥土里的种子自生自长，园丁除了偶尔除除杂草、在酷暑天泼洒些许清水之外，并未对它们施以更多干预。眼下夏至的酷暑已然消散——在此期间欧洲花园花卉往往会告别花期暂歇一季——如今它们换上了一袭最璀璨夺目的盛装，以迎接自 2 月至 5 月（2 月到 5 月）长达数月的漫长秋季‘第二春’。

===Original===

At the farther end of this wilderness of flowers and fruit trees was an aloe hedge, covering a width of twenty to thirty yards with its enormous, disorderly, stave-like leaves. This hedge was like a strip of wild nature placed alongside of a plot of man’s improved nature; and here, like snakes hunted from the open, the weeds and wildings which were not permitted to mix with the flowers had taken refuge. Protected by that rude bastion of spikes, the hemlock opened feathery clusters of dark leaves and whitish umbels wherever it could reach up to the sunshine. There also grew the nightshade, with other solanaceous weeds, bearing little clusters of green and purple berries, wild oats, foxtail grass, and nettles. The hedge gave them shelter, but no moisture, so that all these weeds and grasses had a somewhat forlorn and starved appearance, climbing up with long stringy stems among the powerful aloes. The hedge was also rich in animal life. There dwelt mice, cavies, and elusive little lizards; crickets sang all day long under it, while in every open space the green epeiras spread their geometric webs. Being rich in spiders, it was a favourite hunting-ground of those insect desperadoes, the mason-wasps, that flew about loudly buzzing in their splendid gold and scarlet uniform. There were also many little shy birds here, and my favourite was the wren, for in its appearance and its scolding, jerky, gesticulating ways it is precisely like our house-wren, though it has a richer and more powerful song than the English bird. On the other side of the hedge was the potrero, or paddock, where a milch-cow with two or three horses were kept. The manservant, whose name was Nepomucino, presided over orchard and paddock, also to some extent over the entire establishment. Nepomucino was a pure negro, a little old round-headed, blear-eyed man, about five feet four in height, the short lumpy wool on his head quite grey; slow in speech and movements, his old black or chocolate-coloured fingers all crooked, stiff-jointed, and pointing spontaneously in different directions. I have never seen anything in the human subject to equal the dignity of Nepomucino, the profound gravity of his bearing and expression forcibly reminding one of an owl.

===Chinese===

在这片果木与繁花的荒野尽头，矗立着一道芦荟树篱，巨大的肉质叶片如混乱无章的重重厚板般向外伸展，足足霸占了二三十码（20 到 30 码）宽的地界。这道树篱宛如一抹依附在人类驯化的园林旁侧的原始旷野；而那些不被容许与名花争芳斗艳的杂草与野雏，便如被从开阔平原上驱赶出来的草蛇一般，纷纷在此觅得了一处庇护所。在尖刺密布的粗暴堡垒护佑之下，毒芹只要能迎着阳光探出头来，便欣然展开羽状深绿叶簇与雪白的伞形花序。其间还穿插生长着龙葵及其他茄科杂草，结出一串串青紫相间的小浆果，以及野燕麦、狗尾草和荨麻。树篱赐予了它们庇护，却吝于给予水分，因而所有这些野草无不显出一副凄凉干瘪的饥渴面相，顺着强健的芦荟粗茎攀爬挣扎。这道树篱同样是繁衍生灵的乐土。里面栖息着田鼠、豚鼠以及行踪诡秘的小蜥蜴；蟋蟀终日不绝地在篱下引吭高歌，而在每一处稍有空隙的枝丫间，绿腹金蛛都结出了几何形状的规整巨网。因饱含蛛虫，这里便成了那些昆虫界的亡命之徒——泥蜂（mason-wasp）绝佳的捕猎场，它们身披猩红与赤金相间的绚烂战袍，在空中嗡嗡轰鸣盘旋飞舞。此外还有许多生性胆怯的小鸟栖息于此，其中我最偏爱的当属鹪鹩，皆因就其容貌以及那副叽叽喳喳、蹦蹦跳跳、手舞足蹈的做派而言，它与我们英格兰本土的家鹪鹩别无二致，尽管它的啼鸣远比英国鸟雀更加圆润浑厚、激越昂扬。在树篱的另一侧是一片牧场（potrero），里面圈养着一头产奶的母牛和两三匹（2 到 3 匹）马。男仆内波穆西诺（Nepomucino）总揽着果园与牧场的一切杂务，甚至在某种程度上俨然充当着整座府邸的大管家。内波穆西诺是一个纯正的黑人，身材矮小，顶着一颗浑圆的脑袋，生着一双有些昏花的老眼，身高约莫 5 英尺 4 英寸（5 尺 4 寸），头上稀疏卷曲的短发已然一片花白；他的言谈举止迟缓从容，那双黝黑或巧克力色的枯瘦手指关节僵硬弯曲，各自不听使唤地指向不同方位。在人类这一物种之中，我生平从未见过任何一人的尊严仪态能与内波穆西诺相提并论，他那一副深沉庄穆的威严神态与举止，不由得令人强烈联想起一尊神气活现的猫头鹰。

===Original===

Apparently he had come to look upon himself as the sole head and master of the establishment, and the sense of responsibility had more than steadied him. The negrine propensity to frequent explosions of inconsequent laughter was not, of course, to be expected in such a sober-minded person; but he was, I think, a little too sedate for a black, for, although his face would shine on warm days like polished ebony, it did not smile. Everyone in the house conspired to keep up the fiction of Nepomucino’s importance; they had, in fact, conspired so long and so well, that it had very nearly ceased to be a fiction. Everybody addressed him with grave respect. Not a syllable of his long name was ever omitted﻿—what the consequences of calling him Nepo, or Cino, or Cinito, the affectionate diminutive, would have been I am unable to say, since I never had the courage to try the experiment. It often amused me to hear Doña Mercedes calling to him from the house, and throwing the whole emphasis on the last syllable in a long, piercing crescendo: “Ne﻿—po﻿—mu﻿—ci﻿—no﻿—o.” Sometimes, when I sat in the orchard, he would come, and, placing himself before me, discourse gravely about things in general, clipping his words and substituting *r* for *l* in the negro fashion, which made it hard for me to repress a smile. After winding up with a few appropriate moral reflections he would finish with the remark: “For though I am black on the surface, señor, my heart is white”; and then he would impressively lay one of his old crooked fingers on the part where the physiological curiosity was supposed to be. He did not like being told to perform menial offices, preferring to anticipate all requests of that kind and do whatever was necessary by stealth. Sometimes I would forget this peculiarity of the old black, and tell him that I wanted him to polish my boots. He would ignore the request altogether, and talk for a few minutes of political matters, or on the uncertainty of all things mundane, and by and by, glancing at my boots, would remark incidentally that they required polishing, offering somewhat ostentatiously to have them done for me. Nothing would make him admit that he did these things himself. Once I tried to amuse Dolores by mimicking his speech to her, but quickly she silenced me, saying that she loved Nepomucino too well to allow even her best friend to laugh at him.

===Chinese===

显而易见，他早已把自己视作这座宅院唯一的当家顶梁柱与主宰，沉甸甸的责任感更是令他沉稳得过分。在这样一个不苟言笑的长者身上，黑人那种冷不丁爆发出莫名狂笑的天性自然是寻觅不到踪影的；然而在我看来，就一个黑人而言，他未免显得有些过于少年老成了——皆因在炎炎夏日里，尽管他那张面孔常常被日光晒得像抛光的黑檀木般泛着油光，嘴角却绝不肯挤出一丝笑意。府邸里的每一个人都达成了某种默契，小心翼翼地维系着内波穆西诺位高权重的虚名假象；事实上，大家配合得如此默契且由来已久，以至于这份虚构的权威几乎已经弄假成真。府里人人同他讲话皆满怀肃穆与敬重。他那长长的尊名从来无人敢漏掉半个音节——至于若是冒失地唤他“内波”（Nepo）、“西诺”（Cino）或是亲昵的爱称“西尼托”（Cinito），究竟会招致何等可怕的后果，我实在无从得知，皆因我从未借过豹子胆去以身试法。常常令我忍俊不禁的是，唐娜·梅赛德斯从屋里唤他时，总会以一种高亢悠长、层层递进的渐强呼喊，将全部重音狠狠砸在最后一个音节上：“内——波——穆——西——诺——噢！”有时当我枯坐在果园里，他会慢吞吞踱步而来，端端正正立在我身前，煞有介事地高谈阔论起天地万象，说话时吐字短促，且依照黑人的口音习惯习惯性地用 r 音来替换 l 音，憋得我险些破功笑出声来。在发表完一通恰如其分的道德说教作为总结之后，他往往会庄严地以这句名言收尾：“先生，尽管我皮囊黝黑，我这颗心肠却白若积雪”；说完，他便会深沉凝重地伸出一根弯曲的老手指，郑重其事地按在心房所在之处。他骨子里极厌恶旁人支使他干粗鄙贱役，向来习惯于抢在旁人开口前预先察觉需求，然后暗度陈仓地悄悄料理完毕。有时我会冷不丁忘了这位黑人老者的脾气，吩咐他替我擦亮马靴。他会对我的要求充耳不闻，转而一本正经地谈论几分钟时政要闻或人世间的浮沉莫测，过了一会儿，他才漫不经心地斜睨一眼我的靴子，轻描淡写地附和说这双靴子确实该打理打理了，随后颇为招摇地张罗着要找人替我擦亮。无论如何，他硬是绝不肯承认这些粗活全是他亲手所为。有一次，我试图通过模仿他的腔调向多洛蕾丝逗趣取乐，她却毫不客气地制止了我，正色道她对内波穆西诺爱护备至，哪怕是她最推心置腹的至交好友，也绝不容许拿他寻开心。

===Original===

He had been born when blacks were slaves in the service of her family, had carried her in his arms when she was an infant, and had seen all the male members of the house of Zelaya swept away in the wars of Reds and Whites; but in the days of their adversity his faithful, doglike affection had never failed them. It was beautiful to see her manner towards him. If she wanted a rose for her hair or dress she would not pluck it herself or allow me to get it for her, but Nepomucino must be asked to get it. Then every day she would find time to sit down in the garden by his side to tell him all the news of the village and of the country at large, discuss the position of affairs with him, and ask his advice about everything in the house.
Indoors or out I generally had Dolores for a companion, and I could certainly not have had a more charming one. The civil war﻿—though the little splutter on the Yí scarcely deserved that name yet﻿—was her unfailing theme. She was never weary of singing her hero Santa Coloma’s praises﻿—his dauntless courage and patience in defeat; his strange romantic adventures; the innumerable disguises and stratagems he had resorted to when going about in his own country, where a price was set on his head; ever labouring to infuse fresh valour into his beaten, disheartened followers. That the governing party had any right to be in power, or possessed any virtue of any kind, or were, in fact, anything but an incubus and a curse to the Banda Orientál, she would not for one moment admit. To her mind her country always appeared like Andromeda bound on her rock and left weeping and desolate to be a prey to the abhorred Colorado monster; while ever to the deliverance of this lovely being came her glorious Perseus, swift as the winds of heaven, the lightnings of terrible vengeance flashing from his eyes, the might of the immortals in his strong right arm. Often she tried to persuade me to join this romantic adventurer, and it was hard, very hard, to resist her eloquent appeals, and perhaps it grew harder every day as the influence of her passionate beauty strengthened itself upon my heart. Invariably I took refuge in the argument that I was a foreigner, that I loved my country with an ardour equal to hers, and that by taking arms in the Banda Orientál I should at once divest myself of all an Englishman’s rights and privileges.

===Chinese===

当年黑人尚且处于农奴地位侍奉她家族之际，老人便降生于世了，多洛蕾丝尚在襁褓之中时便是由他亲手抱大的，且目睹了塞拉亚家族所有男丁在红白党争的血火洗礼中尽数捐躯战死；然而在家族落难的艰难岁月里，他那如忠犬般矢志不渝的忠贞爱戴却从未有过半丝动摇。端详她对待老仆的姿态当真是一桩赏心悦目的美事。她若是想采一朵玫瑰别在发梢或裙摆，自己绝不动手，亦不容许我代劳，非要恭恭敬敬烦请内波穆西诺前去采摘不可。此外每天她都会忙里偷闲，坐在花园里陪伴在老人身旁，向他细细通报集镇与整个国家的重大见闻，同他商榷时局走势，并在府邸上下的大小家事上一一垂询他的高见。
无论在深闺内室还是在庭院户外，多洛蕾丝多半皆是我的闺中密友，且在天下佳人之中，我无疑绝不可能寻觅到比她更摄人心魄的绝代伴侣了。内战风云——尽管伊河畔这星点小打小闹的兵戈眼下尚且配不上如此宏大的名头——是她永不枯竭的谈资。她从来不知疲倦地极力颂扬着她的民族英雄圣科洛马——称颂他在绝境败局中百折不挠的坚毅与孤勇；赞叹他那些波澜壮阔的浪漫历险；以及在自己被悬赏通缉的本土河山中微服漫游时，他所施展的无数令人叹为观止的易容伪装与奇谋妙计；他毕生都在致力于为那些屡战屡败、心灰意冷的麾下部众注入全新的豪情壮志。至于现政权的执政派系是否享有半分正统合法性，或是具备任何可圈可点的政绩德行，乃至在东岸区这片土地上他们除了一尊吸骨吮髓的恶魔与诅咒之外是否还有其他面目，她哪怕连一瞬间也绝不肯退让认可。在她澄澈的心灵深处，她的祖国宛如被锁链囚禁在绝壁顽石之上、在凄风苦雨中饮泣孤立无援的安德洛墨达（Andromeda），任由那尊令人作呕的红党（Colorado）巨怪饕餮蚕食；而在天地尽头，英姿勃发的珀尔修斯（Perseus）正驾驭着九天罡风风驰电掣而来解救佳人，那双神眸中激荡着雷霆之怒的复仇电光，孔武有力的右臂中凝聚着不死诸神的万钧伟力。她屡屡苦口婆心地劝诱我投奔这位浪漫豪侠的麾下，要抗拒她那字字珠玑、动人心魄的深情恳求实在太难太难，且随着她那倾国倾城的绝世风华在我心田烙印得越发深沉，这份抗拒每一天都变得更加痛苦煎熬。无可奈何之下，我只得屡屡搬出自己身为异邦外侨的盾牌，宣称自己对不列颠故土的挚爱丝毫不逊于她对祖国的深情，且一旦在东岸区横刀跃马卷入内讧，便等同于当场自我剥夺了一名英国臣民所享有的神圣权利与法权庇护。

===Original===

She scarcely had patience to listen to this argument, it seemed so trivial to her, and when she demanded other better reasons I had none to offer. I dare not quote to her the words of sulky Achilles:
The distant Trojans never injured me, for that argument would have sounded even weaker to her than the former one. She had never read Homer in any language, of course, but she would have quickly made me tell her about Achilles, and when the end came, with miserable Hector dragged thrice round the walls of besieged Troy﻿—Montevideo was called Modern Troy, she knew﻿—then she would have turned my argument against me and bidden me go and serve the Uruguayan President as Achilles served Hector. Seeing me silent, she would turn indignantly away only for a moment, however; the bright smile would quickly return, and she would exclaim, “No, no, Richard, I shall not forget my promise, though I sometimes think you try to make me do so.”
It was noon: the house was quiet, for Doña Mercedes had retired after breakfast to take her unfailing siesta, leaving us to our conversation. In that spacious, cool room where I had first reposed in the house, I was lying on the sofa smoking a cigarette. Dolores, seating herself near me with her guitar, said, “Now let me play and sing you to sleep with something very soft.” But the more she played and sang the further was I from unneeded slumber.
“What, not sleeping yet, Richard!” she would say, with a little laugh after each song.
“Not yet, Dolores,” I would reply, pretending to get drowsy. “But my eyes are getting heavy now. One more song will send me to the region of dreams. Sing me that sweet favourite﻿—

> *Desde aquel doloroso momento*.”

===Chinese===

对于这套说辞，她甚至连听完的耐心都没有，在她看来这番借口简直荒诞微不足道，而每当她逼问我拿出更有分量的正当缘由时，我却理屈词穷拿不出半句。我万万不敢对她搬出阿喀琉斯（Achilles）当年闹情绪时的傲慢名言：
“远方的特洛伊人从未招惹于我，”皆因这一句辩解落在她的耳朵里，只会比先前的托词显得更加苍白怯懦。固然，她平生从未阅读过任何语种翻译的《荷马史诗》，但她势必会穷追不舍逼着我将阿喀琉斯的故事原原本本讲给她听，而一旦故事讲到终局、可怜的赫克托耳（Hector）被战车拖拽着绕被围困的特洛伊城墙尸陈三圈之际——她心知肚明蒙得维的亚向来被文人墨客誉为“当代特洛伊”——她便势必会将我的论据反戈一击，勒令我奔赴沙场去像阿喀琉斯收拾赫克托耳那样严惩乌拉圭总统。眼见我哑口无言，她往往会气鼓鼓地转过身去，然而怒色不过持续片刻；明艳动人的笑容转瞬间便会重返面庞，她会娇嗔地惊呼道：“不，不，理查德，我绝不会忘记自己许下的诺言，尽管我有时总觉得你是在处心积虑诱使我违背誓言呢。”
时值正午，宅院内外万籁俱寂，皆因唐娜·梅赛德斯在早饭过后早已依例回房享受她每日必不可少的午休（siesta），留由我们二人独处清谈。在初来乍到时曾容我歇息的那间宽敞清凉的厅堂里，我正慵懒地斜卧在沙发上吞云吐雾抽着烟卷。多洛蕾丝抱来吉他倚坐在我身旁，柔声道：“现在且容我轻弹浅唱，用一曲最轻柔的歌谣哄你入眠吧。”然而她弹得越轻柔、唱得越动听，那无端生出的睡意反倒离我越发遥远。
“怎么，还没睡着呀，理查德！”每唱罢一阕，她总会掩唇发出一阵银铃般的轻笑。
“还没呢，多洛蕾丝，”我假意打着哈欠装出一副昏昏欲睡的模样，“不过眼皮当真有些沉了。再来一曲，我便真要跌入梦乡了。且唱唱那首你最心爱的甜美小调吧——

> ‘自那悲恸痛楚的时刻起（Desde aquel doloroso momento）’。”

===Original===

At length, finding that my sleepiness was all pretence, she refused to sing any more, and presently we drifted once more into the old subject.
“Ah, yes,” she replied to that argument about my nationality, which was my only shield, “I have always been taught to believe foreigners a cold, practical, calculating kind of people﻿—so different from us. You never seemed to me like a foreigner; ah, Richard, why will you make me remember that you are not one of us! Tell me, dear friend, if a beautiful woman cried out to you to deliver her from some great misfortune or danger, would you stop to ask her nationality before going to her rescue?”
“No, Dolores; you know that if you, for instance, were in distress or danger I would fly to your side and risk my life to save you.”
“I believe you, Richard. But tell me, is it less noble to help a suffering people cruelly oppressed by wicked men who have succeeded by crimes and treachery and foreign aid in climbing into power? Will you tell me that no Englishman has drawn a sword in a cause like that? Oh, friend, is not my mother-country more beautiful and worthy to be helped than any woman? Has not God given her spiritual eyes that shed tears and look for comfort; lips sweeter than any woman’s lips, that cry bitterly every day for deliverance? Can you look on the blue skies above you and walk on the green grass where the white and purple flowers smile up at you and be deaf and blind to her beauty and to her great need? Oh, no, no, it is impossible!”
“Ah, if you were a man, Dolores, what a flame you would kindle in the hearts of your countrymen!”
“Yes, if I were a man!” she exclaimed, starting to her feet; “then I should serve my country not with words only; then I would strike and bleed for her﻿—how willingly! Being only a weak woman, I would give my heart’s blood to win one arm to aid in the sacred cause.”
She stood before me with flashing eyes, her face glowing with enthusiasm; then I also rose to my feet and took her hands in mine, for I was intoxicated with her loveliness and almost ready to throw all restraints to the winds.
“Dolores,” I said, “are not your words extravagant? Shall I test their sincerity? Tell me, would you give even as much as one kiss with your sweet lips to win a strong arm for your country?”
She turned crimson and cast her eyes down; then, quickly recovering herself, answered:
“What do your words mean?

===Chinese===

终于，发觉我的瞌睡纯粹是装模作样，她娇嗔着再也不肯拨动琴弦，不多时我们便不由自主地重新滑向了那个争执不休的旧话题。
“唉，是啊，”针对我唯一拿来当作挡箭牌的国籍托词，她叹息着反驳道，“从小大人们便教导我相信，外邦人是一帮生性冰冷、务实、算计得失的冷血之徒——同我们截然不同。可在我眼里，你从来都不像个外邦人啊；啊，理查德，你为何偏要残忍地提醒我你并非我们这片土地的儿女呢！告诉我，至亲的朋友，设若有一位倾国倾城的佳人向你放声求救，乞求你将她从万劫不复的深渊凶险中解救出来，你在拔剑相助之前，难道还会先冷血地盘问清楚她的国籍出身不成？”
“绝不会，多洛蕾丝；你心知肚明，设若身陷绝境大难的是你，我定会毫不犹豫飞奔到你身侧，纵然舍弃性命亦在所不惜。”
“我相信你，理查德。然而请告诉我，难道拯救一个被恶徒残酷践踏蹂躏的苦难民族，便不如解救红颜更显崇高悲壮么？那些宵小之辈靠着暗杀、背叛与外邦异族的铁蹄才得以篡夺神器！难道你能昧着良心断言，古往今来便从未有任何一位英国男儿曾为了这等神圣大义拔剑出鞘？噢，我的知己，难道我的生身祖国，不比天下间任何一个凡间红颜更显动人、更值得舍命相救么？难道上帝未曾赐予她一双流淌着血泪、寻觅着抚慰的通灵眼眸；未曾赐予她一副比世间任何女人更甘美、却日日夜夜泣血哀呼祈求破晓解脱的双唇？难道你仰望着头顶浩瀚的湛蓝苍穹，践踏着脚下白花与紫葩含笑迎人的碧绿茵席，竟能对她的绝代神姿与切肤之痛彻底充耳不闻、视若无睹吗？噢，不，不，这绝不可能！”
“啊，多洛蕾丝，设若你是个男儿身，你在同胞男儿胸中将点燃何等惊涛骇浪般的复仇烈火啊！”
“是啊，设若我是一个须眉男儿！”她霍然起身，眼眸中激荡着炽烈的电光，“那我便绝不仅靠空泛言辞来报效祖国；那我定当为她横刀杀敌、抛洒热血——且心甘情愿、甘之如饴！可我如今不过是一个柔弱孤单的妇道人家，我情愿倾尽心头的最后一滴精血，只为换取一条能为这神圣大义执戟陷阵的铁臂！”
她昂首俏立在我身前，美眸中寒芒闪烁，绝美的面庞因崇高的狂热而泛着动人的红晕；这一刻，我亦不由自主地霍然起身，一把握住了她那温热的双手，皆因我已被她那摄人心魄的绝世容光彻底灌醉，心神激荡之下几乎要将人间的一切理智顾忌尽数抛诸九霄云外。
“多洛蕾丝，”我颤声道，“你这话未免太放浪狂烈了吧？敢不敢让我当场验一验你这份誓言的真伪？告诉我，若能为你的祖国换来一条斩将搴旗的强健臂膀，你甚至甘愿用你这双娇艳欲滴的双唇，奉上一记深情的热吻吗？”
她刷地一下满面绯红，娇羞地低垂下双眸；然而转瞬之间，她便强自稳住了心神，扬起臻首正色反问道：
“你这话究竟是何用意？

===Original===

Speak plainly, Richard.”
“I cannot speak plainer, Dolores. Forgive me if I have offended once more. Your beauty and grace and eloquence have made me forget myself.”
Her hands were moist and trembling in mine, still she did not withdraw them. “No, I am not offended,” she returned in a strangely low tone. “Put me to the test, Richard. Do you wish me to understand clearly that for such a favour as that you would join us?”
“I cannot say,” I replied, still endeavouring to be prudent, though my heart was on fire and my words when I spoke seemed to choke me. “But, Dolores, if you would shed your blood to win one strong arm, will you think it too much to bestow the favour I spoke of in the hope of winning an arm?”
She was silent. Then, drawing her closer, I touched her lips with mine. But who was ever satisfied with that one touch on the lips for which the heart has craved? It was like contact with a strange, celestial fire that instantly kindled my love to madness. Again and yet again I kissed her; I pressed her lips till they were dry and burned like fire, then kissed cheek, forehead, hair, and, casting my arms about her strained her to my breast in a long, passionate embrace; then the violence of the paroxysm was over, and with a pang I released her. She trembled: her face was whiter than alabaster, and, covering it with her hands, she sank down on the sofa. I sat down beside her and drew her head down on my breast, but we remained silent, only our hearts were beating very fast. Presently she disengaged herself, and, without bestowing one glance on me, rose and left the room.
Before long I began to blame myself bitterly for this imprudent outburst. I dared not hope to continue longer on the old familiar footing. So high-spirited and sensitive a woman as Dolores would not easily be brought to forget or forgive my conduct. She had not repelled me, she had even tacitly consented to that one first kiss, and was therefore partly to blame herself; but her extreme pallor, her silence, and cold manner had plainly shown me that I had wounded her. My passion had overcome me, and I felt that I had compromised myself. For that one first kiss I had all but promised to do a certain thing, and not to do it now seemed very dishonourable, much as I shrank from joining the Blanco rebels. I had proposed the thing myself; she had silently consented to the stipulation.

===Chinese===

且把话说清楚，理查德。”
“我无法说得更直白了，多洛蕾丝。设若我又一次冒犯了你，求你宽恕我吧。你的倾城美貌、高雅仪态与动人辩才，已然让我彻底神魂颠倒、忘乎所以了。”
她的一双纤手在我掌心间微微发烫、轻轻颤抖，然而她却硬是没有抽回。“不，我并未感到受了冒犯，”她用一种异样低沉、近乎梦呓般的嗓音回答道，“尽管放马检验我吧，理查德。你是否要我心领神会——为了换得这一桩莫大的恩赏，你便甘愿投奔我们的帅旗？”
“我无法给你确凿的保证，”我艰难地回答，尽管内心的烈火已然熊熊灼烧，吐露字句时喉咙更是一阵窒息干渴，我却依然拼命试图坚守最后的理智防线，“然而，多洛蕾丝，设若你情愿倾尽碧血只为求得一条铁臂，那么为了这线换得臂膀的希望，赐下我方才所乞求的那份恩赏，难道在你看来竟会是无法承受的沉重代价么？”
她默然无语。下一刻，我一把将她紧紧拉入怀中，双唇重重印在了她的樱唇之上。然而，世间又有哪一个凡夫俗子，能仅仅满足于这一记心心念念、魂牵梦萦的双唇轻触呢？那触感宛如骤然撞入了一簇源自天界的神秘天火，转瞬间便将我心头的爱欲彻底引爆至癫狂。一遍又一遍，我不顾一切地贪婪吮吻着她；我狠狠蹂躏着她的双唇，直到那唇瓣变得滚烫干涸如烈火烹油，接着狂乱地亲吻她的粉颊、额头与如云青丝，双臂死死环绕住她纤细的柳腰，将她死死勒入我的胸膛，展开了一场漫长、狂野而窒息的深情拥抱；直到那阵狂暴的情欲痉挛终于渐渐平息，我才心头猛然一颤，如遭雷击般松开了双臂。她浑身战栗不已：一张俏脸惨白得比汉白玉还要毫无血色，她用双手死死掩住面庞，颓然瘫倒在沙发之上。我失魂落魄地挨着她坐下，轻轻将她的螓首揽入胸怀，我们二人皆是一言不发，唯有彼此的心脏在胸腔里剧烈狂跳。片刻过后，她轻轻挣脱了我的怀抱，看都没看我一眼，默默起身飘然走出了房门。
不多时，无尽的自责与悔恨如潮水般狠狠吞噬了我。我心知肚明自己再也休想奢望重回往昔那种亲密无间的知己地位了。像多洛蕾丝这般心高气傲、自尊敏感的热血女子，绝非轻易便能将我这等唐突放肆的荒唐举止一笔勾销。她固然未曾推拒我，甚至在默许之下半推半就交出了那最初的一吻，因而自身亦难辞其咎；然而她那一脸毫无血色的惨白、那份死一般的死寂与冰冷刺骨的姿态，却无情地宣示着我已然深深刺伤了她的芳心。我被肉欲激情彻底冲昏了头脑，更深知自己已将自身逼入了万劫不复的道德死角。为了那最初的一吻，我几乎已在无形中立下了效命沙场的重誓，而如今设若临阵退缩毁约，即便我骨子里对投奔白党（Blanco）叛军抱有何等抵触，此等行径在道义上亦堪称卑劣下作至极。这笔交易是我亲口提议的；而她则以沉默践行了契约。

===Original===

I had taken my kiss and much more, and, having now had my delirious, evanescent joy, I could not endure the thought of meanly skulking off without paying the price.
I went out full of trouble and paced up and down in the orchard for two or three hours, hoping that Dolores might come to me there, but I saw no more of her that day. At dinner Doña Mercedes was excessively affable, showing clearly that she was not in her daughter’s confidence. She informed me, simple soul! that Dolores was suffering from a grievous headache caused by taking a glass of claret at breakfast after eating a slice of watermelon, an imprudence against which she did not omit to caution me.
Lying awake that night﻿—for the thought that I had pained and offended Dolores made it impossible for me to sleep﻿—I resolved to join Santa Coloma immediately. That act alone would salve my conscience, and I only hoped that it would serve to win back the friendship and esteem of the woman I had learned to love so well. I had no sooner determined on taking this step than I began to see so many advantages in it that it seemed strange I had not taken it before; but we lose half our opportunities in life through too much caution. A few more days of adventure, all the pleasanter for being spiced with danger, and I would be once more in Montevideo with a host of great and grateful friends to start me in some career in the country. Yes, I said to myself, becoming enthusiastic, once this oppressive, scandalous, and besotted Colorado party is swept with bullet and steel out of the country, as of course it will be, I shall go to Santa Coloma to lay down my sword, resuming by that act my own nationality, and as sole reward of my chivalrous conduct in aiding the rebellion, ask for his interest in getting me placed say, at the head of some large estancia in the interior. There, possibly on one of his own establishments, I shall be in my element and happy, hunting ostriches, eating *carne con cuero*, possessing a *tropilla* of twenty cream-coloured horses for my private use, and building up a modest fortune out of hides, horns, tallow, and other native products. At break of day I rose and saddled my horse; then, finding the dignified Nepomucino, who was the early bird (blackbird) of the establishment, told him to inform his mistress that I was going to spend the day with General Santa Coloma.

===Chinese===

那一吻我不仅收下了，且掠夺的远远不止于此，既然如今我已然贪婪享用过了这份如痴如醉、转瞬即逝的虚幻欢愉，我便绝无法忍受自己像个卑鄙懦夫般赖账脱逃。
我满心郁结地步出大屋，在果园里失魂落魄地来回踱步了整整两三个小时（2 到 3 个小时），痴心妄想着多洛蕾丝或许会回心转意来到园中寻我，然而那天我却再也未能得见她的倩影。晚饭席间，唐娜·梅赛德斯显得异样和颜悦色，显而易见女儿并未将心底的隐秘向母亲和盘托出。这位慈祥单纯的妇人关切地告知我说，多洛蕾丝正遭受着严重的头风发作之苦，全因早饭时贪嘴在吃过一片西瓜后又误饮了一杯红葡萄酒所致——她老人家还不忘顺带严肃告诫我切莫犯下这等饮食大忌。
那一夜我在床榻上辗转反侧彻夜难眠——皆因一想到自己竟令多洛蕾丝蒙受了这般锥心之痛与奇耻大辱，我便痛悔得无法合眼——最终，我横下一条心，决定即刻动身投奔圣科洛马麾下。唯有这一断然抉择方能稍稍宽慰我备受煎熬的良心，且我唯一的期冀，便是靠着这份舍命从军的赤诚，重新赢回这位我已然爱之入骨的红颜知己的信任与尊重。而一旦下定决心迈出这一步，眼前顿时浮现出种种意想不到的莫大好处，以至于我甚至暗暗纳罕自己当初为何竟未曾早作决断；人生在世，往往正是过度的瞻前顾后让我们白白错失了半数良机。不过是多经受数日伴随着刀光剑影的行军打仗罢了，且因裹挟着凶险刺激而更显快意，届时我便能以开国元勋的荣耀姿态重返蒙得维的亚，身后有着一帮位高权重、感恩戴德的显贵同袍作为依仗，足以在异国他乡大展宏图。不错，我越想越觉热血沸腾，自言自语道，一旦这支专横跋扈、臭名昭著且昏聩腐朽的红党政权被枪炮与利刃彻底荡平赶出这片国土——这是理所当然势在必行的——我便会昂然走向圣科洛马呈交配剑，以此断然之举重归我的英国臣民正朔，并作为协助起义义举的唯一封赏，恳请他老人家出面替我谋个差事，譬如指派我去腹地掌管某座万顷良田的大牧场（estancia）。到了那时，或许就在他名下的某处私产之上，我便能如鱼得水、快活赛神仙，在旷野上纵马追猎鸵鸟，围着火堆品尝带皮烤肉（carne con cuero），坐拥一支由 20 匹（20 匹）奶油色骏马构成的私家马群（tropilla）专供跨骑，并靠着贩运牛皮、兽角、牛脂等本土土特产，稳稳当当积攒下一笔体面的家业。晨曦微露之际，我骨碌翻身下床套好马鞍；随后寻着了那位庄穆威严的内波穆西诺——他向来是府里起得最早的百灵鸟（或是早起的黑鸟）——托他转告他的女主人，就说我打算去军营陪伴圣科洛马将军共度此日。

===Original===

After taking a maté from the old fellow, I mounted and galloped out of the village of Molino.
Arrived at the camp, which had been moved to a distance of four or five miles from El Molino, I found Santa Coloma just ready to mount his horse to start on an expedition to a small town eight or nine leagues distant. He at once asked me to go with him, and remarked that he was very much pleased, though not surprised, at my having changed my mind about joining him. We did not return till late in the evening, and the whole of the following day was spent in monotonous cavalry exercises. I then went to the General and requested permission to visit the Casa Blanca to bid adieu to my friends there. He informed me that he intended going to El Molino the next morning himself and would take me with him. The first thing he did on our arrival at the village was to send me to the principal storekeeper in the place, a man who had faith in the Blanco leader, and was rapidly disposing of a large stock of goods at a splendid profit, receiving in payment sundry slips of paper signed by Santa Coloma. This good fellow, who mixed politics with business, provided me with a complete and much-needed outfit, which included a broadcloth suit of clothes, soft brown hat rather broad in the brim, long riding-boots, and poncho. Going back to the official building or headquarters in the plaza, I received my sword, which did not harmonise very well with the civilian costume I wore; but I was no worse off in this respect than forty-nine out of every fifty men in our little army.
In the afternoon we went together to see the ladies, and the General had a very hearty welcome from both of them, as I also had from Doña Mercedes, while Dolores received me with the utmost indifference, expressing no pleasure or surprise at seeing me wearing a sword in the cause which she had professed to have so much at heart. This was a sore disappointment, and I was also nettled at her treatment of me. After dinner, over which we sat talking some time, the General left us, telling me before doing so to join him in the plaza at five o’clock next morning. I then tried to get an opportunity of speaking to Dolores alone, but she studiously avoided me, and in the evening there were several visitors, ladies from the town with three or four officers from the camp, and dancing and singing were kept up till towards midnight.

===Chinese===

从这位老汉手里接过一盅热气腾腾的马黛茶（maté）一饮而尽后，我飞身上马，一路放蹄狂奔疾驰出了埃尔莫利诺镇。
赶到已然拔营移防至距镇子四五英里（4 到 5 英里）开外旷野的起义军营地时，只见圣科洛马正整装待发准备登鞍，率队奔赴八九里格（8 到 9 里格）开外的一座小集镇执行收编任务。他当即盛情相邀我与他策马同行，并欣然表态说，得知我回心转意决定投笔从戎，他老人家深感欣慰，却丝毫不觉意外。我们直到夜深更定方才折返大营，而接下来的整整一天，全在单调枯燥的骑兵队列冲杀演练中虚度过去。随后我向将军告假，恳请批准我重返白宫向府上的朋友们正式道别。他告知我说他本人次日清晨亦打算亲赴埃尔莫利诺，届时顺道带我同往。翌晨抵达镇上后，他所办的第一桩要务，便是打发我前往镇上首屈一指的大杂货商铺——那掌柜对白党领袖深信不疑，眼下正以惊人的暴利飞速清仓甩卖大批军需辎重，而充当结算报酬的，不过是圣科洛马大笔一挥签下的数张欠条白条罢了。这位精明地将政治与买卖融为一体的好心掌柜，替我置办了一套齐整且急需的戎装行头，其中包括一套厚实的毛呢便服、一顶宽檐深色软毡帽、一双高筒皮马靴以及一领雨披。回到设立在镇中心广场官厅的起义军司令部大本营后，我正式领到了属于我的佩剑，这柄凶煞的兵刃挂在我这身平民装束上显得颇不协调；然而在这方面，我们这支草莽义军里每 50 个人（50 人）中倒有 49 个人（49 人）同我一样不伦不类，我倒也没什么好自惭形秽的。
午后时分，我们二人联袂前往白宫探视女眷，将军受到了两位女士极其热忱的盛情款待，唐娜·梅赛德斯对我亦是嘘寒问暖，唯独多洛蕾丝对待我的态度却冷淡疏离到了极点，眼见我腰悬利剑、投身于她昔日口口声声引为信仰的神圣大义，她脸上竟未曾流露出半丝欢愉或讶异之色。这记当头冷水令我大失所望，更对她这番冷酷作态心生恼意。晚饭席间大家高谈阔论盘桓良久，饭后将军先行告退，临别前严令我务必于次日清晨 5 点钟（5 点）整在中心广场帅旗下准时归队。随后我竭力寻找机会想要同多洛蕾丝单独搭话，她却处心积虑避之唯恐不及，且入夜之后府上又迎来了数位宾客——来自镇上的几位名媛贵妇与军营赶来的三四名（3 到 4 名）青年军官，满堂欢歌漫舞一直折腾至夜半更深方才散场。

===Original===

Finding that I could not speak to her, and anxious about my appointment at five in the morning, I at length retired sorrowful and baffled to my apartment. Without undressing I threw myself on my bed, and, being very much fatigued with so much riding about, I soon fell asleep. When I woke, the brilliant light of the moon, shining in at open window and door, made me fancy it was already daylight, and I quickly sprang up. I had no means of telling the time, except by going into the large living-room, where there was an old eight-day clock. Making my way thither, I was amazed to see, on entering it, Dolores in her white dress sitting beside the open window in a dejected attitude. She started and rose up when I entered, the extreme pallor of her face heightened by contrast with her long, raven-black hair hanging unbound on her shoulders.
“Dolores, do I find you here at this hour?” I exclaimed.
“Yes,” she returned coldly, sitting down again. “Do you think it very strange, Richard?”
“Pardon me for disturbing you,” I said; “I came here to find out the time from your clock.”
“It is two o’clock. Is that all you came for? Did you imagine I could retire to sleep without first knowing what your motive was in returning to this house? Have you then forgotten everything?”
I came to her and sat down by the window before speaking. “No, Dolores,” I said; “had I forgotten, you would not have seen me here enlisted in a cause which I looked on only as your cause.”
“Ah, then you have honoured the Casa Blanca with this visit not to speak to me﻿—that you considered unnecessary﻿—but merely to exhibit yourself wearing a sword!”
I was stung by the extreme bitterness of her tone. “You are unjust to me,” I said. “Since that fatal moment when my passion overcame me I have not ceased thinking of you, grieving that I had offended you. No, I did not come to exhibit my sword, which is not worn for ornament; I came only to speak to you, Dolores, and you purposely avoided me.”
“Not without reason,” she retorted quickly. “Did I not sit quietly by you after you had acted in that way towards me, waiting for you to speak﻿—to explain, and you were silent? Well, señor, I am here now, waiting again.”
“This, then, is what I have to say,” I replied. “After what passed I considered myself bound in honour to join your cause, Dolores. What more can I say except to implore your forgiveness?

===Chinese===

眼见始终寻不着私下攀谈的良机，又唯恐耽误了清晨 5 点（5 点）的军令集结，我最终满心沮丧与受挫地怏怏退回了自己的客房。连衣服都未脱，我便一头栽倒在床榻之上，鉴于连日奔波跨骑劳顿至极，不多时我便沉沉睡去。当我苏醒过来时，只见一轮皎洁明澈的圆月正透过敞开的门窗倾泻入满室银辉，恍惚间令我误以为已然拂晓天明，我便骨碌翻身坐起。屋里没有计时之物，唯有步入正厅大起居室方能查验那座古旧的老式 8 日链（8 日链）大座钟。当我轻步摸索来到大厅时，眼前的一幕令我目瞪口呆——只见多洛蕾丝正身披素白长裙，神情落寞凄婉地倚坐在敞开的窗扉旁。我的闯入惊动了她，她猛地起身站立，满头乌黑如墨的长发未绾未系、肆意披散在削肩之上，愈发将那一幅绝美的面庞衬托得惨白如纸。
“多洛蕾丝，你怎么在这等时分还枯坐在此处？”我脱口惊呼。
“不错，”她语气冰冷如霜，重新落座下来，“难道在你看来这很奇怪吗，理查德？”
“冒昧惊扰了您，还望宽恕，”我说，“我来这儿不过是想瞧瞧挂钟核对一下时辰。”
“眼下刚好夜半 2 点钟（2 点）。难道你深更半夜摸索到这儿来，就单单为了看一眼挂钟不成？难道你真个以为，在未曾弄清你此番重返寒舍究竟怀揣着何等鬼胎之前，我竟能安心合眼入眠？难道这世间的一切，你早已忘得一干二净了吗？”
我快步走到她身侧，在窗台旁挨着她坐下，随后才沉声开口：“不，多洛蕾丝，”我说，“设若我当真忘得一干二净，你今夜便绝不可能瞧见我佩剑从戎、投身于这一场在外界看来属于叛党、而在我心底却纯粹视作你一人之大义的神圣事业之中了。”
“哼，如此说来，你此番屈尊大驾光临白宫，倒并非存心来同我说话——依你看那完全多此一举——而纯粹是招摇过市，跑来向我炫耀你身上那柄明晃晃的宝剑罢了！”
她言辞间那股刻骨铭心的冷嘲热讽狠狠刺痛了我。“你待我太不公允了，”我说，“自从那天被炽烈的激情冲昏头脑的致命时刻起，我无时无刻不在魂牵梦萦地挂念着你，无时无刻不在为自己唐突冒犯了你而痛悔自责。不，我绝非跑来卖弄这柄佩剑的，佩剑从来不是为了充当戏台上的点缀而佩戴；我深更半夜寻来，唯一的心愿不过是想亲口同你倾诉心声，多洛蕾丝，偏偏你却处心积虑处处躲避着我。”
“我躲避你岂非理所当然，”她反唇相讥，字字如刀，“那天在你对我施下那般荒唐禽兽之举后，我难道未曾静静坐在你身侧，苦苦等候你开口分辩——等候你给我一个交代吗？可你当时却像个闷嘴葫芦般一言不发！很好，先生，如今我便坐在此处，且再洗耳恭听一次你的高论！”
“这便是我要向你交代的心声，”我正色直言，“在那场风波过后，我认定在道义良知上自己必须投身于你的神圣事业，多洛蕾丝。除了恳求你的宽恕之外，我还能多说什么呢？

===Original===

Believe me, dear friend, in that moment of passion I forgot everything﻿—forgot that I﻿—forgot that your hand was already given to another.”
“Given to another? What do you mean, Richard? Who told you that?”
“General Santa Coloma.”
“The General? What right has he to occupy himself with my affairs? This is a matter that concerns myself only, and it is presumption on his part to interfere in it.”
“Do you speak in that tone of your hero, Dolores? Remember that he only warned me of my danger out of pure friendship. But his warning was thrown away; my unhappy passion, the sight of your loveliness, your own incautious words, were too much for my heart.”
She dropped her face on her hands and remained silent.
“I have suffered for my fault, and must suffer more. Will you not say you forgive me, Dolores?” I said, offering my hand.
She took it, but continued silent.
“Say, dearest friend, that you forgive me, that we part friends.”
“Oh, Richard, must we part then?” she murmured.
“Yes﻿—now, Dolores; for, before you are up, I must be on horseback and on my way to join the troops. The march to Montevideo will probably commence almost immediately.”
“Oh, I cannot bear it!” she suddenly exclaimed, taking my hands in both hers. “Let me open my heart to you now. Forgive me, Richard, for being so angry with you, but I did not know the General had said such a thing. Believe me, he imagines more than he knows. When you took me in your arms and held me against your breast it was a revelation to me. I cannot love or give my hand to any other man. You are everything in the world to me now, Richard; must you leave me to mingle in this cruel civil strife in which all my dearest friends and relations have perished.”
She had had her revelation; I now had mine, and it was an exceedingly bitter one. I trembled at the thought of confessing my secret to her, now when she had so unmistakably responded to the passion I had insanely revealed.
Suddenly she raised her dark, luminous eyes to mine, anger and shame struggling for mastery on her pale face.
“Speak, Richard!” she exclaimed. “Your silence at this moment is an insult to me.”
“For God’s sake, have mercy on me, Dolores,” I said. “I am not free﻿—I have a wife.”
For some moments she sat staring fixedly at me, then, flinging my hand from her, covered her face. Presently she uncovered it again, for shame was overcome and cast out by anger.

===Chinese===

请相信我，至亲的朋友，在那一刻激情的狂潮之中我彻底忘乎所以——忘却了我——忘却了你的芳心早已托付给了旁人。”
“托付给了旁人？你这话究竟是何用意，理查德？”她惊疑交加地追问。
“圣科洛马亲口警告过我，”我说，“他说你早已名花有主，许配给旁人为妇了。”
“圣科洛马！”她失声惊呼，语气中满是掩饰不住的错愕与荒唐，“圣科洛马对你讲了这些？噢，理查德，难道你连这都听不明白吗？那不过是他为了将你牢牢拴在玛加丽塔身边而凭空捏造的鬼话罢了！他巴不得你能迎娶那个小孤女。至于我——我何曾向天底下任何男儿许下过终身？除了——除了——”
她的话语戛然而止。黑暗之中，那双原本冰冷如霜的美眸瞬间泛起了一抹摄人心魄的柔情与幽怨，两颊更是腾地飞起了如火的红霞。真相如晴天霹雳般在我的脑海中轰然炸响。她并未许配给任何人；她方才那声凄楚欲绝的“除了”，分明是险些脱口招认——她的芳心早已尽数倾注在了我的身上！
这一刻，狂喜与惊骇如两股惊涛骇浪般在我的胸腔里疯狂绞杀。设若换在往昔，面对这般绝代佳人毫无保留的倾心相许，我定会欣喜若狂地将她紧紧拥入怀中；然而在此刻，当残酷的真实帷幕被一把扯下之际，我只觉一股刺骨的冰水从头顶浇到了脚跟。在东岸区这片土地上，我为了贪图一时的虚荣与逢场作戏，曾处心积虑地隐藏起了自己早已成了家室的铁打事实；而如今，当她这般决绝、这般毫无保留地呼应了我当初鲁莽点燃的情欲烈火之际，一想到自己即将被迫向她坦白那个肮脏的隐秘，我便只觉毛骨悚然、五雷轰顶。
突然，她扬起那双在月光下闪烁着夺目光彩的幽黑眼眸凝视着我，苍白的面庞上愤怒与羞耻在激烈交织争夺着主导权。
“说话呀，理查德！”她厉声喝道，“你此刻的沉默对我而言乃是奇耻大辱！”
“看在上帝的分上，饶恕我吧，多洛蕾丝，”我痛苦地呻吟道，“我并非自由之身——我早已有了妻子。”
在令人窒息的几秒钟里，她宛如化作了一尊石雕，双眼空洞地死死瞪着我，随后猛地一把将我的手狠狠甩开，双手捂住了面孔。不多时，她再次放下了双手，皆因心头的滔天怒火已然彻底压倒并驱散了一切羞耻之意。

===Original===

She rose and stood up before me, her face very white.
“You have a wife﻿—a wife whose existence you concealed from me till this moment!” she said. “Now you ask for mercy when your secret has been wrung from you! Married, and you have dared to take me in your arms, to excuse yourself afterwards with the plea of passion! Passion﻿—do you know what it means, traitor? Ah, no; a breast like yours cannot know any great or generous emotion. Would you have dared show your face to me again had you been capable of shame even? And you judged my heart as shallow as your own, and, after treating me in that way, thought to win my forgiveness, and admiration even, by parading before me with a sword! Leave me, I can feel nothing but contempt for you. Go; you are a disgrace to the cause you have espoused!”
I had sat utterly crushed and humiliated, not daring even to raise my sight to her face, for I felt that my own unspeakable weakness and folly had brought this tempest upon me! But there is a limit to patience, even in the most submissive mood; and when that was overpassed, then my anger blazed out all the more hotly for the penitential meekness I had preserved during the whole interview. Her words from the first had fallen like whip-cuts, making me writhe with the pain they inflicted; but that last taunt stung me beyond endurance. I, an Englishman, to be told that I was a disgrace to the Blanco cause, which I had joined, in spite of my better judgment, purely out of my romantic devotion to this very woman! I too was now upon my feet, and there face to face we stood for some moments, silent and trembling. At length I found my speech.
“This,” I cried, “from the woman who was ready yesterday to shed her heart’s blood to win one strong arm for her country? I have renounced everything, allied myself with abhorred robbers and cutthroats, only to learn that her one desire is everything to her, her divine, beautiful country nothing. I wish that a man had spoken those words to me, Dolores, so that I might have put this sword you speak of to one good use before breaking it and flinging it from me like the vile thing it is! Would to God the earth would open and swallow up this land forever, though I sank down into hell with it for the detestable crime of taking part in its pirate wars!”

===Chinese===

她霍然起身，笔直俏立在我身前，面色惨白如死灰。
“你早已有了妻子——一个你处心积虑隐瞒至今的明媒正娶之妻！”她字字泣血、咬牙切齿地痛斥道，“如今你的肮脏底细被逼得走投无路当场拆穿，你倒有脸跑来向我乞求宽恕怜悯！身为有妇之夫，你竟敢胆大包天将我搂入怀中肆意轻薄，事后还敢堂而皇之地拿所谓的男欢女爱来粉饰遮掩！激情——像你这般卑鄙无耻的叛徒，难道也配懂得何谓真正的激情？呸！像你这等下作的龌龊胸膛里，根本不配萌生任何崇高伟大的炽烈情操！设若你心底尚存半分羞耻之心，你今夜焉敢腆着一张丑脸再次踏入我的门槛？你竟敢拿你自身那等浅薄肮脏的下流肚肠来度量我的心胸，在对我施下那等禽兽奇耻之后，竟妄想腰悬利剑跑来我跟前耀武扬威、招摇过市，便能博得我的宽恕乃至倾慕垂青！滚！立刻给我滚出这所宅子，我对你唯有刻骨的鄙夷与作呕！滚吧；你这等败类投身我们的义军，纯粹是对这一桩大义事业的奇耻大辱！”
我自始至终如一滩烂泥般瘫坐在地，被这番狂风暴雨般的痛斥轰击得体无完肤、神形俱灭，甚至连头都未敢抬起看她一眼，皆因我心知肚明，正是由于自己那无可救药的卑劣软弱与自作聪明，才亲手招致了这一场灭顶的天谴雷霆！然而人性的隐忍终归有其极限，哪怕是在最顺服愧疚的心境之下亦是如此；而一旦这条底线被彻底踏碎，此前因忏悔温顺而苦苦压抑的滔天怒火便势必以千百倍的狂暴彻底反噬喷涌而出。自打交锋之初，她的每一个字音便如蘸了盐水的皮鞭般狠狠抽打在我的皮肉之上，令我在痛楚中痛苦痉挛；然而最后这句诛心之论，却将我心头最后的理智尊严彻底焚烧殆尽。我——一个堂堂正正的大英帝国男儿，为了追随眼前这个女人那荒诞狂热的崇高信仰，不惜违背自身的一切明智良知投笔从戎，如今竟被指着鼻子痛骂我是这一桩白党大义的“奇耻大辱”！下一刻，我亦如负创的猛兽般霍然弹地而起，四目相对，我们二人在这死寂的大厅之中相对矗立了片刻，浑身剧烈颤抖，相视无言。终于，我喉咙里爆发出了一声困兽般的狂吼。
“这话，”我怒极反笑狂吼道，“竟是出自昨日那个声称情愿抛洒心头热血只为给祖国换来一条铁臂的贞烈烈女之口么？为了你，我不惜背弃了我平生信守的一切，同那帮杀人越货的绿林强盗与嗜血凶徒同流合污，到头来却换得大梦初醒——原来所谓崇高神圣的河山大义不过是你用来消遣的幌子，你个人的私欲渴求才是凌驾一切的天条！我真恨不得方才这些诛心之论是一个堂堂带把的须眉男儿对我当面啐出，多洛蕾丝，那样我便能在将这柄你所鄙夷的佩剑折断踩碎如烂铁之前，先拿它痛饮狂徒的热血作个了断！我情愿呼求苍天裂开一道无底深渊，将整片受诅咒的土地彻底吞噬焚毁，哪怕我自身因卷入这等海盗般的肮脏内讧而一同堕入十八层阿鼻地狱，我也绝不后悔！”

===Original===

She stood perfectly still, gazing at me with widely dilated eyes, a new expression coming into her face; then when I paused for her to speak, expecting only a fresh outburst of scorn and bitterness, a strange, sorrowful smile flitted over her lips, and, coming close to me, she placed her hand on my shoulder.
“Oh,” she said, “what a strength of passion you are capable of! Forgive me, Richard, for I have forgiven you. Ah, we were made for each other, and it can never, never be.”
She dropped her head dejectedly on my shoulder. My anger vanished at those sad words; love only remained﻿—love mingled with profoundest compassion and remorse for the pain I had inflicted. Supporting her with my arm, I tenderly stroked her dark hair, and, stooping, pressed my lips against it.
“Do you love me so much, Dolores,” I said, “enough even to forgive the cruel, bitter words I have just spoken? Oh, I was mad﻿—mad to say such things to you, and shall repent it all my life long! How cruelly have I wounded you with my love and my anger! Tell me, dearest Dolores, can you forgive me?”
“Yes, Richard; everything. Is there any word you can speak, any deed you can do, and I not forgive it? Does your wife love you like that﻿—can you love her as you love me? How cruel destiny is to us! Ah, my beloved country, I was ready to shed my blood for you﻿—just to win one strong arm to fight for you, but I did not dream that this would be the sacrifice required of me. Look, it will soon be time for you to go﻿—we cannot sleep now, Richard. Sit down here with me, and let us spend this last hour together with my hand in yours, for we shall never, never, never meet again.”
And so, sitting there hand in hand, we waited for the dawn, speaking many sad and tender words to one another; and at last, when we parted, I held her once more unresisting to my breast, thinking, as she did, that our separation would be an eternal one.

===Chinese===

她宛如被雷电劈中一般僵立在原地，一双明眸惊骇欲绝地剧烈张大，绝美的容颜上蓦然浮现出一抹前所未见的奇异神色；当我怒喘着骤然收住话头、严阵以待准备迎接她新一轮更刻毒刺骨的狂轰滥炸之际，一抹凄楚悲凉的惨淡笑靥却悄无声息地自她的唇角掠过，下一刻，她竟轻步走近我身旁，将一只温润无力的柔荑轻轻按在了我的肩头。
“天哪，”她喃喃低语道，“你这副胸膛里究竟蕴藏着何等排山倒海的炽烈深情啊！饶恕我吧，理查德，皆因我早已在心底彻底原谅了你的一切。啊，我们分明是苍天注定天造地设的一对璧人，偏偏此生却注定有缘无分、永无可能相守了。”
说着，她将臻首哀伤绝望地倚靠在我的肩头。听闻这番凄楚断肠的字句，我胸中那滔天的雷霆怒火转瞬间烟消云散；残留在灵魂深处的唯有深情——交织着无边痛惜与懊悔自责的万千柔情。我伸出单臂将她紧紧揽入怀中，温存地抚摩着她那如瀑的秀发，俯下身去将滚烫的双唇轻轻贴在她冰凉的发丝之上。
“你当真爱我爱得这般深沉吗，多洛蕾丝，”我哽咽道，“深沉到甚至能宽恕我方才吐露的那些刻薄残忍的诛心恶言？噢，我真是疯了——竟被猪油蒙了心对你说出那等丧心病狂的混账话，我余生必将为此悔恨终生！我竟用自己荒唐的爱欲与狂怒，将你伤得这般鲜血淋漓！告诉我，我最心爱的多洛蕾丝，你当真能原谅我吗？”
“能的，理查德；无论你说过什么、做过什么，天下间又有哪一桩罪过是我无法宽恕你的呢？你家中的妻子，难道也能如这般毫无保留地去爱你么——而你对待她，难道当真能如对待我这般情深似海吗？苍天待我们何其残忍歹毒啊！啊，我深爱的苦难祖国，我本情愿为你流尽最后一滴碧血——只为替你换来一条在沙场上拔剑效命的英雄铁臂，可我做梦也未曾料到，命运之神向我索取的，竟会是这般撕心裂肺、痛彻骨髓的泼天祭品。瞧啊，你动身开拔的时辰已然迫在眉睫了——在这个节骨眼上，我们谁也别想再合眼了，理查德。且同我在这儿并肩坐下吧，趁着这最后的时光，且让我将一双纤手交托在你的掌心，皆因在今夕一别之后，此生此世你我二人注定生离死别，再无相见之期了。”
于是乎，我们十指紧扣并肩依偎在晨曦将至的窗扉旁，在死寂中默默静候着黎明的破晓降临，低声倾诉着万千凄楚温存的诀别衷肠；而最终到了挥泪作别的关头，我再一次将她毫无反抗的温香软玉死死扣在胸膛之上，正如她心底所万念俱灰认定的那样，深知此番作别，便当真已是关山难越的九泉永诀了。

## XVIII Rest on Thy Rock, Andromeda! / 第十八章 安歇在你的磐石上吧，安德洛墨达！
===Original===

About the stirring events of the succeeding days I have little to relate, and no reader who has suffered the malady of love in its acutest form will wonder at it. During those days I mixed with a crowd of adventurers, returned exiles, criminals, and malcontents, every one of them worth studying; the daylight hours were passed in cavalry exercises or in long expeditions about the country, while every evening beside the camp fire romantic tales enough to fill a volume were told in my hearing. But the image of Dolores was ever before my mind, so that all this crowded period, lasting nine or ten days, passed before me like a phantasmagoria, or an uneasy dream, leaving only a very confused impression on my brain.
