It had been very pleasant lying there “under the greenwood tree,” while those veracious stories of hags, *lampalaguas*, and apparitions had proved highly entertaining; but a long spell, a whole month perhaps, of that kind of life was not to be thought of; and if I did not get to Rocha now, before the rural police were set to catch runaway rebels, it would perhaps be impossible to do so later on. I determined, therefore, to go my own way, and, after drinking bitter maté, I caught and saddled the dun horse. I really had not deserved the severe censure Lechuza had passed on me the previous evening in reference to horse-stealing, for I had taken the dun with very little more compunction than one is accustomed to feel in England when “borrowing” an umbrella on a rainy day. To all people in all parts of the world, a time comes when to appropriate their neighbour’s goods is held not only justifiable, but even meritorious; to Israelites in Egypt, Englishmen under a cloud in their own moist island, and to Orientals running away after a fight. By keeping the dun over thirty hours in my possession I had acquired a kind of prescriptive right to it, and now began to look on it as my very own; subsequent experience of his endurance and other good qualities enables me to endorse the Oriental saying that a “stolen horse carries you well.”
Bidding farewell to my companions in defeat, who had certainly not been frightened out of their imaginations, I rode forth just when it was beginning to grow light. Roads and houses I studiously avoided, travelling on at an easy gallop, which took me about ten miles an hour, till noon; then I rested at a small rancho, where I fed and watered my horse and recruited my own energies with roast beef and bitter maté. On again till dark; by that time I had covered about forty miles, and began to feel both hungry and tired.

===Chinese===

破晓方至，我便爬起身来走到火堆旁同马里亚诺会合，他早已生起了火，正忙着烧水冲泡清晨的第一剂马黛茶（maté）。我打心眼里厌恶像被猎犬穷追不舍的野兽那般、毫无指望地无限期潜伏在深林灌木丛里的念头；加之圣科洛马早先曾千叮咛万嘱咐，一旦战败，便让我径直奔赴南部海岸的罗恰丘陵（Lomas de Rocha），眼下看来这无疑是上上之策。尽管“在绿林树荫之下”高卧荒野确实惬意无比，而那些关于恶婆娘、兰帕拉瓜巨蚺（*lampalagua*）和孤魂还魂的逼真奇谈亦教人乐不可支；但若要长年累月——哪怕整整一个月——过这等东躲西藏的亡命生涯，那是无论如何也教人消受不起的；况且若是眼下趁着乡勇警宪尚未大规模出动缉拿叛乱逃犯之际逃往罗恰，日后恐怕便再难有这般生路了。思虑至此，我决意独自行路，在咽下一盅苦涩的马黛茶之后，我套上并备好了那匹黄膘马的马鞍。就盗马一事而言，前一天傍晚猫头鹰对我施加的严厉斥责委实有失公允，皆因我牵走那匹黄马时的内疚自责，并不比一个英国绅士在阴雨绵绵的日子里随手“顺走”一把雨伞时的愧疚多上多少。凡是生息在天南海北各个角落的苍生黎庶，终归会迎来那么一个将侵吞占有邻人财物不仅视为天经地义、甚至引为功勋壮举的特定关头：正如在埃及寄居的以色列人，在自己阴湿多雨的本岛上倒了大霉的英格兰臣民，以及在沙场折戟后仓皇跑路的东岸汉子。鉴于这匹黄膘马已被我据为己有超过了三十多个小时（30 多个小时），我仿佛已然在法理上对其享有了某种时效占有权，如今索性心安理得地将其视作自己的私产；而日后它在长途跋涉中所展现出的惊人耐力与诸般神骏品质，更是让我由衷印证了东岸那句古老谚语——“偷来的骏马最脚下生风”。
同那几位并未在沙场败局中被吓破丰富想象力的难兄难弟们依依作别之后，正当东方既白之际，我翻身上马启程远行。一路上我煞费苦心地刻意回避了官道与村舍，任由坐骑以舒缓自如的颠步疾行，每小时约莫能跑出十英里（每小时约 10 英里）的程程，直至正午时分；随后我在一座僻静的小农舍（rancho）歇脚，给战马喂了草料饱饮了清泉，自己亦就着烤牛肉与苦马黛茶重振了周身元气。饭后扬鞭继续赶路直至天色向晚；到彼时为止我已纵马奔驰了约四十英里（约 40 英里），腹中雷鸣饥肠辘辘，浑身上下亦开始泛起阵阵倦意。

===Original===

I had passed several ranchos and estancia houses, but was shy of seeking entertainment at any of them, and so went farther, only to fare worse. When the brief twilight was darkening to night I came upon a broad cart-track, leading, I suppose, to Montevideo from the eastern part of the country, and, seeing a long, low rancho near it, which I recognized as a pulperia, or store, by the flagstaff planted before it, I resolved to purchase some refreshment for myself, then to ride on a mile or two and spend the night under the stars﻿—a safe roof if an airy one. Tying my horse to the gate, I went into the porch-like projection at the end of the rancho, which I found divided from the interior by the counter, with its usual grating of thick iron bars to protect the treasures of gin, rum, and comestibles from drunken or quarrelsome customers. As soon as I came into the porch I began to regret having alighted at the place, for there, standing at the counter, smoking and drinking, were about a dozen very rough-looking men. Unfortunately for me, they had tied their horses under the shadow of a clump of trees some distance from the gate, so that I had missed seeing them on my arrival. Once amongst them, however, my only plan was to disguise my uneasiness, be very polite, get my refreshments, then make my escape as speedily as possible. They stared rather hard at me, but returned my salutation courteously; then going to a disengaged corner of the counter, I rested my left elbow on it and called for bread, a box of sardines, and a tumbler of wine.
“If you will join me, señores, the table is spread,” said I; but they all declined my invitation with thanks, and I began to eat my bread and sardines.
They appeared to be all persons living in the immediate neighbourhood, for they addressed each other familiarly and were conversing about love matters. One of them, however, soon dropped out of the conversation, and, edging away from the others, stood a little space apart, leaning against the wall on the side of the porch farthest from me. I began to notice this man very particularly, for it was plain to see that I had excited his interest in an extraordinary manner, and I did not like his scrutiny.

===Chinese===

沿途我曾途经数座农舍和大庄园（estancia）府邸，但我生性多疑，唯恐自投罗网，不敢冒失前往任何一处打尖求宿，只得硬着头皮继续向前摸索，孰料竟是一步步滑向了更加险恶的境地。当那短暂的黄昏薄暮渐次被漆黑夜幕吞噬之际，我撞上了一条宽阔的车马大道，依我揣度，这大概是从东部地区通往蒙得维的亚（Montevideo）的交通要道；见近旁耸立着一座狭长低矮的农舍，且门前竖立着一根高耸的旗杆，我一眼便认出这是一间杂货铺兼小酒馆（pulperia），于是决计在此采购些果腹口粮，随后策马再往前跑上一两英里，在璀璨繁星之下露天露宿——纵然通风透气了些，好歹算是一顶安全可靠的天幕。我把缰绳系在篱笆门桩上，迈步跨入了农舍尽头一处宛如门廊般的凸出前厅，发觉前厅与内室之间被一道厚重的柜台隔开，柜台上方照例竖着一排粗壮的铁栅栏，以防酗酒滋事或寻衅斗殴的泼皮无赖劫掠里头珍藏的金酒、朗姆酒与各类罐头口粮。然而甫一踏入这处门廊，我心头便立刻泛起了一阵悔意，暗怪自己不该冒失在此下马停留，皆因就在柜台前，正倚着十来个（约 12 个）面相极其凶悍粗鄙的粗鲁汉子，一边吞云吐雾一边开怀畅饮。倒霉的是，他们把坐骑拴在了距离篱笆门有些距离的一丛树荫底下，以至于我进门前竟未曾察觉半点风吹草动。然而既然已然误入狼窝，眼下唯一的上策便是极力掩饰内心的不安与慌乱，举止务必温文尔雅，买齐干粮之后便以迅雷不及掩耳之势溜之大吉。众人拿毒辣的目光狠狠上下打量了我一番，倒也还算客客气气地回应了我的颔首致意；随后我自顾自踱步到柜台一处无人的闲置角落，左肘斜支在台面上，张口要了干面包、一盒沙丁鱼罐头与满满一杯红酒。
“列位爷台若是不嫌弃，酒菜已然齐备，且请同赏光吧，”我客套地让道；众人皆道谢婉拒了我的美意，于是我便自顾自啃起面包与沙丁鱼来。
瞧这伙人的神态言谈，大抵全是近乡近邻的土著熟客，彼此间推心置腹嬉笑怒骂，正唾沫横飞地高谈阔论着男女风月私情。然而未消多时，其中一人便悄然退出了谈话圈子，不动声色地同众人拉开了距离，独自抱臂斜倚在距离我最远的外侧门廊土墙边。我开始格外警惕地留意起这个汉子，皆因任谁都能一眼看穿，我显然以一种异乎寻常的诡异方式激起了他强烈的兴趣，而他那道肆无忌惮的毒辣窥视，更是教我脊背发凉、浑身不自在。

===Original===

He was, without exception, the most murderous-looking villain I have ever had the misfortune to meet: that was the deliberate opinion I came to before I formed a closer acquaintance with him. He was a broad-chested, powerful-looking man of medium height; his hands he kept concealed under the large cloth poncho he wore, and he had on a slouch hat that just allowed his eyes to be seen under the rim. They were truculent, yellowish-green eyes, that seemed to grow fiery and dim and fiery again by turns, yet never for a single instant were they averted from my face. His black hair hung to his shoulders, and he also had a bristly moustache, which did not conceal his brutal mouth, nor was there any beard to hide his broad, swarthy jowl. His jaws were the only part of him that had any motion, while he stood there, still as a bronze statue, watching me. At intervals he ground his teeth, after which he would slap his lips together two or three times, while a slimy froth, most sickening to see, gathered at the corners of his mouth.
“Gandara, you are not drinking,” said one of the gauchos, turning to him. He shook his head slightly without speaking or taking his eyes off my face; whereupon the man who had spoken smiled and resumed his conversation with the others.
The long, intense, soul-trying scrutiny this brutal wretch had subjected me to came to a very sudden end. Quick as lightning a long, broad knife flashed out from its concealment under his poncho, and with one catlike bound he was before me, the point of his horrid weapon touching my poncho just over the pit of my stomach.
“Do not move, rebel,” he said in a husky voice. “If you move one hair’s breadth, that moment you die.”
The other men all ceased talking and looked on with some interest, but did not offer to interfere or make any remark.
For one moment I felt as if an electric shock had gone through me, and then instantly I was calm﻿—never, in fact, have I felt more calm and collected than at that terrible moment. ’Tis a blessed instinct of self-preservation which nature has provided us with; feeble, timid men possess it in common with the strong and brave, as weak, persecuted wild animals have it as well as those that are fierce and bloodthirsty.

===Chinese===

毫无悬念，这是我这辈子有幸撞见过的恶棍之中，模样最为凶神恶煞、杀气腾腾的刽子手雏形：这是在同他深交过招之前，我心头经过深思熟虑后得出的确凿定论。他身材中等，却生着一副极其宽阔敦实的膀臂胸膛，浑身上下透着一股骇人的悍勇蛮力；双手始终深藏在身上那件宽大的粗布毛毡大氅（poncho）底下，头顶歪戴着一顶软呢宽檐帽，帽檐压得极低，仅能勉强露出帽子底下的两只招子。那是一双穷凶极恶、泛着黄绿凶光的狠毒眼珠，眸中的凶芒时而暴涨炽烈、时而晦暗阴沉，却自始至终连半刻都未曾从我的面庞上挪开过分毫。他如墨的乱发披垂及肩，蓄着一抹硬扎扎如钢针般的胡须，却丝毫遮掩不住那张残忍嗜血的血盆大口，亦无长髯去遮盖那张宽阔黝黑的下颚骨。就在他宛如一尊青铜凶神般凝固在原地死死锁定我之际，全身上下唯一尚在蠕动的，便唯有他那副下巴颏了。每隔一阵工夫，他便咬牙切齿地磨磨后槽牙，随后上下嘴唇吧唧作响地猛碰两三下，嘴角边随即泛起一圈黏稠恶心的白沫，令人观之欲呕。
“甘达拉（Gandara），你怎的不喝两口？”身旁一位高乔牧人（gaucho）扭过头冲他嚷嚷道。他仅仅是微微摇了摇头，既未发一言，眼珠子亦未曾离开我的脸庞半寸；方才发问的汉子见状讪讪一笑，扭头继续同其他人闲扯起来。
然而，这个嗜血畜生对我施加的这番漫长、凌厉且直刺灵魂的酷刑般凝视，却以一种猝不及防的爆烈势头骤然迎来了终局。快若闪电一般，一柄宽大修长的凶残利刃倏地从大氅底下的阴影中撕空出鞘，伴随着宛如猎豹扑食般的一记猫腰疾跃，他已然鬼魅般瞬移到了我的鼻尖跟前，那柄狰狞兵刃那冰凉森寒的锋芒尖端，正端端正正顶在我的毛毡大氅之上，直抵我的心窝下腹软肋。
“休得动弹，叛贼，”他喉咙里挤出一阵沙哑粗粝的嘶吼，“你若是敢动一根毫毛，当场便要你的狗命。”
周遭的闲汉们顷刻间全都闭上了嘴巴，饶有兴致地在一旁冷眼旁观，却无一人上前劝阻，亦无一人多嘴置评。
在那么一个刹那，我只觉得仿佛有一道狂暴的高压电光狠狠穿透了我的四肢百骸，但紧接着，我整个人奇迹般地重归于绝对的冰冷沉着——事实上，哪怕回溯平生，我也从未在任何凶险万分的灭顶绝境中，体味过比这一刻更为镇定自若的死寂宁静。这真是大自然赋予万灵的自卫保命天赐本能；怯懦柔弱之辈与骁勇善战的豪杰同样享有这份恩赐，正如任人宰割的孱弱野兽同那些嗜血残暴的丛林猛兽一样，骨子里皆深藏着这份生存警觉。

===Original===

It is the calm which comes without call when death suddenly and unexpectedly rises up to stare us in the face; it tells us that there is one faint chance which a premature attempt to escape or even a slight agitation will destroy.
“I have no wish to move, friend,” I said, “but I am curious to know why you attack me?”
“Because you are a rebel. I have seen you before, you are one of Santa Coloma’s officers. Here you shall stand with this knife touching you till you are arrested, or else with this knife in you here you shall die.”
“You are making a mistake,” I said.
“Neighbours,” said he, speaking to the others, but without taking his eyes from my face, “will you tie this man hand and foot while I stand before him to prevent him from drawing any weapon he may have concealed under his poncho?”
“We have not come here to arrest travellers,” returned one of the men. “If he is a rebel it is no concern of ours. Perhaps you are mistaken, Gandara.”
“No, no, I am not mistaken,” he returned. “He shall not escape. I saw him at San Paulo with these eyes﻿—when did they ever deceive me? If you refuse to assist me, then go one of you to the Alcalde’s house and tell him to come without delay, while I keep guard here.”
After a little discussion one of the men offered to go and inform the Alcalde. When he had left, I said, “My friend, may I finish my meal? I am hungry, and had just begun to eat when you drew your knife against me.”
“Yes; eat,” he said; “only keep your hands well up so that I can see them. Perhaps you have a weapon at your waist.”
“I have not,” I said, “for I am an inoffensive person and do not require weapons.”
“Tongues were made to lie,” he returned, truly enough. “If I see you drop your hand lower than the counter I shall rip you up. We shall then be able to see whether you digest your food or not.”
I began to eat and sip my wine, still with those brutal eyes on my face and the keen knife-point touching my poncho. There was now a ghastly look of horrible excitement on his face, while his teeth-grinding performances became more frequent and the slimy froth dropped continually from the corners of his mouth on to his bosom. I dared not look at the knife, because a terrible impulse to wrest it out of his hands kept rising in me.

===Chinese===

这是一种当死神突如其来且毫无预兆地降临、同你面面相觑生死相贴之际，无需呼唤便自发涌上心头的绝对沉静；它在冥冥中警示着我们，眼前尚存一线生机，而任何冒失盲目的盲动逃窜乃至一丝微小的慌乱颤抖，都将当场把这最后一丝生机碾得粉碎。
“朋友，我丝毫没有乱动的打算，”我神色自若地开口道，“但我着实好奇，你究竟因何由头要对我拔刀相向？”
“皆因你是个谋反作乱的逆贼。老子早先见过你，你是圣科洛马（Santa Coloma）手底下的带兵军官。你且给我老老实实在这儿立着，任由这把刀顶着你的肉，直到官差将你缉拿归案；否则这柄刀锋一旦捅穿你的皮囊，你今晚便得横尸当场。”
“你认错人了，”我心平气和地分辩道。
“四邻八舍的乡亲们，”他一边用眼角的余光冲周遭的汉子们吆喝，眼珠子却自始至终死死焊在我的眉宇之间，“谁上前搭把手把这厮五花大绑起来，且由老子在跟前持刀堵死他，免得他从大氅底下掏出私藏的暗器火器暗算老子？”
“我们是来喝酒消遣的，可不是来替官府拿贼充当捕快的，”其中一个汉子没好气地顶撞道，“这人纵然真是反贼，跟咱们又有一文钱干系？指不定是你自己老眼昏花认错人了吧，甘达拉。”
“放屁，老子断无可能认错！”他暴躁地咆哮道，“休想让他从老子眼皮底下溜走。在圣保罗战场上老子可是拿这两只招子真真切切瞧见他的——老子的招子何曾看走眼过？既然你们这群孬种不肯伸手相助，那就给老子派个人腿脚麻利些去镇长（Alcalde）府上报信，叫他速速率人赶来拿办，老子且在此处替官家把守死关。”
在一番七嘴八舌的嘀咕之后，其中一位汉子自告奋勇动身前去通报镇长。待那人迈出门槛之后，我慢条斯理地开口道：“我的朋友，容我吃完这口晚膳可好？我赶了一整天路饥肠辘辘，方才刚吃了两口，你便拔刀相向了。”
“吃吧，只管吃你的；”他咬牙切齿道，“不过把你的爪子高高举起，给老子亮在眼皮子底下。鬼晓得你腰里头是不是藏着利刃硬家伙。”
“我赤手空拳，”我气定神闲道，“我本是个安分守己的本分良民，平日里从不用兵刃防身。”
“天底下的舌头生来就是为了满嘴跑火车撒谎的，”他冷笑连连，这话倒是不假，“只要老子瞧见你的爪子敢往柜台下方垂落半寸，老子立刻把你开膛破肚。届时咱们大伙儿倒要好好瞧瞧，你肚子里究竟能不能消化得了这顿断头饭。”
于是我便自顾自细嚼慢咽起面包、轻啜起杯中的葡萄酒，而那双嗜血凶残的兽瞳依然死死钉在我的脸上，锋利冰凉的刀尖则寸步不离地抵住我的胸腹大氅。此刻他脸上浮现出一种近乎癫狂的极度亢奋与嗜血红光，上下磨牙的咯咯声响越发密集频繁，嘴角分泌出的黏稠唾沫白沫更是不绝如缕地滴淌落在他自己的胸襟之上。我甚至不敢垂眸直视那柄尖刀，皆因我心头一股想要赤手空拳悍然夺刃的疯狂冲动，正不可遏制地在胸腔中疯狂翻滚咆哮。

===Original===

It was almost too strong to be overcome, yet I knew that even the slightest attempt to escape would be fatal to me; for the fellow was evidently thirsty for my blood and only wanted an excuse to run me through. But what, I thought, if he were to grow tired of waiting, and, carried away by his murderous instincts, to plunge his weapon into me? In that case I should die like a dog, without having availed myself of my one chance of escape through over-caution. These thoughts were maddening, still through it all I laboured to observe an outwardly calm demeanour.
My supper was done. I began to feel strangely weak and nervous. My lips grew dry; I was intensely thirsty and longed for more wine, yet dared not take it for fear that in my excited state even a very moderate amount of alcohol might cloud my brain.
“How long will it take your friend to return with the Alcalde?” I asked at length.
Gandara made no reply. “A long time,” said one of the other men. “I, for one, cannot wait till he comes,” and after that he took his departure. One by one they now began to drop away, till only two men besides Gandara remained in the porch. Still that murderous wretch kept before me like a tiger watching its prey, or rather like a wild boar, gnashing and foaming, and ready to rip up its adversary with horrid tusk.
At length I made an appeal to him, for I began to despair of the Alcalde coming to deliver me. “Friend,” I said, “if you will allow me to speak, I can convince you that you are mistaken. I am a foreigner, and know nothing about Santa Coloma.”
“No, no,” he interrupted, pressing the knife-point warningly against my stomach, then suddenly withdrawing it as if about to plunge it into me. “I know you are a rebel. If I thought the Alcalde were not coming I would run you through at once and cut your throat afterwards. It is a virtue to kill a Blanco traitor, and if you do not go bound hand and foot from here then here you must die. What, do you dare to say that I did not see you at San Paulo﻿—that you are not an officer of Santa Coloma? Look, rebel, I will swear on this cross that I saw you there.”
Suiting the action to the word, he raised the hilt of the weapon to his lips to kiss the guard, which with the handle formed a cross. That pious action was the first slip he had made, and gave the first opportunity that had come to me during all that terrible interview.

===Chinese===

那股夺刀反杀的冲动强烈得几乎要冲破理智的堤坝，但我深知，哪怕露出半点图谋反抗逃遁的蛛丝马迹，都将当场招致灭顶之灾；皆因眼前这厮分明已然渴血发狂，不过是在苦苦寻觅一个将我捅个透心凉的借口罢了。然而转念一想，设若他苦等不耐烦了，兽性大发之下直接将利刃不由分说捅入我的心窝，那又当如何？若真到了那步田地，我岂不是要因过分谨小慎微而错失唯一逃生的天机，像条野狗般窝囊惨死？这些念头教人焦躁得近乎发疯，然而自始至终，我都竭尽全力在皮囊表面维持着泰然自若的沉静表象。
晚餐终于见底了。我开始隐约体味到一阵莫名的虚脱脱力与神经紧绷。双唇干裂焦枯；我渴得嗓子冒烟，极度渴望再痛饮几大杯烈酒，却又断不敢任性妄为，生怕在眼下这般极度紧绷亢奋的神经状态下，哪怕微量的酒精也会麻痹遮蔽我的大脑神智。
“你那位前去搬兵的同伴，要过多久才能领着镇长折返？”沉寂良久之后我终于试探着打破了僵局。
甘达拉置若罔闻默不作声。“早着呢，”身旁另一个看客懒洋洋地接茬道，“反正我是没那份闲心耗在这儿等候了，”说完便拍拍屁股扬长而去。客人们就这样陆陆续续作鸟兽散，直至整间门廊里除开甘达拉之外，便仅剩下两名看热闹的闲汉了。然而眼前这个凶残至极的恶徒依然宛如一头死盯猎物的饿虎般在跟前步步紧逼，抑或更像是一头龇牙咧嘴、嘴角淌着白沫、随时准备用狰狞獠牙将仇敌开膛破肚的狂怒野猪。
最终我不得不再度向他求情，皆因我心头已然对镇长究竟能否赶来替我解围彻底绝望了。“朋友，”我推心置腹道，“只要你肯容我吐露几句肺腑之言，我自能向你证明你当真认错人了。我是个漂洋过海的外邦侨民，对圣科洛马那一套反叛勾当向来一无所知。”
“少废话！”他厉声喝断了我，警告性地将刀尖重重往我的腹肉深处顶了顶，随后又猛然缩回半寸仿佛正欲蓄力狠狠刺下，“老子心知肚明你是个叛贼。老子方才若是料定镇长赶不来，早就一刀把你扎个对穿、回头再割断你的喉管了。亲手宰杀一个白党（Blanco）逆贼乃是天经地义的大功德，今夜你若是不被五花大绑捆着抬出这间门槛，那便唯有横尸当场一条死路。怎么，你难道还敢借着豹子胆抵赖说老子在圣保罗战场上没见过你——抵赖说你不是圣科洛马麾下的得力武官不成？看好了，逆贼，老子敢指着这柄十字架赌咒发誓，老子当日确确实实在沙场上亲眼撞见过你！”
话音未落，他言出法随，顺势将手中利刃的刀柄高高举向自己的嘴唇，去亲吻那护手——那由坚硬护手与刀柄交织构成的十字圣架。这番虚伪虔诚的狂妄举动，乃是他从始至终露出的第一处致命破绽，亦是在这场惊心动魄的酷刑对峙中，命运女神首次垂青赐予我的绝杀良机。

===Original===

Before he had ceased speaking, the conviction that my time had come flashed like lightning through my brain. Just as his slimy lips kissed the hilt, my right hand dropped to my side and grasped the handle of my revolver under my poncho. He saw the movement, and very quickly recovered the handle of his knife. In another second of time he would have driven the blade through me; but that second was all I now required. Straight from my waist, and from under my poncho, I fired. His knife fell ringing on to the floor; he swerved, then fell back, coming to the ground with a heavy thud. Over his falling body I leaped, and almost before he had touched the ground was several yards away, then, wheeling round, I found the other two men rushing out after me.
“Back!” I shouted, covering the foremost of the two with my revolver.
They instantly stood still.
“We are not following you, friend,” said one, “but only wish to get out of the place.”
“Back, or I fire!” I repeated, and then they retreated into the porch. They had stood by unconcerned while their cutthroat comrade Gandara was threatening my life, so that I naturally felt angry with them.
I sprang upon my horse, but, instead of riding away at once, stood for some minutes by the gate watching the two men. They were kneeling by Gandara, one opening his clothes to look for the wound, the other holding a flaring candle over his ashen, corpse-like face.
“Is he dead?” I asked.
One of the men looked up and answered, “It appears so.”
“Then,” I returned, “I make you a present of his carcass.”
After that, digging my spurs into my horse, I galloped away.
Some readers might imagine, after what I had related, that my sojourn in the Purple Land had quite brutalised me; I am happy to inform them that it was not so. Whatever a man’s individual character may happen to be, he has always a strong inclination in him to reply to an attack in the spirit in which it is made. He does not call the person who playfully ridicules his foibles a whitened sepulchre or an unspeakable scoundrel, and the same principle holds good when it comes to actual physical fighting.

===Chinese===

尚未等他的狂言彻底落地，千钧一发的生机便宛如一道狂暴的雷霆闪电般在我的脑海中轰然炸响。就在他那黏腻恶心的厚唇亲吻上刀柄护手的电光石火间，我的右手已然快若奔雷般自肋下猛然下垂，在大氅底下死死扣住了腰间柯尔特左轮手枪的枪柄。他猛地察觉到了异动，极其机敏狠辣地试图重新压低刀柄刺下。只需再过一秒钟的工夫，他手中的尖刀便能将我的肉身扎个透心凉；然而对我而言，抢先到手的这一秒钟已然足够逆转乾坤了。就在腰际之间，隔着厚重的毛毡大氅，我果断扣动了扳机。砰的一声巨响！他的尖刀伴随着刺耳的金属鸣响失手砸在泥地上；他高大的身躯猛烈一晃，随后仰面向后栽倒，伴随着沉闷的巨响重重砸在了泥地之上。我一个箭步纵身凌空跃过他坠落的躯体，甚至还没等他后脑勺沾地，我便已然敏捷地弹跳到了数码开外，随后潇洒掉转马头举目一瞧，只见方才观战的另外两名汉子正惊慌失措地夺门而出向我猛冲而来。
“退后！”我厉声暴喝，手中的黑洞洞枪口已然死死锁定了冲在最前面的汉子眉心。
二人当场吓得原地急刹僵立在原地。
“朋友，我们可不是冲着你来的，”其中一人慌忙举手求饶道，“我们不过是想赶紧逃出这间是非之地罢了。”
“通通退后，否则老子立刻开枪！”我厉声再度警告，两名闲汉这才战战兢兢退缩回了门廊阴影里。方才当他们的刽子手同伙甘达拉明火执仗拿刀顶着我的肚皮要取我性命时，他们一个个冷眼旁观视若无睹，此刻我心头自然对这帮势利小人燃着满腔怒火。
我纵身飞跃上马鞍，然而我并未立刻扬鞭落荒而逃，反而在栅栏门桩前勒马驻足了数分钟，居高临下地冷眼端详着那两名汉子。只见他们正扑通跪倒在甘达拉身侧，一人手忙脚乱地撕扯扒开他的衣襟寻觅弹孔伤口，另一人则高举着一根摇曳晃动的蜡烛，将昏黄的烛光映照在那张面如死灰、宛如僵尸般的狰狞面庞之上。
“他咽气了吗？”我居高临下扬声喝问道。
其中一人诚惶诚恐地抬起头，颤声应道：“瞧这模样，大抵是死透了。”
“既然如此，”我嘴角泛起一抹冷笑回敬道，“那我便把他的死尸当成一件赠礼赏给你们了！”
抛下这句冰冷刺骨的嘲弄，我双腿猛夹马腹将马刺狠狠扎入坐骑皮肉，扬长而去策马绝尘狂奔。
读到此处，若干读者在回味完这番血雨腥风的自述之后，或许会想当然地揣度我在紫色国土的漂泊岁月已然将我彻底异化成了一头冷酷嗜血的野兽；我很欣慰地向诸位通报，实情绝非如此。不论一个凡人平日里的心性操守何其温良敦厚，当他猝不及防遭遇致命袭击时，骨子里向来会激荡起一种以牙还牙、以暴制暴的强烈本能冲动。面对一个拿你的微小瑕疵善意打趣取乐的挚友，你决计不可能破口大骂对方是粉饰的坟墓或无可救药的人渣；而在性命相搏的赤裸裸肉体搏杀之中，这一至高法则同样颠扑不破。

===Original===

If a French gentleman were to call me out, I daresay I should go to the encounter twirling my moustache, bowing down to the ground, all smiles and compliments; and that I should select my rapier with a pleasant kind of feeling, like that experienced by the satirist about to write a brilliant article while picking out a pen with a suitable nib. On the other hand, if a murderous brute with truculent eyes and gnashing teeth attempts to disembowel me with a butcher’s knife, the instinct of self-preservation comes out in all its old original ferocity, inspiring the heart with such implacable fury that after spilling his blood I could spurn his loathsome carcass with my foot. I do not wonder at myself for speaking those savage words. That he was past recall seemed certain, yet not a shade of regret did I feel at his death. Joy at the terrible retribution I had been able to inflict on the murderous wretch was the only emotion I experienced when galloping away into the darkness﻿—such joy that I could have sung and shouted aloud had it not seemed imprudent to indulge in such expression of feeling.

===Chinese===

设若是一位风度翩翩的法兰西绅士按贵族规矩向我提出单挑决斗，我敢说自己定会优雅地捻弄着小胡子、躬身行一个无可挑剔的贴地大礼，满面堆笑尽显溢美之词；且在挑选刺剑之时心头定然会洋溢着一种怡然自得的审美愉悦，恰如一位即将挥毫泼墨撰写绝妙讽刺文章的文坛巨擘在悉心挑选一支称手合意的羽毛笔尖一般。然而反之，设若是一个生着凶恶鼠目、咬牙切齿嘴角狂喷白沫的嗜血暴徒，胆敢手持屠夫剔骨尖刀图谋将我开膛破肚，那么埋藏在骨髓深处的求生自卫本能便会以其最原始、最野蛮狂暴的狰狞面目全面复苏，用一种不共戴天的滔天杀意填满我的胸膛，以至于在亲手将这畜生的狗血洒向黄土之后，我甚至恨不得用靴尖狠狠踢翻踹碎他那具令人作呕的肮脏死尸。回想起自己当时脱口而出的那番野蛮残酷的讥诮话语，我对自己没有产生半点惊诧或懊恼。那恶汉已然彻底回天乏术一命呜呼，这已是不争的铁证，然而对于他的死，我心头自始至终未曾掠过哪怕半丝半缕的悔恨与自责。在策马长驱没入苍茫夜色的疾驰中，我灵魂深处唯一激荡翻腾的，便唯有亲手将雷霆天谴降临在那个嗜血恶徒头顶的无上复仇狂喜——那是一种如此沛莫能御的极乐狂喜，以至于设若不是在逃亡途中肆意宣泄情感未免显得过于鲁莽孟浪，我真恨不得当场放声高歌、对着浩渺苍天引吭长啸。

## XXI Liberty and Dirt / 第二十一章 自由与泥垢
===Original===

After my terrible adventure I did not rest badly that night, albeit I slept on an empty stomach (the sardines counting as nothing), and under the vast, void sky, powdered with innumerable stars. And when I proceeded next day on my journey, *God’s light*, as the pious Orientals call the first wave of glory with which the rising sun floods the world, had never seemed so pleasant to my eyes, nor had earth ever looked fresher or lovelier, with the grass and bushes everywhere hung with starry lace, sparkling with countless dewy gems, which the epeiras had woven overnight. Life seemed very sweet to me on that morning, so softening my heart that when I remembered the murderous wretch who had endangered it I almost regretted that he was now probably blind and deaf to nature’s sweet ministrations.
Before noon I came to a large, thatched house, with clumps of shady trees growing near it, also surrounded with brushwood fences and sheep and cattle enclosures.
The blue smoke curling peacefully up from the chimney and the white gleam of the walls through the shady trees﻿—for this rancho actually boasted a chimney and whitewashed walls﻿—looked exceedingly inviting to my tired eyes. How pleasant a good breakfast, with a long siesta in the shade after it, would be, thought I; but, alas! was I not pursued by the awful phantoms of political vengeance? Uncertain whether to call or not, my horse jogged straight on towards the house, for a horse always knows when his rider is in doubt and never fails at such times to give his advice. It was lucky for me that on this occasion I condescended to take it. “I will, at all events, call for a drink of water and see what the people are like,” I thought, and in a few minutes I was standing at the gate, apparently an object of great interest to half a dozen children ranging from two to thirteen years old, all staring at me with wide-open eyes. They had dirty faces, the smallest one dirty legs also, for he or she wore nothing but a small shirt. The next in size had a shirt supplemented with a trousers-like garment reaching to the knees; and so on, progressively, up to the biggest boy, who wore the cast-off parental toggery, and so, instead of having too little on, was, in a sense, overdressed. I asked this youngster for a can of water to quench my thirst and a stick of fire to light my cigar.

===Chinese===

经历了那场凶险万分的惊魂恶战之后，那一夜我睡得倒也还不算太坏，尽管我是腹中空空露宿荒野（那几尾沙丁鱼几乎算不上什么果腹之物），头顶覆着浩瀚辽阔、洒满漫天繁星的无垠穹苍。当翌日我再度启程赶路之际，“天主之光”（*God’s light*）——虔诚的东岸人习惯以此来指称初升旭日将万道霞光倾泻在人间的破晓晨曦——在我眼中从未显得这般明媚动人，而大地亦从未显得如此娇艳清新；草丛与灌木丛处处悬挂着织网金蛛在一夜之间织就的璀璨星罗蛛网，晶莹剔透地缀满了数不胜数的露珠宝石。在那个清晨，生命在我看来是如此甜美温存，以至于我的心肠彻底软化了下来，每当回想起昨夜企图置我于死地的那个嗜血恶徒，我甚至有些惋惜他如今大概已然双目失明双耳失聪，再也无法领受大自然这般甜美温柔的抚慰了。
正午前夕，我行至一座宽阔的茅草大宅院跟前，屋舍周围簇拥着几蓬浓密的遮荫树林，四周圈着灌木柴草树篱以及关养牛羊的栏圈。
一缕青烟自烟囱里悠然袅袅升起，白垩粉刷的泥墙在浓密树荫间若隐若现——皆因这座农舍（rancho）竟然破天荒地耸立着真正的烟囱，且墙面粉刷得雪白——这幅景致对我这双困顿疲惫的眼眸而言，无疑具备着难以抗拒的诱人魅力。倘若能美美饱餐一顿丰盛的早膳，饭后再在浓荫树下舒舒服服睡上一场漫长的午觉，那该是何等快活惬意啊，我心下暗想；然而唉！身后不是正有政治复仇的恐怖幽灵在穷追不舍吗？正当我犹豫不决不知是否该上前叩门之际，座下的战马却自作主张径直向宅院小跑而去，皆因战马向来最通人性，每逢骑手心猿意马拿不定主意之际，它总会毫不犹豫地代为定夺。万幸的是，在这一回我屈尊听从了坐骑的高见。“无论如何，我索性进去讨口水喝，顺带瞧瞧这户人家究竟是何等底细，”我暗自思忖。不出几分钟工夫，我便勒马伫立在了篱笆门前，显然成了六个（6 个）年龄在两岁至十三岁（2 岁到 13 岁）不等的孩童万分好奇的围观焦点，小家伙们一个个瞪大了圆滚滚的眼珠子死死打量着我。他们一个个小脸蛋脏兮兮的，个头最小的那个连两条小泥腿都裸露在外，皆因他（或她）身上仅仅套着一件短小的开衫汗衫。稍大一些的那个除汗衫外还套着一条长及膝盖的类似套裤的破裤衩；以此类推层层递进，直至个头最大的那个大男孩，身上套着的竟是大人淘汰下来的旧衣裤，以至于与衣不蔽体的弟妹们截然相反，在某种程度上他反倒显得有些“衣冠楚楚”、过于拖沓厚重了。我客气地向这个大后生讨要一听清水来解渴，并求一根带火星的木柴来点燃卷烟。

===Original===

He ran into the kitchen, or living-room, and by and by came out again without either water or fire. “*Papita* wishes you to come in to drink maté,” said he.
Then I dismounted, and, with the careless air of a blameless, nonpolitical person, strode into the spacious kitchen, where an immense cauldron of fat was boiling over a big fire on the hearth; while beside it, ladle in hand, sat a perspiring, greasy-looking woman of about thirty. She was engaged in skimming the fat and throwing the scum on the fire, which made it blaze with a furious joy and loudly cry out in a crackling voice for more; and from head to feet she was literally bathed in grease﻿—certainly the most greasy individual I had ever seen. It was not easy under the circumstances to tell the colour of her skin, but she had fine large Juno eyes, and her mouth was unmistakably good-humoured, as she smiled when returning my salutation. Her husband sat on the clay floor against the wall, his bare feet stretched straight out before him, while across his lap lay an immense surcingle, twenty inches broad at least, of a pure white, untanned hide; and on it he was laboriously working a design representing an ostrich hunt, with threads of black skin. He was a short, broad-shouldered man with reddish-grey hair, stiff, bristly whiskers and moustache of the same hue, sharp blue eyes, and a nose decidedly upturned.
He wore a red cotton handkerchief tied on his head, a blue check shirt, and a shawl wound round his body in place of the chiripa usually worn by native peasants. He jerked out his “*Buen dia*” to me in a short, quick, barking voice, and invited me to sit down.
“Cold water is bad for the constitution at this hour,” he said. “We will drink maté.”
There was such a rough, burr-like sound in his speech that I at once concluded he was a foreigner, or hailed from some Oriental district corresponding to our Durham or Northumberland.
“Thank you,” I said, “a maté is always welcome. I am an Oriental in that respect if in nothing else.” For I wished everyone I met to know that I was not a native.
“Right, my friend,” he exclaimed. “Maté is the best thing in this country. As for the people, they are not worth cursing.”
“How can you say such a thing,” I returned. “You are a foreigner, I suppose, but your wife is surely an Oriental.”

===Chinese===

他飞奔进厨房兼起居室，不一会儿空着双手晃晃悠悠折返了出来，手里既没有舀来清水，亦未曾取来火星。“爹爹（*Papita*）请您进屋喝马黛茶（maté），”他脆生生答道。
于是我翻身下马，摆出一副安分守己、不问政事的清白顺民那般漫不经心的神态，大摇大摆踱步跨入了那间宽敞开阔的大厨房。只见灶膛里熊熊烈火正烧得正旺，灶上一口巨大无朋的生铁大锅里正沸腾翻滚着滚烫的牛油；一位年约三十岁（约 30 岁）、大汗淋漓且浑身油光锃亮的健硕妇人正手执一把长柄大油勺端坐在铁锅旁。她正忙于撇去滚油上的油脂浮沫，并将撇出的油渣随手泼洒在烈火之中，引得火苗狂喜地爆发出烈焰怒吼，伴随着噼啪炸响的清脆爆裂声贪婪地索求着更多柴油；从头到脚她整个人简直如同在油缸里彻底浸泡过一般——毫无疑问，这是我生平有幸目睹过的最为油头粉面的脂膏尤物。在眼下这般油光满面的境况下，委实难以辨清她面皮原本的天然肤色，但她却生着一双神似天后朱诺的顾盼生辉的硕大明眸，那张嘴唇亦透着掩饰不住的敦厚温和，当她含笑回应我的躬身致意时，满面堆笑尽显和善。她的丈夫则背靠土墙直挺挺坐在泥地上，赤脚径直向前伸展，膝头平铺着一根至少二十英寸宽（至少 20 英寸宽）的巨大生牛皮马肚带，通体雪白未经鞣制；他正用一根根黑色细皮条，埋头吃力地在上面编织刺绣出一幅高乔人围猎美洲鸵的精美图腾。他是个身材矮小、膀大腰圆的敦实汉子，蓄着一头红灰斑驳的短发，连鬓胡须与大八字胡亦呈现出如出一辙的刚硬钢针质感，生着一双敏锐锐利的湛蓝眼眸，鼻梁则明显大大咧咧地向上翘起。
他头上缠着一条红棉布方巾，身穿一件蓝白方格衬衫，腰间胡乱裹着一条羊毛大披肩，用以替代本地土著高乔人惯常穿着的马裤披裙（chiripa）。他以一种短促、洪亮宛如犬吠般的嗓音向我吐出了一句“日安”（*Buen dia*），并挥手招揽我坐下叙话。
“这个时辰喝生冷凉水最伤脾胃骨髓，”他大声说道，“咱们来喝马黛茶。”
他的吐字发音里夹杂着一股极其浓重粗粝的卷舌苏格兰喉音，以至于我当场断定他定然是个外邦侨民，抑或是出自东岸某个口音相当于我们英格兰达勒姆或诺森伯兰郡的蛮荒边鄙之地。
“多谢老哥，”我答道，“一盅马黛茶向来最受人待见。在这桩嗜好上，即便别处不像，我也算是个地道的东岸人了。”皆因我极欲让沿途遇上的每一个人皆心知肚明我绝非本地土著。
“说得在理，我的朋友！”他朗声赞道，“马黛茶乃是这片天下间绝无仅有的上好至宝。至于这片土地上的牛鬼蛇神，连赏他们一顿臭骂都算抬举他们了。”
“老哥何出此言呢，”我接茬分辩道，“依我瞧，老哥您自身是个漂洋过海的外邦侨胞，可尊夫人想必断断是个地道的东岸名媛吧。”

===Original===

The Juno of the grease-pot smiled and threw a ladleful of tallow on the fire to make it roar; possibly this was meant for applause.
He waved his hand deprecatingly, the bradawl used for his work in it.
“True, friend, she is,” he replied. “Women, like horned cattle, are much the same all the world over. They have their value wherever you find them﻿—America, Europe, Asia. We know it. I spoke of men.”
“You scarcely do women justice﻿—

> *La mujer es un angel del cielo*,”

===Chinese===

这位油锅旁的朱诺（Juno）莞尔一笑，顺手舀起一满勺滚烫的牛油狠狠泼在篝火之上，引得烈焰轰然咆哮狂舞；想必这便是她表达激赏喝彩的独特肢体语言吧。
男主人手里握着钻皮子用的锥子，颇有些自嘲地摆了摆手。
“不瞒你说，朋友，她确实是本地人，”他应承道，“天底下的娘们儿，正如长角的牛犊一般，走遍五湖四海大体全是一个德行。不论你在天涯海角哪一处撞见她们——美洲也好，欧洲也罢，哪怕到了亚洲，横竖都有她们的安身身价。这桩道理咱们心知肚明。我方才骂的乃是这帮大老爷们儿。”
“老哥对女中豪杰未免有些有失公允了——

> 女人是来自天国的安琪儿（*La mujer es un angel del cielo*），”

===Original===

I returned, quoting the old Spanish song.
He barked out a short little laugh.
“That does very well to sing to a guitar,” he said.
“Talking of guitars,” spoke the woman, addressing me for the first time; “while we are waiting for the maté, perhaps you will sing us a ballad. The guitar is lying just behind you.”
“Señora, I do not play on it,” I answered. “An Englishman goes forth into the world without that desire, common to people of other nations, of making himself agreeable to those he may encounter on his way; this is why he does not learn to perform on musical instruments.”
The little man stared at me; then, deliberately disencumbering himself of surcingle, threads, and implements, he got up, advanced to me, and held out his hand.
His grave manner almost made me laugh. Taking his hand in mine, I said:
“What am I to do with this, my friend?”
“Shake it,” he replied. “We are countrymen.”
We then shook hands very vigorously for some time in silence, while his wife looked on with a smile and stirred the fat.
“Woman,” he said, turning to her, “leave your grease till tomorrow. Breakfast must be thought of. Is there any mutton in the house?”
“Half a sheep﻿—only,” she replied.
“That will do for one meal,” said he. “Here, Teofilo, run and tell Anselmo to catch two pullets﻿—fat ones, mind. To be plucked at once. You may look for half a dozen fresh eggs for your mother to put in the stew. And, Felipe, go find Cosme and tell him to saddle the roan pony to go to the store at once. Now, wife, what is wanted﻿—rice, sugar, vinegar, oil, raisins, pepper, saffron, salt, cloves, cumin seed, wine, brandy﻿—”
“Stop one moment,” I cried. “If you think it necessary to get provisions enough for an army to give me breakfast, I must tell you that I draw the line at brandy. I never touch it﻿—in this country.”
He shook hands with me again.
“You are right,” he said. “Always stick to the native drink, wherever you are, even if it is black draught. Whisky in Scotland, in the Banda Orientál rum﻿—that’s my rule.”
The place was now in a great commotion, the children saddling ponies, shouting in pursuit of fugitive chickens, and my energetic host ordering his wife about.
After the boy was despatched for the things and my horse taken care of, we sat for half an hour in the kitchen sipping maté and conversing very agreeably.

===Chinese===

我引经据典，随口念出了那句古老的西班牙老歌谣。
他从鼻腔里喷出了一声短促干瘪的嗤笑。
“抱着吉他弹唱两句，这套酸词倒还算应景，”他哂笑道。
“说到弹吉他，”一旁的女主人头一遭主动搭腔冲我开口道，“趁着水还没烧开冲茶的当口，尊客不妨赏光给咱们唱上一曲民谣小调如何？吉他就搁在您身后的长凳上呢。”
“夫人，在下委实不通丝竹乐理，”我彬彬有礼地推托道，“一个英格兰汉子踏入江湖天地，向来缺乏其他异邦民族那种在旅途中处处逢迎讨好路人的玲珑心思；这正是为何英国绅士从来不屑于去苦练乐器弹奏的缘由所在。”
那个矮壮汉子猛地瞪大蓝眼珠死死盯住了我；紧接着，他慢条斯理地将膝头的牛皮肚带、皮条和锥子家什尽数搁在地上，拍拍尘土爬起身来，大步流星跨到我跟前，不由分说向我伸出了一只毛茸茸的大手掌。
他那副凝重肃穆的神态险些教我当场破功笑出声来。我伸手握住他的大手，打趣道：
“我的朋友，您这是唱的哪一出呢？”
“握手啊！”他大声吼道，“咱们是同乡英国老乡！”
于是我们二人便神情肃穆、极具力道地久久紧紧握手不放，室内一时间陷入了绝对的静默，而他的浑家则在一旁含笑注视着我们，手里的长勺依然不紧不慢地搅动着滚沸的油锅。
“婆娘，”他扭过头冲浑家吩咐道，“先把这锅烂油搁到明天再熬。眼下该张罗正经早膳了。屋里头还有羊肉吗？”
“仅剩下半只羊了，”妇人温顺地应道。
“半只羊对付一顿早膳足够了，”他快人快语道，“喂，特奥菲洛（Teofilo），快撒腿跑去吩咐安塞尔莫（Anselmo）去鸡窝里抓两只肥雏鸡来——记住，必须挑最肥的。立刻拔毛洗净。顺带去鸡窝里给你们娘亲摸半打（6 枚）新鲜鸡蛋来搁在杂烩炖锅里。还有，费利佩，快去把科斯梅找来，叫他赶紧备好那匹枣骝色小马，立刻飞马奔赴杂货铺采办。来，婆娘，快开张单子瞧瞧短缺什么——大米、白糖、香醋、香油、葡萄干、胡椒、藏红花、细盐、丁香、孜然芹籽、红酒、白兰地……”
“且慢且慢，老哥！”我急忙失声拦阻道，“设若您觉得非得给全军将士备齐整整一个连队的粮饷才配得上招待我用一顿早饭，那我丑话说在前头，我唯独绝不沾白兰地。在这片土地上我向来滴酒不沾烈性白兰地。”
他又激动地狠狠同我握了一次手。
“你说得太对了！”他大声赞道，“走遍天下，就得认准当地的土法浆水，哪怕那是泻药一般的黑药汤。在苏格兰便痛饮威士忌，在东岸区就豪饮朗姆烈酒——这便是我老约翰的至高金科玉律。”
刹那间，整座庄园院落陷入了一片鸡飞狗跳的热闹喧嚣之中，孩童们忙着给小马配鞍，在草地上嗷嗷乱叫追逐四处奔逃的扑棱公鸡，而我这位精力旺盛的东道主则像个统帅般把浑家支使唤得团团转。
在打发大男孩飞马前去采办、坐骑亦被悉心照料妥当之后，我们在厨房里并肩端坐了半个时辰（半小时），品啜着浓郁的马黛茶，展开了一场无比融洽畅快的促膝长谈。

===Original===

Then my host took me out into his garden behind the house to be out of his wife’s way while she was engaged cooking breakfast, and there he began talking in English.
“Twenty-five years I have been on this continent,” said he, telling me his history, “eighteen of them in the Banda Orientál.”
“Well, you have not forgotten your language,” I said. “I suppose you read?”
“Read! What! I would as soon think of wearing trousers. No, no, my friend, never read. Leave politics alone. When people molest you, shoot ’em﻿—those are my rules. Edinburgh was my home. Had enough reading when I was a boy; heard enough psalm-singing, saw enough scrubbing and scouring to last me my lifetime. My father was a bookseller in the High Street, near the Cowgate﻿—you know! Mother, she was pious﻿—they were all pious. Uncle, a minister, lived with us. That was all worse than purgatory to me. I was educated at the High School﻿—intended for the ministry, ha, ha! My only pleasure was to get a book of travels in some savage country, skulk into my room, throw off my boots, light a pipe, and lie on the floor reading﻿—locked up from everyone. Sundays just the same, They called me a sinner, said I was going to the devil﻿—fast. It was my nature. They didn’t understand﻿—kept on ding-donging in my ears. Always scrubbing, scouring﻿—you might have eaten your dinner off the floor; always singing psalms﻿—praying﻿—scolding. Couldn’t bear it; ran away at fifteen, and have never heard a word from home since. What happened? I came here, worked, saved, bought land, cattle; married a wife, lived as I liked to live﻿—am happy. There’s my wife﻿—mother of six children﻿—you have seen her yourself, a woman for a man to be proud of. No ding-donging, black looks, scouring from Monday to Saturday﻿—you couldn’t eat your dinner off my kitchen floor. There are my children, six of ’em, all told, boys and girls, healthy, dirty as they like to be, happy as the day’s long; and here am I, John Carrickfergus﻿—Don Juan all the country over, my surname no native can pronounce﻿—respected, feared, loved; a man his neighbour can rely on to do him a good turn; one who never hesitates about putting a bullet in any vulture, wild cat, or assassin that crosses his path. Now you know all.”
“An extraordinary history,” I said, “but I suppose you teach your children something?”
“Teach ’em nothing,” he returned, with emphasis.

===Chinese===

随后，趁着女主人忙于在灶台前热火朝天操持早膳的当口，为了腾出宽敞地界免得碍手碍脚，我的东道主便领着我漫步踱到了宅院后方的那片果园菜地之中，并在那里同我用一口纯正的家乡英语推心置腹地倾吐起衷肠来。
“我漂洋过海踏足这片南美大陆整整二十五年了（25 年），”他向我历数起自己的流亡发迹野史，“其中有整整十八年（18 年）全是在东岸区这片地界上度过的。”
“难得的是，您倒一点儿也没荒废遗忘自己的母语乡音，”我接茬道，“想必您平日里时常博览群书吧？”
“读书？哈！你还不如劝我天天西装革履穿长裤呢！拉倒吧，我的朋友，我这辈子打死也不碰书本。莫问国事政治。谁敢上门寻衅滋事，直接赏他一颗花生米子弹——这便是我老约翰的行事铁律。我的老家在爱丁堡。打小我就把这辈子的书全读吐了；听腻了那帮卫道士唱赞美诗的假正经，看够了把地板擦得锃光瓦亮的虚伪洁癖，足够恶心我一辈子了。我老爹当年是皇家英里大道靠近牛门峡一带的开明书商——那地界你晓得吧！我老娘是个笃信圣经的虔诚信徒——全家上下没一个不道貌岸然。我那当牧师的亲舅舅就跟我们同住一个屋檐下。那段暗无天日的日子对我而言比十八层炼狱还要煎熬百倍。我早年在爱丁堡皇家高中念书——长辈们本一心盼着我日后接班当牧师布道呢，哈哈！我当年唯一的乐子，便是偷偷弄到一本描写蛮荒异域食人族的探险游记，像做贼一样溜回自己的闺房，甩脱马靴，点燃烟斗，反锁房门把所有人全挡在门外，四仰八叉横躺在地上如饥似渴地偷读。哪怕到了安息日礼拜天我依然如法炮制。他们指着我的鼻子骂我是无可救药的罪人，断言我早晚要一头栽进地狱魔窟——且是快马加鞭地堕落。可这就是老子骨子里的天性！他们那帮榆木疙瘩根本不懂——只晓得成天像丧钟一样在我耳根子旁叮咚作响说教不休。整天洗啊擦啊——地板干净得甚至能直接盛饭趴在上面吃；整天没完没了地唱赞美诗——祈祷——斥责训诫。我实在忍无可忍；刚满十五岁（15 岁）那年我便离家出走远走高飞，自那之后直到今天，我连老家的一纸音信都未曾过问过。后来怎样呢？我漂洋过海闯荡到了这里，流血流汗做工攒钱，购置了自己的草场庄园与牛羊牲口；迎娶了贤惠浑家，按自己骨子里最向往的方式快活过活——老子这辈子活得快活如神仙！瞧瞧我那浑家——生养了六个健壮的骨肉儿女（6 个孩子）——你刚才也亲眼目睹了，那当真是个教任何男子汉大丈夫都引以为傲的贤内助。既没有整天像敲丧钟般的指责唠叨，亦没有横眉冷对的丧气脸色，更没有从周一到周六永无休止的洗洗擦擦——在老子的厨房泥地上，你可休想直接趴在地上捡饭粒吃。再瞧瞧我那些小崽子们，前前后后足足六个，有男有女，健壮得像牛犊，随心所欲想把自己滚得多脏就滚得多脏，整天乐天逍遥不知愁滋味；而老子我呢，约翰·卡里克弗格斯（John Carrickfergus）——在全邦上下人人尊称我一声‘唐·胡安’（Don Juan），皆因我的英国苏格兰大姓这群本地土著根本嚼不烂舌头念不出来——受人敬畏、受人爱戴；在四邻八舍眼里，我是一个随叫随到随时肯拔刀相助的过硬硬汉；谁若是敢像秃鹫、野猫或杀手那般不知死活横插在我的去路上，老子拔枪射穿他的脑门连眼皮都不眨一下。如今你全晓得了吧！”
“当真是一段惊心动魄的旷世传奇，”我由衷慨叹道，“不过我猜想，您平日里总归会教授孩子们些许文化知识吧？”
“一星半点都不教！”他斩钉截铁掷地有声地回绝道。

===Original===

“All we think about in the old country are books, cleanliness, clothes; what’s good for soul, brain, stomach; and we make ’em miserable. Liberty for everyone﻿—that’s my rule. Dirty children are healthy, happy children. If a bee stings you in England, you clap on fresh dirt to cure the pain. Here we cure all kinds of pain with dirt. If my child is ill I dig up a spadeful of fresh mould and rub it well﻿—best remedy out. I’m not religious, but I remember *one* miracle. The Saviour spat on the ground and made mud with the spittle to anoint the eyes of the blind man. Made him see directly. What does that mean? Common remedyof the country, of course. *He* didn’t need the clay, but followed the custom, same as in the other miracles. In Scotland dirt’s wickedness﻿—how’d they reconcile that with Scripture? I don’t say *Nature*, mind, I say, *Scripture*, because the Bible’s the book they swear by, though they didn’t write it.”
“I shall think over what you say about children, and the best way to rear them,” I returned. “I needn’t decide in a hurry, as I haven’t any yet.”
He barked his short laugh and led me back to the house, where the arrangements for breakfast were now completed. The children took their meal in the kitchen, we had ours in a large, cool room adjoining it. There was a small table laid with a spotless white cloth, and real crockery plates and real knives and forks. There were also real glass tumblers, bottles of Spanish wine, and snow-white *pan creollo*. Evidently my hostess had made good use of her time. She came in immediately after we were seated, and I scarcely recognized her; for she was not only clean now, but good-looking as well, with that rich olive colour on her oval face, her black hair well arranged, and her dark eyes full of tender, loving light. She was now wearing a white merino dress with a quaint maroon-coloured pattern on it, and a white silk kerchief fastened with a gold brooch at her neck. It was pleasant to look at her, and, noticing my admiring glances, she blushed when she sat down, then laughed. The breakfast was excellent. Roast mutton to begin, then a dish of chickens stewed with rice, nicely flavoured and coloured with red Spanish pimenton. A fowl roasted or boiled, as we eat them in England, is wasted, compared with this delicious *guiso de potto* which one gets in any rancho in the Banda Orient.

===Chinese===

“在咱们英国那片老迈腐朽的故土上，大人们满脑子装的全是书本、整洁卫生和光鲜衣衫；整天煞有介事地琢磨着何为对灵魂、大脑与肚皮大有裨益的清规戒律；结果反倒把孩子们折磨得生不如死。给每一个人最纯粹的自由——这便是老子至高无上的家训。浑身裹满泥巴的小崽子才是最生龙活虎、最无忧无虑的快乐天使。在英格兰若是被蜜蜂蛰了，人们习惯抓一把新鲜的湿泥巴糊在伤口上来镇痛消肿。可在这片自由的热土上，咱们全凭这一捧泥巴来医治人世间的所有疑难杂症。我家的小崽子若是闹了病痛，老子抄起铁锹掘出一铁锨新鲜泥土，在患处狠狠揉搓擦拭一番——天下间绝没有比这更药到病除的无上灵丹妙药了。我虽不信那些假模假样的宗教教条，但我却死死铭记着圣经里的‘一桩’通天神迹。救主当年吐唾沫在泥地上，用唾沫和了泥抹在那瞎子的眼眶上，那盲人立刻双目重见光明。这昭示着何等至理呢？分明是在昭示着，泥土原本就是天地间最普世通行的治病良方嘛。救主何等通天法力，祂老人家本无需借助凡泥，却依然顺从了这份天地风俗，正如祂在其他神迹中所行的一样。在苏格兰那帮道貌岸然的伪君子眼里，污垢泥巴便是万恶的罪愆——可他们究竟该如何向圣经经文自圆其说呢？你且记好了，我嘴里吐露的并非什么‘自然造化’，我强调的乃是‘圣经经文’本身，皆因圣经本就是那帮伪君子指天发誓顶礼膜拜的无上经典，尽管那部巨著压根不是他们自己执笔写就的。”
“关于对待孩童的教养之道，以及如何抚育后代的至理名言，我定会细细咂摸深思，”我微笑着回应道，“反正我膝下尚无半个子嗣，眼下倒也无需急于拍板定论。”
他再度爆发出了一声短促如犬吠般的豪迈大笑，领着我折返回了宅邸主屋，此时早膳的筹划已然齐备妥当。孩子们全在宽敞的厨房里各自大快朵颐，而我们则被延请到了毗邻厨房的一间宽大阴凉的正厅雅座之中。一张小巧的长桌上整齐铺陈着一袭洁白无瑕的雪白桌布，桌上摆放着货真价实的考究瓷盘，以及货真价实的不锈钢刀叉。此外竟然还破天荒地备齐了真正的玻璃水杯、西班牙红酒，以及洁白如积雪的土制克里奥尔白面包（*pan creollo*）。显而易见，方才这段工夫我的女东道主以惊人的麻利劲头打理好了一切。待我们方才落座，她便款步迈入了正厅，那一瞬间我险些揉揉眼睛不敢相认；皆因眼前的佳人不仅洗净了周身的油污尘滓，整个人更是显得明艳动人、楚楚动人，那张鸭蛋秀脸上泛着健康饱满的深橄榄肤色，墨染般的青丝梳理得一丝不苟层次分明，深邃的漆黑明眸里流淌着脉脉含情的万千柔波。她此刻换上了一袭雪白的细毛呢连衣裙，裙摆上绣着质朴雅致的栗红色滚边暗纹，颈项间系着一条雪白的真丝方巾，用一枚精致的纯金胸针端庄地别在锁骨中央。凝视着这般风韵犹存的佳人委实令人心旷神怡，而察觉到我眼中流露出的惊艳激赏神色，她在垂眸落座之际面颊泛起了一抹娇羞的绯红，随后扑哧一声忍俊不禁笑出了声来。这顿早膳的水准端的是登峰造极。先是喷香扑鼻的烤全羊肉垫底，随后端上了一大钵用藏红花与红辣椒精心调味焖煨的大米炖肥雏鸡，色泽金黄诱人汤汁浓郁。

===Original===

After the meats we sat for an hour cracking walnuts, sipping wine, smoking cigarettes, and telling amusing stories; and I doubt whether there were three happier people in all Uruguay that morning than the un-Scotched Scotchman, John Carrickfergus, his un-ding-donging native wife, and their guest, who had shot his man on the previous evening.
After breakfast I spread my poncho on the dry grass under a tree to sleep the siesta. My slumbers lasted a long time, and on waking I was surprised to find my host and hostess seated on the grass near me, he busy ornamenting his surcingle, she with the maté-cup in her hand and a kettle of hot water beside her. She was drying her eyes, I fancied, when I opened mine.
“Awake at last!” cried Don Juan pleasantly. “Come and drink maté. Wife just been crying, you see.”
She made a sign for him to hold his peace.
“Why not speak of it, Candelaria?” he said. “Where is the harm? You see, my wife thinks you have been in the wars﻿—a Santa Coloma man running away to save his throat.”
“How does she make that out?” I asked in some confusion and very much surprised.
“How! Don’t you know women? You said nothing about where you had been﻿—prudence. That was one thing. Looked confused when we talked of the revolution﻿—not a word to say about it. More evidence. Your poncho, lying there, shows two big cuts in it. ‘Torn by thorns,’ said I. ‘Sword-cuts,’ said she. We were arguing about it when you woke.”
“She guessed rightly,” I said, “and I am ashamed of myself for not telling you before. But why should your wife cry?”
“Woman like﻿—woman like,” he answered, waving his hand. “Always ready to cry over the beaten one﻿—that is the only politics they know.”
“Did I not say that woman is an angel from heaven,” I returned; then, taking her hand, I kissed it. “This is the first time I have kissed a married woman’s hand, but the husband of such a wife will know better than to be jealous.”
“Jealous﻿—ha, ha!” he laughed. “It would have made me prouder if you had kissed her cheek.”
“Juan﻿—a nice thing to say!” exclaimed his wife, slapping his hand tenderly.
Then while we sipped maté I told them the history of my campaign, finding it necessary, when explaining my motives for joining the rebels, to make some slight deviations from the strictest form of truth.

===Chinese===

在风卷残云般将肥美肉食尽数扫荡一空之后，我们围坐在一起整整消磨了一小时（1 小时）的悠闲时光，惬意地敲碎核桃坚果，轻啜醇酒，吞云吐雾抽着卷烟，彼此分享着各类妙趣横生的荒野笑谈；我甚至深信，在那个明媚的早晨，在整个乌拉圭全境的辽阔山川之中，绝不可能寻觅到比我们三个人更为快活逍遥的神仙眷侣了——一位早已彻底褪去了苏格兰刻板习气的豪迈汉子约翰·卡里克弗格斯，一位从不唠叨说教的本地土著贤妻，以及他们这位在昨夜刚刚拔枪亲手毙敌逃亡的英伦落难浪子。
早餐过后，我将自己的毛毡大氅顺势铺展在一株参天大树底下的干燥青草地上，惬意地享受起午间小憩的悠长清梦。这一觉我睡得极其深沉香甜，待到悠悠转醒睁开双眸之际，我惊诧地发现男主人与女主人正双双并肩静坐在我身旁的草地上，他正低头全神贯注地在牛皮肚带上穿针引线雕琢图腾，而她则纤手捧着一盅马黛茶，身旁煨着一壶滚烫的沸水。在我睁开眼睛的那一刹那，我隐约瞥见她正悄悄拭去眼角沁出的泪花。
“总算睡醒了，老弟！”唐·胡安爽朗和蔼地朗声唤道，“快过来喝口热腾腾的马黛茶吧。你瞧瞧，我家婆娘方才正为你抹眼泪呢。”
妇人慌忙悄悄扯了扯丈夫的衣襟，示意他莫要多嘴饶舌。
“这有什么好避讳遮掩的，坎德拉里亚（Candelaria）？”汉子大咧咧地嚷嚷道，“这又不是什么丢人现眼的坏事！老弟，你有所不知，我家浑家断定你定是刚刚在兵荒马乱的沙场上逃出生天的义士——是圣科洛马手底下一心只顾保住自个儿项上人头的亡命军官。”
“尊夫人究竟是从何处瞧出破绽的呢？”我心头猛地一紧，既颇感窘迫无地自容，又着实大惑不解。
“从何处？哈哈，你难道还不懂得女人那副七窍玲珑心吗？你进门之后对自己从何处来绝口不提——这叫谨慎防备，此乃破绽其一。我们方才闲聊谈及革命叛乱的风吹草动时，你神色闪烁慌乱——连半个字都不敢搭茬置评，此乃破绽其二。更为过硬的铁证还在后头呢：你扔在那儿的那领毛毡大氅，上面赫然豁开了两道触目惊心的大口子。‘定是在乱荆棘丛里挂破的，’老子当时这般揣测。‘分明是刀剑劈砍撕裂的伤痕！’她却一口咬定。正当我们两口子为了这事争执不休的当口，你这老弟便醒转过来了。”
“尊夫人料事如神法眼无差，”我羞愧难当地坦白道，“方才未曾对二位如实相告，在下委实自惭形秽。然而，尊夫人因何缘故要为此暗自垂泪呢？”
“妇道人家嘛——妇人之仁，”他轻描淡写地摆了摆手应道，“但凡瞧见吃了败仗落难的倒霉蛋，她们那眼泪便像断了线的珠子般往下掉——这便是天底下娘们儿唯一能读懂的军国大政了。”
“我方才难道不曾说过，女人乃是来自天国的安琪儿吗，”我由衷动容地接茬道；随后我上前一步，轻轻执起妇人的玉手，在她手背上印下了一记庄严的吻礼，“这是我生平头一遭亲吻一位有夫之妇的纤手，但我深信，能迎娶这般绝代贤妻的伟丈夫，断不至于心胸狭隘到为此大吃干醋。”
“吃醋？哈哈哈哈！”他仰天豪迈大笑起来，“老弟，设若你方才大着胆子直接亲吻她的香腮面颊，老子心头反倒会感到更加无上荣光与自豪呢！”
“胡安——瞧你这张嘴胡咧咧些什么呢！”他的浑家娇嗔地轻啐了一声，含情脉脉地在他坚实的手背上轻轻拍打了一记。
随后，我们围坐在一起啜饮着清香的马黛茶，我便将自己投身行伍随军征战的前因后果如实向他们全盘托出，唯独在剖白自己为何会鬼迷心窍投奔叛军的真实动机上，不得不违心地稍作粉饰与善意的微小遮掩。

===Original===

He agreed that my best plan was to go on to Rocha to wait there for a passport before proceeding to Montevideo. But I was not allowed to leave them that day; and, while we talked over our maté, Candelaria deftly repaired the telltale cuts in my poncho.
I spent the afternoon making friends with the children, who proved to be very intelligent and amusing little beggars, telling them some nonsensical stories I invented, and listening to their bird’s-nesting, armadillo-chasing, and other adventures. Then came a late dinner, after which the children said their prayers and retired, then we smoked and sang songs without an accompaniment, and I finished a happy day by sinking to sleep in a soft, clean bed.
I had announced my intention of leaving at daybreak next morning; and when I woke, finding it already light, I dressed hastily, and, going out, found my horse already saddled standing, with three other saddled horses, at the gate. In the kitchen I found Don Juan, his wife, and the two biggest boys having their early maté. My host told me that he had been up an hour, and was only waiting to wish me a prosperous journey before going out to gather up his cattle. He at once wished me goodbye, and with his two boys went off, leaving me to partake of poached eggs and coffee﻿—quite an English breakfast.
I then rose and thanked the good señora for her hospitality.
“One moment,” she said, when I held out my hand, and, drawing a small silk bag from her bosom, she offered it to me. “My husband has given me permission to present you with this at parting. It is only a small gift, but while you are in this trouble and away from all your friends it perhaps might be of use to you.”
I did not wish to take money from her after all the kind treatment I had received, and so allowed the purse to lie on my open hand where she had placed it.
“And if I cannot accept it﻿—” I began.
“Then you will hurt me very much,” she replied. “Could you do that after the kind words you spoke yesterday?”
I could not resist, but, after putting the purse away, took her hand and kissed it.
“Goodbye, Candelaria,” I said, “you have made me love your country and repent every harsh word I have ever spoken against it.”
Her hand remained in mine; she stood smiling, and did not seem to think the last word had been spoken yet.

===Chinese===

他万分赞同我当下的谋划，认定眼下最明智的方略莫过于径直赶赴罗恰，在当地僻静处潜伏等待首府签发一份正规护照，随后再大摇大摆返回蒙得维的亚。然而那一整天他们断不肯放我启程辞行；正当我们围炉品茗高谈阔论之际，坎德拉里亚已然取过针线，以巧夺天工的娴熟针法将我毛毡大氅上那两道暴露身份的狰狞刀痕缝补得天衣无缝。
整个下午我全在同那群孩童嬉戏打闹中度过，这帮小泥猴骨子里聪慧机敏至极且讨人喜欢，我给他们胡编乱造了许多光怪陆离的童话寓言，又饶有兴致地倾听他们眉飞色舞地吹嘘起如何掏鸟蛋、在荒野上围追堵截犰狳（armadillo）的种种惊险趣事。及至夜幕降临，一顿丰盛的晚宴再次摆开，饭后孩童们虔诚地做完晚祷便各自回房安歇，随后我们几个大人一边抽着卷烟，一边毫无伴奏地清唱起各类质朴民谣，最终，在一张柔软洁净的温软床榻深处，我沉沉坠入黑甜乡，为这幸福完满的一天画上了圆满的句点。
前一天我早已向主人家告假，声明自己将在翌日拂晓破晓时分策马启程；当我翌日惊醒睁眼发觉窗外天光微亮之际，便手忙脚乱穿戴齐备赶忙跨出门去，赫然发现自己的坐骑早已被悉心备好马鞍，同另外三匹备好鞍的骏马（3 匹备好鞍的马）并排拴在篱笆门桩旁。在厨房里，唐·胡安、他的浑家以及两个个头最大的大男孩正围坐在一起品啜着清晨的第一道早茶。男主人告诉我，他早在一个时辰前便已起身忙碌了，仅仅是为了在跨马出巡料理牛群之前亲自向我互道珍重祝我一路顺风。他当即豪迈地同我拱手道别，随后领着两个儿子扬长而去，留下我一人在餐厅里享用荷包蛋与热咖啡——这真是一顿地地道道的英格兰式正统早点。
用罢早餐我起身向这位善良贤惠的女主人致谢，感激她全家上下对我的盛情款待。
“且慢，尊客，”当我伸出右手欲行告别之际她轻声挽留道，顺势从温软的胸襟深处掏出了一只精致小巧的真丝荷包，双手捧着递到了我的面前，“我家夫君临行前特意恩准我，在临别之际将此物赠予尊客充作盘缠。这不过是微不足道的一点薄礼，但眼下您正逢落难多事之秋，远离故土亲友，或许能解您的燃眉之急也未可知。”
在受尽了这户人家无微不至的温存体贴之后，要我倒收下这位仁厚妇人的银两盘缠，委实大违我的做人底线，因而我只是任由那只沉甸甸的钱袋静静躺在自己平摊的手心之上。
“设若在下断不敢收受这份厚礼……”我吞吞吐吐地开口推托。
“那您可当真要狠狠伤透我的心肠了，”她眼眶微红地轻声嗔怪道，“在您昨日对我吐露了那么多肺腑之言之后，您难道忍心做出这般生分绝情之举吗？”
我再也无力推脱，只得妥帖将钱袋收纳贴身藏好，随后轻轻执起她温润的柔荑贴在唇边深情亲吻。
“珍重，坎德拉里亚，”我动容地说道，“你不仅让我深沉地爱上了你的祖国，更让我为自己过去针对这片土地所吐露过的每一句刻薄中伤而痛悔终生。”
她的手依然依依不舍地温存留存在我的掌心之中；她站在晨曦中含苞浅笑，似乎冥冥中觉得这最后的诀别之言尚未彻底诉尽一般。

===Original===

Then, seeing her there looking so sweet and loving, and remembering the words her husband had spoken the day before, I stooped and kissed her cheek and lips.
“Adieu, my friend, and God be with you,” she said.
I think there were tears in her eyes when I left her, but I could not see clearly, for mine also had suddenly grown dim.
And only the day before I had felt amused at the sight of this woman sitting hot and greasy over her work, and had called her Juno of the grease-pot! Now, after an acquaintance of about eighteen hours, I had actually kissed her﻿—a wife and the mother of six children, bidding her adieu with trembling voice and moist eyes! I know that I shall never forget those eyes, full of sweet, pure affection and tender sympathy, looking into mine; all my life long shall I think of Candelaria, loving her like a sister. Could any woman in my own ultra-civilised and excessively proper country inspire me with a feeling like that in so short a time? I fancy not. Oh, civilisation, with your million conventions, soul and body withering prudishnesses, vain education for the little ones, going to church in best black clothes, unnatural craving for cleanliness, feverish striving after comforts that bring no comfort to the heart, are you a mistake altogether? Candelaria and that genial runaway John Carrickfergus make me think so. Ah, yes, we are all vainly seeking after happiness in the wrong way. It was with us once and ours, but we despised it, for it was only the old, common happiness which Nature gives to all her children, and we went away from it in search of another grander kind of happiness which some dreamer﻿—Bacon or another﻿—assured us we should find. We had only to conquer Nature, find out her secrets, make her our obedient slave, then the earth would be Eden, and every man Adam and every Woman Eve. We are still marching bravely on, conquering Nature, but how weary and sad we are getting! The old joy in life and gaiety of heart have vanished, though we do sometimes pause for a few moments in our long forced march to watch the labours of some pale mechanician seeking after perpetual motion and indulge in a little dry, cackling laugh at his expense.

===Chinese===

彼时彼刻，凝视着眼前这位神色如此温存甜美、满怀深情仁爱的妇人，回想起她夫君在前一日吐露的那番豪迈豁达的金玉良言，我情不自禁俯下身去，深情地吻向了她的粉颊与双唇。
“永别了，我的朋友，愿天主时刻庇佑你，”她柔声呢喃道。
当最终转身跨出门槛的那一刻，我依稀瞥见她的眸底闪烁着晶莹的泪光，然而我的视线已然无法分辨得真切，皆因我自己的双眼亦在刹那间被夺眶而出的滚烫泪水彻底遮蔽模糊了。
而在仅仅一天之前，目睹这位妇人浑身油垢、汗流浃背地围着油锅操劳的狼狈模样，我还曾在心底刻薄地打趣讥讽，戏称她是油罐旁的朱诺！然而在仅仅相识了约十八个小时（约 18 个小时）之后，我竟然真真切切地亲吻了她——一位有夫之妇、六个健壮孩童的亲生母亲（6 个孩子），用颤抖哽咽的嗓音和满眶的热泪向她依依惜别！我心知肚明自己此生此世绝不可能忘怀那一双眸子——那一双倒映在我的眼帘深处、盛满了纯洁无瑕的挚爱深情与温柔体恤的明澈双眸；在未来的漫漫余生里，我将永远怀念坎德拉里亚，像珍爱亲生手足胞妹一般深爱着她。试问在我那极度文明、礼教森严且循规蹈矩得令人窒息的英格兰故土之上，天下间又有哪一位贵妇名媛能在如此短暂的须臾时光里，激荡起我灵魂深处这般纯粹至诚的波澜呢？我想绝无可能。啊，虚伪的现代文明啊，你那成千上万条繁文缛节，你那摧残身心活力的道貌岸然与假正经，你那强加给无辜孩童的无用虚妄教育，你那身披最考究的黑色礼服正襟危坐去教堂礼拜的伪善，你那对一尘不染的病态洁癖，以及你那为了追逐根本无法给心灵带来半分慰藉的物质奢靡而展开的狂热角逐——难道你从头到尾根本就是一场彻头彻尾的弥天大错吗？坎德拉里亚与那位生性豁达开朗的英国逃兵约翰·卡里克弗格斯，逼得我不得不对此深信不疑。啊，不错，我们全人类都在以南辕北辙的荒唐途径虚妄地追逐着幸福。真切的幸福曾经一度常驻在我们身旁任由我们采撷，可我们却弃之如敝履，皆因那不过是博爱的大自然平等恩赐给每一个儿女的质朴寻常之乐，而我们却头也不回地背道而驰，转而去寻觅某种被某些空想家——如培根（Bacon）之流——赌咒发誓宣称我们终将寻得的更为宏大辉煌的虚妄极乐。他们宣称我们只需征服大自然，穷尽造化的全部奥秘，令造化沦为人类任劳任怨的驯服奴隶，人间便将化作无忧乐园伊甸园，而天底下的每一个凡人皆将化作无所不能的亚当与夏娃。如今我们依然在征服大自然的漫漫征途上雄赳赳气昂昂地大步挺进，然而我们整个人类文明却变得何其困顿颓唐、何其凄凉悲哀啊！生命中原本那种质朴的天伦之乐与心底的纯真豁达早已荡然无存，尽管在这场漫长窒息的强行军征途之中，我们偶尔也会驻足片刻，冷眼旁观某个面色惨白的机械狂徒苦苦钻研永动机的徒劳滑稽身影，并拿他的荒唐可悲稍作消遣，挤出一丝干瘪刺耳的冷笑罢了。

## XXII A Crown of Nettles / 第二十二章 荨麻之冠
===Original===

After leaving John and Candelaria’s home of liberty and love, nothing further worth recording happened till I had nearly reached the desired haven of the Lomas de Rocha, a place which I was, after all, never destined to see except from a great distance. A day unusually brilliant even for this bright climate was drawing to a close, it being within about two hours of sunset, when I turned out of my way to ascend a hill with a very long, ridge-like summit, falling away at one end, appearing like the last sierra of a range just where it dies down into the level plain; only in this instance the range itself did not exist. The solitary hill was covered with short tussocks of yellow, wiry grass, with occasional bushes, while near the summit large slabs of sandstone appeared just above the surface, looking like gravestones in some old village churchyard, with all their inscriptions obliterated by time and weather. From this elevation, which was about a hundred feet above the plain, I wished to survey the country before me, for I was tired and hungry, so was my horse, and I was anxious to find a resting place before night. Before me the country stretched away in vast undulations towards the ocean, which was not, however, in sight. Not the faintest stain of vapour appeared on the immense crystalline dome of heaven, while the stillness and transparency of the atmosphere seemed almost preternatural. A blue gleam of water, southeast of where I stood and many leagues distant, I took to be the lake of Rocha; on the western horizon were faint blue cloud-like masses with pearly peaks. They were not clouds, however, but the sierras of the range weirdly named Cuchilla de las Animas﻿—Ghost-haunted Mountains. At length, like a person who puts his binocular into his pocket and begins to look about him, I recalled my vision from its wanderings over illimitable space to examine the objects close at hand. On the slope of the hill, sixty yards from my standpoint, were some deep green, dwarf bushes, each bush looking in that still brilliant sunshine as if it had been hewn out of a block of malachite; and on the pale purple solanaceous flowers covering them some humblebees were feeding.

===Chinese===

在告别了约翰与坎德拉里亚那座充满自由与仁爱的港湾之后，一路风平浪静并无多余的奇闻轶事值得载入青史，直至我行将抵达梦寐以求的避难圣地罗恰丘陵（Lomas de Rocha）——然而造化弄人，这处胜地最终我也仅仅注定只能在遥远的天际回眸远眺罢了。对于这片晴空万里的明朗气候而言，那一天的阳光亦显得异乎寻常的璀璨夺目，待到时近向晚、距离落日约两小时（距落日约 2 小时）的光景，我偏离了前行的路径，策马登临了一座耸立着漫长山脊状峰顶的高耸山丘；山丘的一侧向外陡然跌落，极像是一条绵延山脉在行将沉降湮没入一马平川的大平原之际留下的最后一抹残峰，唯独在此处，那条山脉本身却根本不复存在。这座形单影只的孤丘之上覆满了一丛丛枯黄坚韧的马蔺硬草，间或点缀着几丛零星的野灌木；而靠近峰顶之处，大块大块的砂岩石板赫然探出了地表，看起来宛如某座古老乡村教堂墓园里的斑驳墓碑，上面的全部碑铭早已在漫长岁月与风霜雨雪的侵蚀下荡然无存。从这处高出平原约一百英尺（约 100 英尺）的高地之上，我极目远眺着眼前的浩渺江山，皆因我自身早已饥肠辘辘困顿不堪，座下的战马亦是疲惫至极，我迫切渴望在夜幕降临前寻得一处安身栖所。在我眼前，苍茫的平原宛如浩瀚汪洋般起伏延展，一直向着大洋深处逶迤奔流，然而海平线本身却并未显现于目力所及之处。在苍穹浩瀚剔透的水晶巨碗之上，未曾漂浮着哪怕最微弱的一丝云翳水汽，天地间的大气是如此死寂澄澈，近乎显现出一种超自然的空灵。在极目东南方向数里格开外的一抹碧水蓝光，依我辨识大抵便是罗恰湖的所在；而在极西的地平线上，则耸立着一片宛如淡蓝云团、顶上泛着珍珠母贝般光泽的巍峨峰峦。然而那些并非天际的浮云，而是被当地人赋予了神秘离奇名号的山峦——“幽灵山脉”（Cuchilla de las Animas，意为灵魂与幽灵盘踞之山）。良久之后，恰如一个将双筒望远镜收纳入兜里转而开始打量周遭景物的人一般，我终于将目光从漫无边际的极目远方收了回来，细细端详起近在眼前的咫尺风物。就在距我伫立处六十码（60 码）开外的山坡之上，生长着几丛深碧色的低矮灌木，在依旧炽烈璀璨的夕阳余晖沐浴之下，每一株灌木看起来皆仿若由一整块孔雀石精雕细琢而成；在那覆满枝头的淡紫色茄科花序之上，几只大黄蜂正在贪婪地采蜜授粉。

===Original===

It was the humming of the bees coming distinctly to my ears that first attracted my attention to the bushes; for so still was the atmosphere that at that distance apart﻿—sixty yards﻿—two persons might have conversed easily without raising their voices. Much farther down, about two hundred yards from the bushes, a harrier hawk stood on the ground, tearing at something it had captured, feeding in that savage, suspicious manner usual with hawks, with long pauses between the bites. Over the harrier hovered a brown milvago hawk, a vulture-like bird in its habits, that lives by picking up unconsidered trifles. Envious at the other’s good fortune, or fearing, perhaps, that not even the crumbs or feathers of the feast were going to be left, it was persecuting the harrier by darting down at intervals with an angry cry and aiming a blow with its wing. The harrier methodically ducked its head each time its tormentor rushed down at it, after which it would tear its prey again in its uncomfortable manner. Farther away, in the depression running along at the foot of the hill, meandered a small stream so filled with aquatic grasses and plants that the water was quite concealed, its course appearing like a vivid green snake, miles long, lying there basking in the sunshine. At the point of the stream nearest to me an old man was seated on the ground, apparently washing himself, for he was stooping over a little pool of water, while behind him stood his horse with patient, drooping head, occasionally switching off the flies with its tail. A mile farther on stood a dwelling, which looked to me like an old estancia house, surrounded by large shade trees growing singly or in irregular clumps. It was the only house near, but after gazing at it for some time I concluded that it was uninhabited. For even at that distance I could see plainly that there were no human beings moving about it, no horse or other domestic animal near, and there were certainly no hedges or enclosures of any description.
Slowly I went down the hill, and to the old man sitting beside the stream. I found him engaged in the seemingly difficult operation of disentangling a luxuriant crop of very long hair, which had somehow﻿—possibly from long neglect﻿—got itself into great confusion.

===Chinese===

最初吸引我目光投向那些灌木的，正是清晰传入我耳鼓的一阵阵蜜蜂嗡嗡轰鸣声；皆因周遭的大气是如此安详死寂，以至于相隔整整六十码（60 码）的遥远距离，两个人无需刻意提高嗓门便可轻松促膝长谈。而在山坡下方更深邃处，距离灌木丛约二百码（约 200 码）开外的开阔地上，一只白尾鹞鹰正孤傲地伫立在泥土之上，撕扯吞噬着刚刚擒获的猎物，以猛禽特有的凶悍多疑姿态进食，在每一次撕咬吞咽的间隙都要警惕地停顿张望良久。在鹞鹰头顶上方盘旋盘桓着一只黄头叫鹰，一种在习性上极似秃鹫、全凭捡拾残羹冷炙为生的食腐猛禽。出于对同侪好运的眼红嫉妒，抑或是唯恐连骨屑羽毛都捞不到半点残渣，它不断自高空俯冲而下尖声啼鸣，借由振翅拍击企图横加骚扰驱逐那只鹞鹰。而鹞鹰则极为从容老练地在每一次强盗凌空扑击之际低头闪避，随后再度以那种极不舒坦的拧巴姿势接续撕扯着肉食。更远处，在顺着山脚蜿蜒伸展的地势凹陷之中，流淌着一条涓涓小溪，溪流之中繁茂地生长着海量水草与水生植物，以至于水面几乎被彻底掩蔽不见，整条水系看起来宛如一条横卧在日光之下沐浴艳阳、长达数英里的翠绿巨蟒。在小溪距离我最近的一处浅滩旁，一位老者正盘腿坐在地上，似乎正在俯身梳洗沐浴，他正弓着背凑在一处小水洼上方，身后静静伫立着他的坐骑，温驯地耷拉着脑袋，偶尔漫不经心地摇晃尾巴驱赶虻蝇。在约莫一英里开外的开阔地上耸立着一栋宅邸，在我眼中俨然是一座上了年岁的大庄园（estancia）大宅，四周环绕着零星独植或成簇错落的高耸遮荫乔木。这是方圆周遭唯一的人烟建筑，然而在屏息端详了良久之后，我得出了此地已荒无人烟的结论。皆因即便隔着这般遥远的距离，我亦能真切地看清宅院前后毫无半个大活人走动的踪迹，近旁亦无战马或牛羊等家畜生灵出没，且四周绝无任何规整的树篱与篱笆围墙。
我勒马徐徐走下山丘，缓步踱到了那位静坐在溪畔的老翁身旁。我发觉他正致力于一项看起来极其繁重艰巨的大工程——梳理理顺他头顶那一蓬极其茂密滋长、却因常年疏于打理而彻底纠缠打结成一团乱麻的苍苍白发。

===Original===

He had dipped his head into the water, and with an old comb, boasting about seven or eight teeth, was laboriously and with infinite patience drawing out the long hairs, a very few at a time. After saluting him, I lit a cigarette, and, leaning on the neck of my horse, watched his efforts for some time with profound interest. He toiled away in silence for five or six minutes, then dipped his head in the water again, and, while carefully wringing the wet out, he remarked that my horse looked tired.
“Yes,” I replied; “so is his rider. Can you tell me who lives in that estancia?”
“My master,” he returned laconically.
“Is he a good-hearted man﻿—one who will give shelter to a stranger?” I asked.
He took a very long time to answer me, then said:
“He has nothing to say about such matters.”
“An invalid?” I remarked.
Another long pause; then he shook his head and tapped his forehead significantly; after which he resumed his mermaid task.
“Demented?” said I.
He elevated an eyebrow and shrugged his shoulders, but said nothing.
After a long silence, for I was anxious not to irritate him with too much questioning, I ventured to remark:
“Well, they will not set the dogs on me, will they?”
He grinned, and said that it was an establishment without dogs.
I paid him for his information with a cigarette, which he took very readily, and seemed to think smoking a pleasant relief after his disentangling labours.
“An estancia without dogs, and where the master has nothing to say﻿—that sounds strange,” I remarked tentatively, but he puffed on in silence.
“What is the name of the house?” I said, after remounting my horse.
“It is a house without a name,” he replied; and after this rather unsatisfactory interview I left him and slowly went on to the estancia.
On approaching the house I saw that there had formerly been a large plantation behind it, of which only a few dead stumps now remained, the ditches that had enclosed them being now nearly obliterated. The place was ruinous and overgrown with weeds. Dismounting, I led my horse along a narrow path through a perfect wilderness of wild sunflowers, horehound, red-weed, and thorn-apple, up to some poplar trees where there had once been a gate, of which only two or three broken posts remained standing in the ground.

===Chinese===

他将脑袋整颗浸泡在冰凉的溪水之中，手里握着一把仅剩七八枚残齿（7 到 8 枚齿）的破旧旧木梳，极其吃力、满怀近乎无限的耐心，极缓慢地一小缕一小缕地将纠结的发丝梳理顺畅。在向他拱手致意之后，我点燃了一支卷烟，下巴轻搭在战马的脖颈上，满怀浓厚的兴致津津有味地观摩起他的这番苦战。他默不作声地埋头苦干了约五六分钟（5 到 6 分钟），随后再度把脑袋狠狠扎入水中浸湿，一边小心翼翼地拧干发丝上的水渍，一边冷不丁漫不经心地吐露道我的马儿看起来疲乏得很。
“正是，”我答道，“马背上的骑手亦累得够呛。老丈能否指点一二，那座大庄园里眼下究竟住着何方神圣？”
“我的东家，”他言简意赅惜字如金地应道。
“东家可是位善心肠的长者——肯收留萍水相逢的外乡人借宿一宿吗？”我追问道。
他沉吟了极其漫长的一炷香工夫，方才慢悠悠吐出一句：
“东家在这些琐事上向来说不上话。”
“莫非是位抱恙缠绵病榻的病家？”我试探道。
又是一阵漫长的窒息沉默；随后他缓缓摇了摇头，意味深长地伸出一根手指轻轻敲了敲自己的脑门前额；紧接着便再度转过头去料理起他那宛如美人鱼般复杂的梳妆大业。
“心智失常了？”我恍然大悟道。
他挑了挑眉毛耸了耸肩，却紧闭双唇未发一言。
在经过了一阵长时间的屏息静默之后——皆因我唯恐因刨根问底而惹得这老丈心生厌烦——我壮着胆子试探着打破了僵局：
“那么，府上总不至于放出恶犬来撕咬生客吧？”
他嘴角泛起一抹诡异的冷笑，声称那是一处连半只看门恶犬都没有的僻静府邸。
我赏给他一支香烟以充作打探风声的酬劳，他极麻利地伸手接了过去，似乎深感在历经了方才梳理乱发的大汗淋漓之后，吞云吐雾一番委实是无上的舒坦解脱。
“一座既无恶犬看门、家主又说不上半句话的大庄园——这听起来可真够稀奇古怪的，”我意有所指地试探道，可他却只是在青烟缭绕中默然吞吐。
“那座宅院究竟唤作何名呢？”在重新翻身上马之后我回首询问道。
“那是一处没有名号的宅院，”他冷冷地丢下这句没头没脑的回应；在这番着实教人摸不着头脑的谈话告终之后，我便辞别了老翁，策马信步徐徐朝着那座庄园走去。
正当迫近宅院之际，我赫然瞧见宅邸后方早年间分明曾是一片开阔齐整的大林园，然而眼下仅残存着几根枯死的干树桩，当年环绕林盘的壕沟亦已几乎被泥土淤塞铲平。整座宅院破败不堪荒烟蔓草。我翻身下马，牵着坐骑沿着一条穿行在野生向日葵、欧夏至草、藜草与曼陀罗荒草丛林间的逼仄羊肠小道徐徐前行，一路摸索行至了几株高耸的白杨树跟前；此处早年间显然曾矗立着一座气派的大门，而如今地界上仅剩下两三根残破倾斜的断木桩依旧孤零零插在泥土之中。

===Original===

From the old gate the path ran on, still through weeds, to the door of the house, which was partly of stone and partly of red brick, with a very steep, sloping, tiled roof. Beside the ruined gate, leaning against a post, with the hot afternoon sun shining on her uncovered head, stood a woman in a rusty-black dress. She was about twenty-six or twenty-seven years old, and had an unutterably weary, desponding expression on her face, which was colourless as marble, except for the purple stains under her large, dark eyes. She did not move when I approached her, but raised her sorrowful eyes to my face, apparently feeling little interest in my arrival.
I took off my hat to salute her, and said:
“Señora, my horse is tired, and I am seeking for a resting place; can I have shelter under your roof?”
“Yes, caballero; why not?” she returned in a voice even more significant of sorrow than her countenance.
I thanked her, and waited for her to lead the way; but she still remained standing before me with eyes cast down, and a hesitating, troubled look on her face.
“Señora,” I began, “if a stranger’s presence in the house would be inconvenient﻿—”
“No, no, señor, it is not that,” she interrupted quickly. Then, sinking her voice almost to a whisper, she said: “Tell me, señor, have you come from the department of Florida? Have you﻿—have you been at San Paulo?”
I hesitated a little, then answered that I had.
“On which side?” she asked quickly, with a strange eagerness in her voice.
“Ah, señora,” I returned, “why do you ask me, only a poor traveller who comes for a night’s shelter, such a question﻿—”
“Why? Perhaps for your good, señor. Remember, women are not like men﻿—implacable. A shelter you shall have, señor; but it is best that I should know.”
“You are right,” I returned, “forgive me for not answering you at once. I was with Santa Coloma﻿—the rebel.”
She held out her hand to me, but, before I could take it, withdrew it and, covering her face, began to cry. Presently recovering herself and turning towards the house, she asked me to follow.
Her gestures and tears had told me eloquently enough that she too belonged to the unhappy Blanco party.
“Have you, then, lost some relation in this fight, señora?” I asked.
“No, señor,” she replied; “but if our party had triumphed, perhaps deliverance would have come to me. Ah, no; I lost my relations long ago﻿—all except my father.

===Chinese===

顺着残破的旧门桩，小径在荒草乱荆之中继续向前延伸，直通宅邸的大门；整栋建筑下半部由坚硬乱石垒砌、上半部由红砖夯筑而成，覆着一道坡度极陡的倾斜瓦顶。在坍塌的旧门桩旁侧，一位身披一袭锈迹斑斑的陈旧黑裙的妇人正斜倚着木桩孑然而立，任由午后炽热滚烫的艳阳暴晒在她未覆头巾的发丝之上。她看起来约莫二十六七岁（约 26 到 27 岁），面容上刻满了无法用言语描摹的倦怠绝望之色，那张面孔苍白得宛如一尊大理石雕塑，唯独在两只硕大幽深的漆黑眸子下方，浮现着两抹乌青微紫的凄凉泪痕。当我策马走到她近前之际，她并未移步挪动半分，仅仅是缓缓抬起那双盛满哀愁的眸子凝视着我的脸颊，对我这不速之客的降临似乎全无半分兴致。
我脱帽致意向她躬身行礼，开口道：
“夫人，我的战马疲惫不堪，而在下正苦苦寻觅一处落脚安歇之地；不知能否容我在贵府的屋檐下借宿一宿？”
“可以的，先生；天下间又有何不可呢？”她轻声应承道，那嗓音里所流露出的深沉哀恸，甚至比她凄凉的面庞更为教人动容。
我向她致以谢意，静候着她引路前行；可她依然僵立在我跟前，双眸低垂，脸上浮现出一抹犹豫不决、欲言又止的惶恐局促。
“夫人，”我见状试探道，“设若在这宅院里平添一位外邦生客委实有所不便……”
“不，不，先生，绝非那般缘故，”她急促地出言打断了我。紧接着，她将嗓音压得极低，近乎化作了一阵微弱的耳语呢喃：“告诉我，先生，您莫非是从佛罗里达省（Florida）赶来的吗？您——您莫非曾亲身涉足过圣保罗（San Paulo）战场吗？”
我心头微微一沉稍作迟疑，随后坦然承认确实如此。
“敢问您是为哪一方阵营效命的？”她急促地追问道，嗓音里涌动着一股异乎寻常的迫切渴望。
“啊，夫人，”我苦笑着分辩道，“在下不过是一个只求借宿一夜果腹安歇的落难旅人，您何苦非要向在下逼问这等要命的诛心关窍呢——”
“因何缘故？或许这恰恰是为了您的身家性命着想，先生。且请牢记，女人终归不同于男子汉——我们断不至于那般心狠手辣赶尽杀绝。庇护之所我定会赐予您，先生；但由我预先探明虚实总归是最好的。”
“夫人言之有理，”我心悦诚服地应道，“方才未曾当场吐露实情，还望夫人海涵。我此前正是跟随叛军将领圣科洛马（Santa Coloma）冲锋陷阵的。”
她神色剧震，下意识向我伸出了一只玉手，可尚未等我伸手相握，她便倏地将手收了回去，用双掌死死捂住面颊，情不自禁失声呜咽痛哭起来。良久之后她才稍稍克制住胸中的波澜，掉转娇躯面朝主屋，示意我随她入内。
她这番激越的体态与夺眶而出的热泪，已然无比雄辩且清晰地向我昭示：她自身同样是不幸的白党（Blanco）阵营中的一员忠良。
“莫非夫人在这场鏖战之中，痛失了哪位至亲手足骨肉不成？”我低声垂询道。
“没有，先生，”她凄然答道，“但设若我们这一方阵营当真大获全胜，或许我的无上救赎便会如期降临了吧。啊，不；我的至亲骨肉早在多年前便已尽数捐躯战死了——除了我的老父亲之外。

===Original===

You shall know presently, when you see him, why our cruel enemies refrained from shedding *his* blood.”
By that time we had reached the house. There had once been a verandah to it, but this had long fallen away, leaving the walls, doors, and windows exposed to sun and rain. Lichen covered the stone walls, while, in the crevices and over the tiled roof, weeds and grass had flourished; but this vegetation had died with the summer heats and was now parched and yellow. She led me into a spacious room, so dimly lighted from the low door and one small window that it seemed quite dark to me coming from the bright sunlight. I stood for a few moments trying to accustom my eyes to the gloom, while she, advancing to the middle of the apartment, bent down and spoke to an aged man seated in a leather-bound easy-chair.
“Papa,” she said, “I have brought in a young man﻿—a stranger who has asked for shelter under our roof. Welcome him, papa.”
Then she straightened herself, and, passing behind the chair, stood leaning on it, facing me.
“I wish you good day, señor,” I said, advancing with a little hesitation.
There before me sat a tall, bent old man, wasted almost to a skeleton, with a grey, desolate face and long hair and beard of a silver whiteness. He was wrapped in a light-coloured poncho, and wore a black skullcap on his head. When I spoke he leant back in his seat and began scanning my face with strangely fierce, eager eyes, all the time twisting his long, thin fingers together in a nervous, excited manner.
“What, Calixto,” he exclaimed at length, “is this the way you come into my presence? Ha, you thought I would not recognise you! Down﻿—down, boy, on your knees!”
I glanced at his daughter standing behind him; she was watching my face anxiously, and made a slight inclination with her head.
Taking this as an intimation to obey the old man’s commands, I went down on my knees, and touched my lips to the hand he extended.
“May God give you grace, my son,” he said, with tremulous voice. Then he continued: “What, did you expect to find your old father blind then? I would know you amongst a thousand, Calixto. Ah, my son, my son, why have you kept away so long? Stand, my son, and let me embrace you.”
He rose up tottering from his chair and threw his arm about me; then, after gazing into my face for some moments, deliberately kissed me on both cheeks.

===Chinese===

待到您待会儿亲眼目睹了他的尊容，您自会心知肚明，当年那些灭绝人性的残暴仇敌究竟因何由头，唯独对他老人家的性命法外开恩、未曾痛下杀手。”
说话间我们已然迈步走到了主屋檐下。宅邸早年间显然曾修筑有一道典雅的长廊凉台，然而长廊早坍塌殆尽，留下一堵堵光秃秃的土石外墙、门框与窗棂任由烈日与暴雨经年肆虐摧残。苍灰的地衣覆满了粗粝的石墙，而在碎瓦裂痕与瓦顶沟壑之间，各色杂草荒蓬曾一度疯狂滋长；然而这些乱草早已在盛夏的酷暑炙烤下枯萎凋敝，此刻化作了一片干枯焦黄的残骸。她领着我跨入了一间宽敞宏大的厅堂，从低矮的门扉与一扇逼仄的小窗户里透入的天光是如此微弱晦暗，以至于从烈日炎炎的户外陡然迈步而入的我，只觉得眼前漆黑如墨几乎目不能视。我僵立在原地数息工夫，竭力让双眸适应这室内昏暗幽深的阴影，而妇人则款步走向大厅中央，缓缓俯下身去，对着一位端坐在一张生牛皮蒙皮宽大扶手椅上的风烛残年老翁轻声耳语。
“爸爸，”她柔声道，“我领进了一位年轻后生——一位上门求宿的外邦客人。且请爸爸向客人致意吧。”
随后她端正挺拔起身姿，踱步绕到了扶手椅后方，双手轻搭在椅背上，静静端详着我。
“晚辈向老前辈请安，”我带着几分拘谨迟疑，迈步上前躬身问候道。
呈现在我眼帘深处的，是一位身形高大却极度佝偻萎靡的枯瘦老者，全身上下瘦削得宛如一具行走的活骷髅，生着一张苍白凄凉的枯槁面庞，满头长发与长髯尽数化作了雪白夺目的银丝。他浑身上下紧紧裹着一领浅色的毛毡大氅，头顶扣着一顶黑色的丝绒无沿小瓜皮帽。待我开口问候之际，他猛地将身躯深陷在椅背里，双眸迸射出一种异乎寻常的暴烈狂乱与极度焦渴的凶芒，死死上下打量研判着我的面庞，而那一双修长干瘪如枯枝般的枯槁手指，则在极度的神经亢奋战栗中不由自主地剧烈来回翻卷扭动。
“怎么，卡利克斯托（Calixto）！”沉寂良久之后他冷不丁爆发出了一声雷霆般的厉声断喝，“你竟然敢这般大模大样地擅闯老夫的殿堂？哈，你莫非以为你这老爹当真老眼昏花认不出你来了吗！给老子跪下——跪下，小畜生，跪在老子面前！”
我惊愕地瞥了一眼伫立在他身后的长女；只见她正神色焦虑万分地紧盯着我的双眸，悄无声息地向我微微颔了颔首。
意会到了这是让我顺从这位疯癫老人的号令，我毫不犹豫屈膝双膝跪倒在尘埃之中，躬身以唇轻轻触碰了他颤颤巍巍伸出的一只枯手。
“愿上帝赐你恩典，我的儿啊，”他用颤抖哽咽的嘶哑喉音喃喃自语道。紧接着他接续狂呼道：“怎么，你难不成还指望着你的老父亲双目全盲不成？便是在千军万马的滔天人海之中，老子亦能一眼把你揪出来，卡利克斯托！啊，我的骨肉，我的亲儿啊，你因何缘故狠心在外漂泊了这么多年啊？快快平身，我的儿，且让为父好好抱一抱你！”
他颤巍巍踉跄着从扶手椅上挣扎爬起身来，伸出一副枯瘦的双臂死死将我揽入怀中；随后神色狂热地死死端详了我的眉宇良久，郑重其事地在我两侧面颊上重重各亲吻了一记。

===Original===

“Ha, Calixto,” he continued, putting his trembling hands upon my shoulders and gazing into my face out of his wild, sunken eyes, “do I need ask where you have been? Where should a Peralta be but in the smoke of the battle, in the midst of carnage, fighting for the Banda Orientál? I did not complain of your absence, Calixto﻿—Demetria will tell you that I was patient through all these years, for I knew you would come back to me at last wearing the laurel wreath of victory. And I, Calixto, what have I worn, sitting here? A crown of nettles! Yes, for a hundred years I have worn it﻿—you are my witness, Demetria, my daughter, that I have worn this crown of stinging-nettles for a hundred years.”
He sank back, apparently exhausted, in his chair, and I uttered a sigh of relief, thinking the interview was now over. But I was mistaken. His daughter placed a chair for me at his side. “Sit here, señor, and talk to my father, while I have your horse taken care of,” she whispered, and then quickly glided from the room. This was rather hard on me, I thought; but while whispering those few words she touched my hand lightly and turned her wistful eyes with a grateful look on mine, and I was glad for her sake that I had not blundered.
Presently the old man roused himself again and began talking eagerly, asking me a hundred wild questions, to which I was compelled to reply, still trying to keep up the character of the long-lost son just returned victorious from the wars.
“Tell me where you have fought and overcome the enemy,” he exclaimed, raising his voice almost to a scream. “Where have they flown from you like chaff before the wind?﻿—where have you trodden them down under your horses’ hoofs?﻿—name﻿—name the places and the battles to me, Calixto?”
I felt strongly inclined just then to jump up and rush out of the room, so trying was this mad conversation to my nerves; but I thought of his daughter Demetria’s white, pathetic face, and restrained the impulse. Then in sheer desperation I began to talk madly as himself. I thought I would make him sick of warlike subjects. Everywhere, I cried, we had defeated, slaughtered, scattered to the four winds of heaven, the infamous Colorados. From the sea to the Brazilian frontier we have been victorious. With sword, lance, and bayonet we have stormed and taken every town from Tacuarembó to Montevideo.

===Chinese===

“哈，卡利克斯托，”他颤抖着将一双枯槁的老手重重按在我的双肩之上，用那一双深陷眼眶、狂乱嗜血的幽深眼眸死死盯着我，接续嘶吼道，“为父难道还需要多嘴盘问你这些年究竟去了何方吗？一个佩拉尔塔（Peralta）家族的热血男儿，除了伫立在硝烟弥漫的沙场前线，置身于血雨腥风的修罗屠场之中，为了东岸区的锦绣山河舍生忘死浴血奋战之外，还能身在何方！为父从未怪罪过你的长期不归，卡利克斯托——德梅特里亚（Demetria）尽可以向你作证，在漫长煎熬的漫漫岁月里，老夫是何等含辛茹苦坚忍不拔，皆因为父心知肚明你终归有一天会头戴胜利的月桂冠冕凯旋荣归！而老夫我呢，卡利克斯托，孤坐在这一方囚牢之中，头上戴着的又是何物？一顶荨麻编织的荆棘毒冠！不错，整整一百年了（100 年），老夫头顶上死死扣着这顶荨麻冠冕——我的闺女德梅特里亚，你可以向上天指天作证，老夫戴着这顶剧毒灼痛的荨麻之冠，已然足足煎熬了一百年！”
言罢，他整个人仿佛被抽空了最后一丝元气一般，虚脱瘫倒回了宽大的扶手椅深处，我忍不住长长松了一口气，暗自庆幸这场惊心动魄的疯癫问询终于画上了休止符。然而我却料错了。他的闺女迅速搬来了一把木椅轻手轻脚搁在老人的座椅旁。“先生，且请在此屈尊落座宽慰陪伴家父片刻，容我去院里替您打理照料坐骑，”她贴着我的耳廓轻声耳语呢喃道，随后身形宛如一抹幽灵般飘然滑出了房门。这对我而言委实是一桩残酷的煎熬苦役，我心下暗自叫苦；然而就在轻声耳语的那寥寥数语间，她温润的指尖轻轻拂过了我的手背，那一双满怀企盼哀婉的明眸投向我时满盛着无尽的感激涕零，为了这位苦难佳人的面子，我由衷庆幸方才自己未曾露出任何马脚坏了大事。
未消片刻，疯老人再度打起了精神，满面通红情绪激昂地滔滔不绝起来，连珠炮般抛出了一百个（100 个）荒诞狂暴的癫狂诘问，逼得我不得不硬着头皮逐一应对，竭力苦苦维系着自己作为这位从血海硝烟中凯旋归来的久别长子的英雄人设。
“快快向为父如实招来，你究竟在何方将敌军杀得丢盔弃甲全军覆没？”他扯着尖锐刺耳的破锣嗓子声嘶力竭地尖叫起来，“他们究竟在何处像狂风卷残云般在你面前抱头鼠窜？你究竟在何处用铁骑的万钧铁蹄将他们践踏成肉泥？报出地名来——快把那些血战的地名与战役一五一十向老夫报上名来，卡利克斯托！”
彼时彼刻，我心头委实涌起了一股想要当场拔腿夺门而逃的强烈冲动，这等同疯癫狂人面面相觑的荒诞对谈对我的神经简直是一场灭顶酷刑；然而一想到他的长女德梅特里亚那张毫无血色、楚楚可怜的悲戚面庞，我终究生生克制住了逃跑的念头。在极度的绝望与抓狂驱使之下，我索性心一横，彻底放弃了理智，开始比他还要疯癫地大肆信口开河起来。我心想索性用最狂暴的战争血腥让他彻底听腻闭嘴。我振臂高呼，宣称我们已然在四海八荒将那帮恶贯满盈的红党（Colorado）恶徒杀得一败涂地、碎尸万段，将他们的残肢断臂轰散到了九霄云外的四面八方！从浩瀚大洋直至巴西边境，我们的大军所向披靡捷报频传！凭借着吹毛断发的佩剑、长矛与森寒刺刀，我们以秋风扫落叶之势攻克并强占了自塔夸伦博（Tacuarembó）直抵蒙得维的亚的沿途所有城镇重镇！

===Original===

Every river from the Yaguaron to the Uruguay had run red with Colorado blood. In forests and sierras we had hunted them, flying like wild beasts from us; we had captured them in thousands, only to cut their throats, crucify them, blow them from guns, and tear them limb by limb to pieces with wild horses.
I was only pouring oil on the blazing fire of his insanity.
“Aha!” he shouted, his eyes sparkling, while he wildly clutched my arm with his skinny, claw-like hands, “did I not know﻿—have I not said it? Did I not fight for a hundred years, wading through blood every day, and then at last send you forth to finish the battle? And every day our enemies came and shouted in my ears, ‘Victory﻿—victory!’ They told me you were dead, Calixto﻿—that their weapons had pierced you, that they had given your flesh to be devoured of wild dogs. And I shouted with laughter to hear them. I laughed in their faces, and clapped my hands and cried out, ‘Prepare your throats for the sword, traitors, slaves, assassins, for a Peralta﻿—even Calixto, devoured of wild dogs﻿—is coming to execute vengeance! What, will God not leave one strong arm to strike at the tyrant’s breast﻿—one Peralta in all this land! Fly, miscreants! Die, wretches! He has risen from the grave﻿—he has come back from hell, armed with hellfire to burn your towns to ashes﻿—to extirpate you utterly from the earth!’ ”
His thin, tremulous voice had risen towards the close of this mad speech to a reedy shriek that rang through the quiet, darkening house like the long, shrill cry of some waterfowl heard at night in the desolate marshes.
Then he loosened his hold on my arm and dropped back moaning and shivering into his seat. His eyes closed, his whole frame trembled, and he looked like a person just recovering from an epileptic fit; then he seemed to sink to sleep. It was now getting quite dark, for the sun had been down some time, and it was with the greatest relief that I saw Doña Demetria gliding like a ghost into the room. She touched me on the arm and whispered, “Come, señor, he is asleep now.”
I followed her out into the fresh air, which had never seemed so fresh before; then, turning to me, she hurriedly whispered, “Remember, señor, that what you have told me is a secret. Say not one word of it to any other person here.”

===Chinese===

从亚瓜龙河到乌拉圭河的每一条滔滔江河，尽数被红党恶贼的滔天狗血染成了殷红血水！在密林与连绵崇山峻岭深处，我们像围猎野兽般四处穷追猛打他们抱头逃窜的残兵游勇；我们生擒俘虏了数以千计的（成千上万）溃兵俘虏，仅仅是为了将他们统统割断喉管、钉死在十字架上、绑在加农炮口轰成齑粉，并用未驯的野马将他们的骨肉四肢大卸八块五马分尸！
孰料我的这番疯言狂语，不过是在他那熊熊燃烧的疯癫狂焰上泼洒了一桶烈性火油罢了。
“哈哈！”他狂喜地仰天咆哮起来，两只眼珠子迸射出炽烈疯狂的血红精光，骨瘦如柴宛如鹰爪般的枯爪死死抠住了我的臂膀皮肉，“为父难道不知晓——老夫难道不曾未卜先知吗？老夫当年岂不是亦曾整整血战了一百年（100 年），每日每夜皆在尸山血海之中奋勇泅渡，直至最终派遣你这亲骨肉出征去替为父收官荡平寰宇！可每日每夜那些该死的恶贼偏偏跑来老夫耳根子旁聒噪大叫：‘大捷——大捷！’他们骗老夫说你早已战死沙场了，卡利克斯托——说他们的乱刃早已洞穿了你的心窝，说他们早已把你的血肉骨骸扔去喂了荒野上的恶犬！老夫听罢当场放声狂笑！老夫狠狠啐在他们的脸上，拼命拍巴掌嘲弄喝道：‘洗干净你们的狗脖子等着引颈受戮吧，卖国贼、走狗奴才、刽子手们，皆因一位佩拉尔塔——正是被你们以为喂了野狗的卡利克斯托——正提刀杀将回来向你们讨还滔天血债！怎么，苍天难道连一条惩戒暴君心窝的铁臂都不肯留下——难道在这片辽阔热土上连一位佩拉尔塔的骨血都不肯保留了吗！逃吧，跳梁小丑们！受死吧，无耻恶徒！他已然从九泉坟冢之中破土还阳——他已然从无间炼狱杀将回来，携带着九幽冥火誓要将你们的城池焚作灰烬——将你们这帮恶类从天地间彻底斩草除根绝灭干净！’”
在狂乱演讲临近尾声之际，他那微弱战栗的破锣喉音已然拔高成了一种尖锐刺耳、如芦笛般撕心裂肺的凄厉尖啸，在昏暗死寂的整栋老宅里久久回荡，宛如荒凉沼泽深处的暗夜里冷不丁传出的某种水鸟悠长凄绝的厉鸣一般。
紧接着，他抠住我手臂的枯爪缓缓脱了力，整个人呜咽战栗着颓然瘫软倒回了皮椅深处。他紧闭双目，全身上下剧烈颤抖不休，模样活像是一个刚刚从癫痫剧烈抽搐中勉强回过神来的重症病人；随后，他似乎渐渐滑入了昏睡的梦乡。此时室内已然黑透了，皆因夕阳早已西沉了多时，正是在这极度的如释重负之中，我赫然瞧见唐娜·德梅特里亚宛如一抹虚无飘渺的幽灵般悄然滑步飘入了厅堂。她伸出纤手轻轻拍了拍我的臂膀，温存耳语道：“来吧，先生，他如今总算是安睡过去了。”
我跟随着她款步迈出房门，重新踏入了户外的清新空气之中，平生我从未觉得天地间的夜风是这般沁人心脾；随后，她回过身来，急促地向我压低嗓音耳语叮嘱道：“切记，先生，您方才同我吐露的身世底细乃是绝密。对这里的其他任何闲杂人等，断断不可泄露半个字。”

## XXIII The Red Flag of Victory / 第二十三章 胜利的红旗
===Original===

She then led me to the kitchen at the end of the house. It was one of those roomy, old-fashioned kitchens still to be found in a few estancia houses built in colonial times, in which the fireplace, raised a foot or two above the floor, extends the whole width of the room. It was large and dimly lighted, the walls and rafters black with a century’s smoke and abundantly festooned with sooty cobwebs; but a large, cheerful fire blazed on the hearth, while before it stood a tall, gaunt woman engaged in cooking the supper and serving maté. This was Ramona, an old servant on the estancia.
There also sat my friend of the tangled tresses, which he had evidently succeeded in combing well out, for they now hung down quite smooth on his back and as long as a woman’s hair. Another person was also seated near the fire, whose age might have been anything from twenty-five to forty-five, for he had, I think, a mixture of Indian blood in his veins, and one of those smooth, dry, dark faces that change but little with age. He was an undersized, wiry-looking man with a small, intensely black moustache, but no whiskers or beard. He seemed to be a person of some consequence in the house, and when my conductress introduced him to me as “Don Hilario,” he rose to his feet and received me with a profound bow. In spite of his excessive politeness I conceived a feeling of distrust towards him from the moment I saw him; and this was because his small, watchful eyes were perpetually glancing at my face in a furtive manner, only to glance swiftly away again whenever I looked at him; for he seemed quite incapable of meeting the gaze of another. We drank maté and talked a little, but were not a lively party. Doña Demetria, though she sat with us, scarcely contributed a word to the conversation; while the long-haired man﻿—Santos by name, and the only peon on the establishment﻿—smoked his cigarette and sipped his maté in absolute silence.
Bony old Ramona at length dished up the supper and carried it out of the kitchen; we followed to the large living-room, where I had been before, and gathered round a small table; for these people, though apparently poverty-stricken, ate their meals after the manner of civilised beings. At the head of the table sat the fierce old white-haired man, staring at us out of his sunken eyes as we entered.

===Chinese===

随后她领着我穿过廊道，径直迈向了宅邸尽头的大厨房。那是唯有在少数殖民时代修建的大庄园（estancia）府邸里方能觅得踪影的古朴宽大厨房，其灶膛地势比地面高出一两英尺，横向贯穿了整面墙壁的全部宽度。室内宽阔幽暗昏黄，墙壁与粗壮的椽木在整整一个世纪的风霜炊烟熏烤下早已一片漆黑，其间密布着悬垂累累的乌黑蛛网；然而灶膛里却正欢快地升腾着熊熊温暖的篝火，篝火前伫立着一位身材修长干瘪的老妇人，正忙于烹煮晚膳并冲泡马黛茶（maté）。这便是拉莫娜（Ramona），大庄园里上了年岁的老女仆。
白昼里我撞见的那位满头纠结乱发的老兄亦端坐在此处，他显然已然大功告成把乱发彻底梳理通顺了，皆因满头青丝此刻正如深闺妇人的秀发一般柔顺光洁地披垂在后背之上。在篝火近旁还端坐着另一位汉子，年纪看起来大抵在二十五岁到四十五岁（25 岁到 45 岁）之间皆有可能，皆因依我辨识，他周身的血液里定然掺杂着印第安土著的血统，生着一张光滑干瘪、泛着深褐色的阴鸷面孔，这类面容向来极少随岁月流逝而显露苍老痕迹。他身材矮小瘦削，却透着一股精悍强劲的筋骨力道，嘴唇上蓄着一撇精修短小、漆黑如墨的小胡须，面颊与下颌却光洁溜溜毫无须髯。在此宅邸之中他俨然是位颇具分量的要紧人物，当引路的女主人向我将他引见为“唐·希拉里奥”（Don Hilario）之际，他霍然起身，向我行了一个极具仪式感的贴地深躬礼。尽管他言谈举止间客套谦卑得近乎谄媚，然而从目光相撞的第一眼起，我心头便不由自主地对他萌生出了一股强烈的戒备与猜忌；皆因他那一双细小机警的鼠目，始终在以一种鬼鬼祟祟的阴暗眼神偷偷斜睨着我的面颊，且每逢我抬眼与他正视之际，他又会以迅雷不及掩耳之势慌忙闪躲开视线；他分明是一个骨子里根本不敢同旁人坦荡对视的阴鸷之徒。我们围坐在一起品啜着马黛茶闲话家常，席间的气氛却沉闷压抑至极。唐娜·德梅特里亚（Doña Demetria）纵然同我们平起平坐，却自始至终难得吐露半个字；而那位长发汉子——名唤桑托斯（Santos），亦是庄园里唯一的长工雇工（peon）——则在绝对的死寂中默默吞云吐雾抽着卷烟、啜饮着茶水。
骨瘦如柴的老仆拉莫娜终于盛好了热腾腾的晚膳，端着盘碟快步迈出了厨房；我们尾随其后折返回了方才我曾踏足过的那间宽敞大厅，在正中央的一张小巧方桌旁团团围坐；皆因这户人家纵然家徒四壁显出一派穷困潦倒之相，却依然恪守着文明礼仪阶层的用餐规矩。在长桌的主位首座之上，端坐着那位满头银丝、目光如炬的凶悍老翁，当我们推门而入之际，他那一双深陷眼窝的厉眸便死死定格在我们身上。

===Original===

Half rising from his seat, he mentioned to me to take a chair near him, then, addressing Don Hilario, who sat opposite, he said, “This is my son Calixto, just returned from the wars, where, as you know, he has greatly distinguished himself.”
Don Hilario rose and bowed gravely. Demetria took the other end of the table, while Santos and Ramona occupied the two remaining seats.
I was greatly relieved to find that the old man’s mood had changed; there were no more wild outbursts like the one I had witnessed earlier in the evening; only occasionally he would fix his strange, burning eyes on me in a way that made me exceedingly uncomfortable. We began the meal with broth, which we finished in silence; and while we ate, Don Hilario’s swift glances incessantly flew from face to face; Demetria, pale and evidently ill at ease, keeping her eyes cast down all the time.
“Is there no wine this evening, Ramona?” asked the old man in querulous tones when the old woman rose to remove the broth basins.
“The *master* has not ordered me to put any on the table,” she replied with asperity, and strongly emphasising the obnoxious word.
“What does this mean, Don Hilario?” said the old man, turning to his neighbour. “My son has just returned after a long absence; are we to have no wine for an occasion like this?”
Don Hilario, with a faint smile on his lips, drew a key from his pocket and passed it silently to Ramona. She rose, muttering, from the table and proceeded to unlock a cupboard, from which she took a bottle of wine. Then, going round the table, she poured out half a tumblerful for each person, excepting herself and Santos, who, to judge from his stolid countenance, did not expect any.
“No, no,” said old Peralta, “give Santos wine, and pour yourself out a glass also, Ramona. You have both been good, faithful friends to me, and have nursed Calixto in his infancy. It is right that you should drink his health and rejoice with us at his return.”
